## Next version: RS-A-nestedtime (2026-11-11, branch `claude/rs-a-nestedtime` off af25b72)

PR #46 (RS-A-altmodules) merged as af25b72 at 2026-11-11 16:25:02Z, when the user said "Yes, auto-merge 46" (9 of 9 jobs passed). The user, on this item: "Yes, continue with RS-A-nestedtime once PR 46 merges."

- What was wrong, CONFIRMED here on af25b72 (Windows, `repro-nestedtime.js`): before `git status`, the loop over folders of tracked files in `farWorktree` (`gitfacts.js`) ran `farNested`, a whole repo check, on each subfolder that holds a `.git`, and looked at the time once in 500 folders of tracked files. One folder can hold any number of such subfolders. With 5 s given and N subfolders, each with a `.git` file to a gitdir whose config names two `lfs.storage` folders of 120 links to a folder at the end of a 900-character path: N=1 1.33 s (status read), N=6 5.60 s, N=12 12.35 s, the last two `git-timeout`. About 0.93 s for each. Opus saw 22.4 s for 24.
  - The folder needs three or more tracked files: with one or two, only those names are looked up and the subfolders aren't listed (the first run here showed nothing for that reason).
  - Windows and macOS check every subfolder on disk; elsewhere only one named like a tracked file, so there the shape needs a tracked file for each (INFERRED from the code, not run).
- Fix (`gitfacts.js`, both copies, one line): `if (g && r.left() < 800) return 'git-timeout';` before `farNested`, as the loop over submodules below it already does.
- After, CONFIRMED here: N=1 1.30 s (status read), N=6 4.87 s, N=12 5.07 s, both `git-timeout`. The most past the time is one check.
- Test: "git status: the time is looked at before each folder with a .git is checked (RS-A-nestedtime)", both copies, every system: four tracked files that are now folders with a `.git` file. `Date.now` stands still and jumps to 500 or 900 ms before the end of the 5 s at the first look at one of those `.git` files; the looks are counted, no seconds are measured. 500 ms left: `git-timeout` after one look. 900 ms left, and time in hand: all four checked and status read. Four folders with no `.git` and 500 ms left: four looks and status read. Fails on af25b72.
- Mutations (`mutate-nestedtime.js`, each through `--only 'RS-A-nestedtime'`): the parent's file; 400 for 800; 1000 for 800; the time looked at after the check; the time looked at for folders with no `.git` too; another reason word. All 6 fail; unchanged passes. The fifth passed until the case with no `.git` was added.
- Docs: SECURITY.md's paragraph on the status check gained two sentences.
- Not shown: macOS and Linux times; more than 12 subfolders here.
- Still deferred: RS-A-modlinks, RS-A-gitdirlinks, RS-A-abovegit, RS-A-altsmall, RS-A-dotwords, RS-A-sharefold, RS-A-storecost, RS-A-biglisting.

## Next version: RS-A-altmodules (2026-11-11, branch `claude/rs-a-altmodules` off f7df599, merged in PR #46 as af25b72)

PR #45 (RS-A-altread) merged as f7df599 at 2026-11-11 15:19:50Z, when the user said "Yes, auto-merge 45" (9 of 9 jobs passed; CI on main passed).

The item had two parts. One is fixed, the other is kept as it is, with the reason.

- Part one, fixed: a repo's settings and its alternates each had a work limit of their own. CONFIRMED here on f7df599 (Windows, `repro-altmodules2.js`): stores of 120 links to a folder at the end of a 900-character path, two named by `lfs.storage` and two next to alternates: `''` in 1.73 s (2 and 1: 1.30 s; 1 and 2: 1.31 s).
  - Fix (`gitbin.js`, both copies): `limitedWalk(fn, repo)` takes `{ left }`; `farGit` makes one of 8 MiB and gives it to both walks, so the alternates start with what the values left. `farLfsEntries` (`.lfsconfig`) is one walk and is unchanged.
  - After, CONFIRMED here: 2 and 1, 1 and 2, 2 and 2 give `many-config-paths` in 1.1 s; 1 and 1, 2 and 0, 0 and 2 give `''` in 0.9 s.
  - Test: "git pointers: a repo's lfs.storage folders and its alternates share one limit on the work (RS-A-altmodules)", both copies: those five counts but 2 and 2. About 400 folders, 480 links, 3 to 5 s in each copy. Fails on f7df599.
  - Mutations (`mutate-altmodules.js`, each through `--only 'RS-A-altmodules'`): the parent's file; the values walk without the repo's limit; the alternates walk without it; what a walk left not kept; every walk starting with the whole limit; the limit doubled; the limit halved. All 7 fail the test; unchanged passes.
- Part two, kept as it is: each module gitdir under `.git/modules` is checked with limits of its own. CONFIRMED here on f7df599 (`repro-altmodules.js`, `farModules` with no time limit): 1, 4 and 8 module gitdirs, each naming 2 alternates beside stores of 110 links: `''` in 0.90, 3.32 and 6.24 s, counting 6.12M, 23.97M and 47.76M.
  - What bounds it: the hook gives `farModules` what is left of 5 s and it stops with `git-timeout` when under 800 ms are left, checked between gitdirs. CONFIRMED here with 5 s: the 8-gitdir shape gives `git-timeout` in 4.21 s (f7df599) and 4.71 s (with the fix). git is kept out.
  - Why no limit for all module gitdirs: plain ones count too. CONFIRMED here: 1,000 module gitdirs with names of 60 characters and a `core.worktree` each, in a repo at a path of 192 characters, count 6.57M in 0.82 s, so 2,000 count about 13M (INFERRED by doubling). A limit with room for that (32 MiB with a margin of two) allows about 5 s of the slow shape, which is what the time allows already, and it would add a way to refuse a real repo for good where the time limit refuses for one run.
  - 2,000 module gitdirs give `many-submodules` before any is checked (CONFIRMED here).
- Docs: MANUAL.md section 16 row `many-config-paths` gained a sentence; SECURITY.md says the alternates start with what the values left, and how module gitdirs are bounded.
- Dropped on the user's word ("Yes, drop it", 2026-11-11): changing code messages to `pwsh`. On ee252b5 the user-facing `powershell.exe` suggestions outside the watchdog installer are `install.ps1:153` and `tools/migrate-from-jb-memory.js:1209` (watchdog commands, which need Windows PowerShell 5.1) and `tools/migrate-from-jb-memory.js:1234` (runs `install.ps1` again, which works under 5.1 and 7). No skill file names PowerShell. CONFIRMED here by reading.
- Still deferred: RS-A-altsmall, RS-A-dotwords, RS-A-sharefold, RS-A-storecost, RS-A-biglisting, and the four items of round 1 below.

### RS-A-altmodules round 1 (2026-11-11, on f7df599..2526562, PR #46)

Fable on `gitbin.js` and the test, Opus on the decision to keep a limit for each module gitdir, Sonnet on the doc additions, each from an export under `E:/DevTemp`. None found anything wrong in the changed code. Opus's verdict on the decision: sound, with a caveat.

- Fixed in this batch, all wording (each seen here on reading the text):

  - "The same goes for `.lfsconfig`." had ended up after the new sentences; it is back where it was (Sonnet).
  - The alternates bullet's sentence was hard to read and is now three (Sonnet). MANUAL.md's row says "share that limit" (Sonnet; its own suggestion, "link-following work", was not taken: the limit counts all path work).
  - SECURITY.md said the time adds up "only as far as" 5 seconds and that a limit with room for plain module gitdirs "would allow as long as the time does". Both said too much (Opus, Fable): the gitdir being checked can end a little past the time, and the second holds for one limit on all work only. It now says so, gives 4.2 to 4.9 seconds, and names the limit on links alone as left for later.
- CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run: `work` belongs to one `farGit` call and nothing inside a walk calls `farGit` or `limitedWalk`; the second walk starts with what the first left (`[[8388608,5006698],[5006698,1731098]]`); the test's body gives `''` five times on the old file and `''`, `''`, `''`, `many-config-paths`, `many-config-paths` on the new. One store of 120 links counts 3.38M, two leave 1.73M, three are over by about 1.65M.
- Real repos, CONFIRMED by Fable and by Opus, not re-run, each scaled from 200 to 300 gitdirs: 2,000 submodule gitdirs count 2.5M to 4.6M in the values walk at a path of about 200 characters; two plain alternates count about 3,600. No repo was found that passed before and is refused now. INFERRED by Opus: in such a repo an alternate whose own folders hold 2,500 to 3,500 links would now be refused.
- The decision, CONFIRMED by Opus by reading, not re-run: only the PreCompact hook reaches `farModules`, `left` is always passed, the time is never more than 5 s, and after `git-timeout` nothing lets git run. 2,000 plain module gitdirs: 11.4M to 14M (Fable and Opus, scaled), so "about 13 million" holds.
- Deferred, older and unchanged by this PR (`gitfacts.js` is the same file before and after):
  - New, RS-A-nestedtime, FLAGGED for the time seen, CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run, seen on f7df599 and 2526562 (Windows; INFERRED also macOS): the loop over tracked folders in `gitfacts.js` (lines 190 to 218) runs `farNested`, a whole `farGit`, on each subfolder that holds a `.git`, and looks at the time once in 500 folders. With 5 s and N subfolders that each name 2 stores of 120 links: N=12 took 11.4 s (11.2 s on f7df599), N=24 took 22.4 s, each ending in `git-timeout`. Repro: a repo with N tracked subfolders, each with a `.git` file to a gitdir whose config names two `lfs.storage` folders of 120 links to a folder at the end of a 900-character path; time the PreCompact status check with 5 s. Fix idea: look at the time before each `farNested`.

One limit on link work for all module gitdirs (or on work past about 16K for each) would refuse the slow shape after about 2 gitdirs, 1.7 s where the time allows 4.7 s, and leave plain repos alone. It would not replace the time limit: the walks of `lfs` stores aren't counted.

## Next version: RS-A-altread (2026-11-11, branch `claude/rs-a-altread` off 3cb56b5, main at ee252b5 merged in after round 1, merged in PR #45 as f7df599)

PR #43 (RS-A-altcase and RS-A-storelinks) merged as 3cb56b5 at 2026-11-11 03:38:57Z, when the user said "Yes, auto-merge 43".

- What was wrong, CONFIRMED here on 3cb56b5 (Windows): reading each alternate's own `info/alternates` counted toward no limit. 6 alternates, each listing 3,000 letter cases of one sibling folder whose own `info/alternates` is 62,000 bytes of comment lines: `''` in 8.34 s (one such alternate 1.67 s). Off Windows the same reads need a real folder per line, so the time there is INFERRED lower and was not measured.
- Fix (`gitbin.js`, both copies, one line in `farAlternates`): after an alternates file is read, its length comes off `WALK_LEFT`, the work limit `limitedWalk` sets for all of a repo's alternates (8 MiB), and below zero the answer is `many-config-paths`. The repo's own `objects/info/alternates` is counted too. A file over 64 KB was refused before and still is, so about 125 files of the largest size reach the limit.
- After, CONFIRMED here: the shape above gives `many-config-paths` in 0.07 s (one alternate 0.06 s). 100 alternates with a 60 KB comment-only file each: `''` in 0.05 s; 150: `many-config-paths` in 0.07 s.
- Test: "git pointers: reading the alternates files counts toward the limit on the work (RS-A-altread)", both copies: 100 files of 60 KB pass, 150 give `many-config-paths`, 150 files of one line pass. It runs on all three systems, makes about 310 folders and 9 MB of files, and takes 1 to 2 s in each copy. Fails on 3cb56b5 (the parent's `gitbin.js`).
- Mutations (`mutate-altread.js` in the session's scratch folder, each run through `--only 'RS-A-altread'`): the parent's file, half the length counted, twice the length, a fixed 60,000 for each file, the length compared but not taken off. All 5 fail the test; unchanged passes.
- Docs: MANUAL.md section 16 row `many-config-paths` and SECURITY.md's alternates bullet each gained a sentence.
- Not shown: the Windows-only shape is not a test (18,000 lines of letter cases; the 150-file shape covers the same line of code on every system). That no real repo has megabytes of alternates files is INFERRED: git writes a line per alternate.
- Repro: `repro-altread.js <repo root> <temp base> [FG]` in the session's scratch folder; the shapes are described above and in its header.
- Still deferred: RS-A-altmodules (each module gitdir has limits of its own; `lfs.storage` and the alternates each have a work limit of their own).

## Next version: RS-A-altcase and RS-A-storelinks (2026-11-10, branch `claude/rs-a-altcase` off cc06828, merged in PR #43 as 3cb56b5)

Both FLAGGED in PR #42's round 1. The user: "Yes, both together".

- The faults, CONFIRMED here on cc06828 (Windows, drive E:, junctions; `repro-altcase.js` in the session's scratchpad), each a repo whose `.git/objects/info/alternates` lists the shape, all answered `''`:
  - A: one alternate holding 1,000 links to a nearby folder, named in 100 letter cases: 10.78 s (one spelling 0.12 s).
  - B: 12 alternates, each with an `lfs` folder next to it of 125 links to a folder at the end of a 900-character path: 5.51 s (one store 0.46 s, two 0.91 s).
  - C: one alternate holding 4,000 plain folders, named in 100 letter cases: 6.29 s (20 spellings 1.26 s, one 0.06 s).
  - D (RS-A-altlines): 5,000 lines with nothing there 0.39 s, 2,000 lines 0.15 s. Left as it is, still deferred.
- Why: an alternate is known by the exact text of its path, so one folder is walked once per spelling (or 100 folders once each), and since RS-A-manylinks each walk of links has a limit of its own, with up to 64 stores and no limit on alternates.
- The fix (`gitbin.js`, both copies):
  - `farGit` runs its `farAlternates` loop inside `limitedWalk`: all of a repo's alternates and the stores next to them share the limit on the work a config's path values have (8 MiB). Past it the answer is `many-config-paths`.
  - `MAX_ALT_ENTRIES`, 100,000: `stores.alt` counts the entries the walks of the alternates' own folders looked at (the `tally` of `linkedEntries`), for one repo, its commondir and the alternates of alternates together. Once the walks before held that many, the next alternate answers `many-entries`. Plain folders cost listings, which the limit on the work doesn't count.
  - The call for an alternate's own alternates now passes `stores` on.
- After the fix, CONFIRMED here (the same script): A `many-config-paths` in 1.68 s, B `many-config-paths` in 1.33 s, C `many-entries` in 2.31 s. Still `''`: one spelling of A (0.11 s), one and two stores of B, 20 spellings and one of C, and D (0.62 s for 5,000 lines; the times vary from run to run by about a third).
- Test: "git pointers: a repo's alternates and the stores next to them share one limit on the work and one on the entries (RS-A-altcase, RS-A-storelinks)", both copies. Two stores of 120 long-path links pass and three give `many-config-paths` (110 until round 1). 19 alternates, each with one link to the same folder of 5,000 files, pass and 22 give `many-entries`; so do 21 as the alternates of an alternate, and 11 each in a gitdir and its commondir (8 each pass). On Windows also one alternate in 22 letter cases (19 pass). It makes 5,000 files and under 500 folders, and takes 10 to 13 s in each copy.
- Single changes to the fix (`mutate-altcase.js`, scratchpad), each run against the test, CONFIRMED here: all 8 fail it (the parent's file, no `limitedWalk` around the loop, the count doubled or halved, no check before a walk, the walk not adding to the count, an alternate's alternates with a count of their own, each gitdir with a count of its own). A first run left the last one passing; the commondir assertions were added for it.
- What a user can meet: a repo whose alternates hold more than 100,000 entries between them besides loose objects, or thousands of links, is now refused. INFERRED rare: a real alternate holds its packs and up to 256 loose-object folders.
- Still deferred: RS-A-altlines (D above), and RS-A-altmodules (each module gitdir is checked on its own, so each has these limits of its own; not measured here).
- Docs: MANUAL.md section 16's `many-entries` and `many-config-paths` rows and one SECURITY.md bullet. `SHA256SUMS` regenerated.
- Not run: macOS, WSL (symlinks), the two long suites (CI), a real link to a share.

## Next version: RS-A-manylinks (2026-11-10, branch `claude/rs-a-manylinks` off b3e7047, merged in PR #42 as cc06828)

Deferred from RS-A-foldkeys' review, and FLAGGED in PR #41's round 1 for the largest times seen. Taken next on my recommendation; the user: "Yes, continue".

- The fault, CONFIRMED here on b3e7047 (Windows, drive C:, junctions; `repro-manylinks.js` in the session's scratchpad): a repo whose `.git/zz` holds 3,000 links, each to a folder of its own 40 levels down: 9.2 s, `''`. With 9,999 links to folders two levels down: 5.4 s, `''`. The walks' budgets count entries, and an entry that is a link costs what its target's path does. An `lfs.storage` store of those shapes was already refused (`many-config-paths`, 2.2 to 3.0 s), since that walk runs inside the limit on a config's path values. CONFIRMED by Fable in PR #41's round 1, not re-run: three stores next to alternates, each of 1,000 symlinks to a chain of 37 links, 57.9 s in WSL.
- The fix (`gitbin.js`, both copies): `linkedEntries` now wraps its walk (`linkedEntriesIn`) in a limit on the work `localPath` does for the links it follows, `MAX_LINK_WORK`, 4 MiB counted as the limit on path values counts (`WALK_LEFT`). Inside a check of config values the smaller of the two holds, and what the links cost is taken off that check's own limit. A link that can't be followed because the work ran out answers `many-entries`; one that leads to a share or loops answers `linked-gitdir` as before.
- Why 4 MiB: 3,000 links to nearby folders were accepted before (1.3 s) and stay accepted (1.5 s here); half the limit would refuse them. About 3,500 such links fit, or about 350 to folders 40 levels down.
- After the fix, CONFIRMED here (the same script): both slow shapes answer `many-entries` in 1.3 to 1.8 s, in `.git/zz` and as a store. 12, 300 and 3,000 links to folders two levels down answer `''` in 16 ms, 0.14 s and 1.5 s.
- Test: "git pointers: links in a gitdir or a store are followed up to a limit on the work, then git is kept out (RS-A-manylinks)", both copies, with junctions (symlinks off Windows) that all lead to one folder at the end of a path 900 characters long (about 400 parts named `d`). A link costs what walking its target's path does, each part by the length of the path up to it, so such a link counts about 27,000 of the 4 MiB with no folder of its own. 12 and 125 links in a gitdir pass, 220 give `many-entries`; links that loop give `linked-gitdir`; a gitdir and a store next to its alternate with 125 each pass (the limit is one walk's); a store of 125 passes and one of 220 doesn't; two stores of 125 pass and three give `many-config-paths`. It fails with b3e7047's `gitbin.js` in both copies, and takes 12 to 13 s in each (most of it the checks' own counted work, about 0.8 s for a walk at the limit).
  - Margins, CONFIRMED here (`probe-longpath.js`, scratchpad): the most links accepted is 149 on Windows (junctions, base path 28 characters) and 149 in WSL (symlinks, Node 18, base 22). A longer temp path means fewer parts and a few more links: INFERRED 150 to 153 for the temp paths of the check script and macOS (round 1's replay of the counting). 125 is over half of either and 220 under twice either, so the halved and doubled limits both fail the test.
  - The first versions gave each link a folder of its own 40 levels down, 33,600 folders in all. That test passed in 12 to 24 s, but three runs took 481, 961 and 1,308 s. CONFIRMED here, not the code under test: during the 481 s both test processes had no CPU time and no I/O count for 7 minutes (a sampler every 15 s, `sample-procs.ps1`), `rm` calls of the check script hung the same way, and Windows logged ReFS event 147 for `rm.exe` on E:, an I/O of 373 s. The test's steps timed alone (`time-links-steps.js`, 8 rounds) took 15 to 24 s with no check over 2 s. Which ReFS work stalls is not known; the test no longer makes those folders (LESSONS, 2026-11-10).
  - Until they age out of the 20 kept failed runs, the folders of the earlier mutation runs (about 38,000 entries each) make a prune in `dev/release-check.sh` slow: a series of ten `--only` runs took 4 min 47 s for 75 s of tests.
- Single changes to the fix (`mutate-links.js`, scratchpad), each run against the test, CONFIRMED here: 8 of 9 fail it (the parent's file, the limit doubled or halved, no limit set, the outer limit not restored or restored whole, the reason always `linked-gitdir` or always `many-entries`). The ninth passes: the walk's limit set even where the outer one is smaller. INFERRED from the code: it changes no answer, since what was used is still taken off the outer limit, which then answers `many-config-paths`; it only lets a walk do up to 4 MiB of work past an outer limit already near its end.
- What a user can meet: a `.git` or an LFS or git-annex store with thousands of links in it is now refused (`many-entries`). INFERRED rare: git-new-workdir and Android's repo tool link about a dozen entries of a gitdir.
- Docs: MANUAL.md section 16's `many-entries` row and one SECURITY.md sentence. `SHA256SUMS` regenerated.

The test's margins are measured on Windows and in WSL, and INFERRED for macOS (its path limit is 1,024 characters, the test's longest path about 900 plus nothing: the links are in short paths).

## Next version: RS-A-altstores (2026-11-10, branch `claude/rs-a-altstores` off 06bfe98, merged in PR #41 as b3e7047)

Deferred from RS-A-storagewalk's review and flagged (time). Taken next on my recommendation under the user's standing word of 2026-11-10 to work the deferred items one at a time: it was the only open item still flagged.

- The fault, CONFIRMED here on 06bfe98 (Windows; `repro-altstores.js` in the session's scratchpad, which counts `fs.readdirSync` calls): `.git/objects/info/alternates` with the lines `x/0` to `x/19` and `.git/objects/x/lfs` holding 10,100 folders: 410,171 listings, 30 s, answer `''`. With one line: 30,114 listings, 2.8 s. `farAlternates` in `gitbin.js` walked the store next to an alternate (`<alt>/../lfs`) for every line, twice for each (two of its candidates are the same folder), with no limit on how many.
- The fix (`gitbin.js`, both copies): the stores next to alternates are kept for one repo's check (`altStores`, made in `farGit` for each repo checked, a submodule or nested repo on its own, and shared by the gitdir's and the commondir's alternates), by the exact path `localPath` found, as for `lfs.storage`. A folder already seen costs no disk call. The two limits of the `lfs.storage` stores hold for them, counted apart: more than 64 existing folders, or a further one once those before it held 20,000 looked-at entries, answer `many-config-paths`.
- After the fix, CONFIRMED here: the 20 lines give 20,132 listings in 1.07 s (one walk of the store by the gitdir's own walk, since this repro puts it inside `.git`, and one as a store); one line 20,113. 9,000 lines (the most `x/<n>` lines a 64 KB file holds is near that): 3.8 s, `''`.
- Times for stores outside `.git`, CONFIRMED here (`probe-alt-worst.js`, scratchpad; on drive C:, where a store of 10,100 folders walks in 0.7 to 0.95 s): one store 0.94 to 1.45 s; three stores of 9,997, 10,000 and 10,000 looked-at entries, the most accepted, 2.8 to 4.2 s over two runs; a fourth after those, or a third after two full ones, `many-config-paths`. A repo can have both kinds of store, so the worst accepted case is now the sum of the two (`lfs.storage` stores and stores next to alternates). INFERRED, not built: about twice the figures above.
- Test: "git pointers: an LFS store next to an alternate is walked once, and too many are refused (RS-A-altstores)", both copies. Counted listings of the store folders; 64 and 65 stores; lines with no store; a linked worktree whose own alternates and the main repo's count together; the 20,000 entries; a linked store after a clean one (skipped where links can't be made, as here). It fails with 06bfe98's `gitbin.js` in both copies.
- Single changes to the fix (`mutate-alt.js`, scratchpad), each run against the test: 11 of 12 fail it here (a repeat walked again, no limit or 63 or 65 stores, no entries limit or one later, entries not counted, the limit answering `''`, a file counted as a store, stores not shared between the gitdir and the commondir, and the parent's file). The twelfth, a store's answer dropped, passes here because the link assertion is skipped; in WSL the same input gives `linked-gitdir` with the fix and `''` with the change (`alt-link-wsl.js`, scratchpad: CONFIRMED there through a script, not through the test, which CI runs).
- What a user can meet: a repo whose alternates have more than 64 `lfs` folders beside them, or three with the first two holding 10,000 entries or more, is now refused (`many-config-paths`). INFERRED rare: a repo has one or two alternates.

## Next version: RS-A-dottracked (2026-11-10, branch `claude/rs-a-dottracked` off b53b065, merged in PR #40 as 06bfe98)

Deferred from PR #39's review and flagged as serious (access, Windows). Taken next on my recommendation under the user's standing word of 2026-11-10 to work the deferred items one at a time ("continue").

- The fault, CONFIRMED here on b53b065 (Windows, Git 2.56.0.windows.4; `repro-dottracked.js` and `repro-dottracked3.js` with `repro-dottracked3.sh` in the session's scratchpad). `gitfacts.js` `farWorktree` looked up a tracked name as written. Node opens `d.` literally and finds nothing, so the entry was passed over:
  - A tracked file `s.`, `s ` or `s..`, with a folder `s` on disk that holds a `.git` pointing at a share: `''`. With the file tracked as `s`: `submodule-pointer`.
  - What git opens, from `git status` on an index made in WSL, with the tracked content on disk under the plain names. Tracked `d./f` and `e /f`: found (git opens `d/f` and `e/f`), with every setting tried. Tracked file `u.`: not found by default, found with `core.fscache=false`, which a repo's own config can set. `k/f`, the control: found.
  - Not a fault: a gitlink `m.` or `x./m` was already refused on b53b065 (`farNested` goes through `localPath`, which has the rule since PR #39). Names with a stream's name after them (`s::$INDEX_ALLOCATION/f`, `t::$DATA`, `v:$I30:$INDEX_ALLOCATION/f`) are not found by git with `core.protectNTFS` and `core.fscache` each on and off (`repro-dottracked4.js`), so they get no rule. That was run on the Dev Drive (ReFS); plain NTFS was not tried.
  - That a link `d` to a share would be followed for a tracked `d./f` is INFERRED: making such a link needs a right this session doesn't have (EPERM), and a junction can't name a share.
- The fix: on Windows a tracked path with a name that ends in a dot or a space keeps status from being read (`odd-path`). It is `gitbin.js`'s `dotEnd`, now exported, called once per index entry in `farWorktree`. Elsewhere `dotEnd` is always false, so nothing changes there. The cost: a repo on Windows whose index holds such a name (one that can't be checked out there with git's defaults) gets no file list in its snapshot.
- Test: "git status: a tracked name that ends in a dot or a space keeps status unread on Windows (RS-A-dottracked)", both copies. It passes with the fix and fails with b53b065's `gitfacts.js` at its first dotted name (`undefined` for `odd-path`). Run on Windows only so far.
- Docs: MANUAL.md's `odd-path` row for the file list and one sentence in SECURITY.md. `SHA256SUMS` regenerated.
- Round 1, on b53b065..8ef8f24 (PR #40). Fable on the code (65 repo pairs on a ReFS and an NTFS drive, indexes made in WSL or written raw, real `git status` five ways), Sonnet on the docs. Nothing CONFIRMED in the changed code. CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run: the rule returns before every use of a tracked name; no repo without such a name changed its answer; in WSL nine names answer as before; stream names, 8.3 names (NTFS), device names, letter case and 300-character paths show no mismatch between git and the check.
  - Three older holes of the same class, in the same loop, each CONFIRMED here on 8ef8f24 (`repro-dott-r1.js`, scratchpad; it writes the index as a file) and fixed in the batch after it, pulled in without the user's word for it as part of this item. Windows git takes a backslash in a tracked name as a separator and finds `d\s`, `x/d\f` and `E:d/f` under the plain disk path.
    - A tracked `d\s` with three more names in the root, `d/s` on disk holding a `.git` that points at a share: `''`. The listing branch looked for a folder named `d\s`. With `d\s` alone: `submodule-pointer`.
    - A tracked `d\f` or `x/d\f` with `d` a junction to a folder the check refuses: `''`, where `d/f` gives `linked-folder`. The folders on the way were split on `/` alone.
    - A tracked `E:d/f` with `d` such a junction: `filter-check` here (Fable, with `check-attr` stood in for, saw status read). No rule refused it.
  - The fix: on Windows a tracked path with a backslash, or with a drive letter and a colon at its start, gives `odd-path`, next to the dot rule. A colon elsewhere is left alone. The cost is the same as the dot rule's: no file list on Windows for a repo with such a name, which git there refuses to check out.
  - Test: the same test, with an index written as a file for names Windows git won't add. It now covers gitlinks and both separators, the two gaps Fable named. Single changes to the rule (`mutate-dott.js`, `mutate-dott2.js`): the dot rule alone (8ef8f24's), no backslash part, no drive letter part, no dot rule, a colon anywhere, and a drive letter after any `/` each fail it. A backslash refused on every system passes on Windows and can only fail elsewhere: left to CI.
  - Docs (Sonnet): fourteen references to a section by its place were wrong after the new section went in. Each CONFIRMED here; those fourteen and one more now name their section (PLAYBOOK, Writing code and docs, step 10). Round 2's docs review found about 25 more by place in older sections ("section at the top", "section below", "the next section", from the RS-H-nopoint section down), CONFIRMED by Sonnet, not re-run: still to be named, not done in this PR.
  - Round 2, on 8ef8f24..eb3476b. Fable on the code (about 60 repos with indexes written raw, ReFS and NTFS, WSL for Linux), Sonnet on the docs. Nothing CONFIRMED in the changed code: the stop rule is met. CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run: a tracked `d\f` behind a junction is read on 8ef8f24 and `odd-path` on eb3476b; names without a backslash or a letter and colon at the start answer the same on both, on Windows (11 sets) and in WSL (17 sets); the test's index writer gives the bytes `git update-index` gives for 18 name sets; the rule's single changes each fail the test, the one for every system in WSL. A correction to this section's first bullet: with `core.protectNTFS` off git does open `dd/f` for a tracked `dd::$INDEX_ALLOCATION/f`, and Node resolves the same name, so the check looks at the same folder (`linked-folder` on both versions). INFERRED by Fable, low, not acted on: every drive-letter name it tried was already kept from status on 8ef8f24 by another check, so that half makes the refusal explicit; a name such as `1:f` is kept from status only by `check-attr` failing. Docs (Sonnet): one false sentence here, corrected (above); two INFERRED wording points deferred, since they change shipped files after a clean round: the test's name says only "a dot or a space", and MANUAL.md's new clause is Windows-only by its place and not by a word.
  - Not run: macOS, a real link to a share (no right to make one here), the long suites. HYPOTHESIZED by Fable, not tested: for a tracked `C:s` as a folder, git looks at `C:\s\.git` at the drive's root; the rule now refuses the name either way.

## Next version: RS-A-foldkeys and RS-A-trailingdot (2026-11-10, branch `claude/rs-a-foldkeys` off f97553a, merged in PR #39 as b53b065)

Both were deferred from PR #38's reviews and flagged as serious: they are about access (a folder or file the check never looks at), on Windows. The user pulled them in on 2026-11-10.

- RS-A-foldkeys, the fault, CONFIRMED here on f97553a (Windows, `repro-fold.js` in the session's scratchpad; each case below is also in the test). Four sets of "already seen" (five keyed places, since one set is keyed at two) were keyed by `toLowerCase()` on Windows, and JavaScript lower-cases `k` and the Kelvin sign (U+212A) alike while NTFS and ReFS keep them as two names. With the look-alike named first, the second was skipped and the answer was `''`:
  - `farConfig`'s set of config files read: `.git/config` including `k.cfg`, then `<U+212A>.cfg` whose `core.attributesFile` names a share. Alone: `network-config`.
  - `linkedEntries`' set of folders walked: `.git/refs/heads/k` next to `.git/refs/heads/<U+212A>` holding a junction loop. Alone: `linked-gitdir`.
  - `moduleGitdirs`' set, twice: `.git/modules/k` next to a gitdir `.git/modules/<U+212A>` whose config names a share (`farModules`), and the same for the `objects` folder of two such gitdirs.
  - `farGitConfig`'s set of folders searched for a `.git`: the session in `w/k/link`, a junction to `w/<U+212A>/sub`, where `w/k` is a repo whose config names a share. From a plain folder there: `network-config`.
  - The review named the second and guessed at the third and fourth; the first and the last are new, found by searching for the pattern.
- The fix: each set is keyed by the exact text. A config file named again in another case is read again, within the 50 files. A folder a link leads to is told by the system's own path for it (`onDisk`, rounds 1 to 3 below), so the walks' sets know it under one text.
- RS-A-trailingdot, the fault, CONFIRMED here on f97553a: Windows programs drop dots and spaces from a name's end, and Node opens the name as written. `cmd /c dir k.` lists `k`; `k./a` reaches `k/a`. With `lfs.storage = <path>/k.`, git-lfs 3.8.1 reports `LfsStorageDir=.../k.` and `git add` of a tracked file writes the object into `k/objects/...` (run here). `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` run with the folder `k.` answers `.../k` (run here). The check answered `''` for `lfs.storage = k.`, `k..`, `k./a` (the folder `k` holding a junction loop) and for a session folder given as `k.` (the repo `k` naming a share in its config).
  - Not a fault: a `.git` file with `gitdir: ../real.`. git itself answers "gitfile does not point to a valid repository" (run here).
- The fix: `dotEnd(p)`, Windows only: a path with a name that ends in a dot or a space, `.` and `..` apart. It is refused in `oddRelative` (a value, an include, an alternates line, a pointer, a link's target: `odd-config-path` or the caller's reason), in `farGitConfig` for the session folder and `farNested` for a nested repo's (`odd-path`), and in `localPath` itself (never a local path, whoever asks). Since round 1 (below) one caller is left out: values under `alias` and `pretty` (`WORDS_KEY`), where `farPathValue` asks `driveRelative` in place of `oddRelative` and passes `words` to `localPath`. Quoted values (`storage = "k "`) are decoded before the check and refused too (run here through a real config file).
- The test: "git pointers: two names JavaScript lower-cases alike are two names, and a name Windows opens under another is refused (next version)". It fails on f97553a's `gitbin.js` in both copies. 16 single changes of the fix each fail it: 15 on Windows, and the Windows-only switch of `dotEnd` in WSL (where `k.` must stay a name like any other). The list is `muts-fold.json` in the scratchpad, not kept in the repo. One junction is made, on Windows only, with `busyRetry`.
- What a user can meet: on Windows, a config value, include, pointer or session folder with a name that ends in a dot or a space is now refused. For a file's or folder's name that was INFERRED never in a real repo (Windows programs can't make or open such a name); round 1 showed it wrong for values that are not paths, and aliases and log formats are now left out (below). Still refused: a path setting, or a key of one's own that ends like one, whose value has a word ending in a dot or a space at its end or before a slash. A file system that folds `k` and the Kelvin sign (macOS's) has one folder for the two, which the checks then read once for each text.
- Seen and left, older, same pattern in two comparisons (INFERRED from the code on f97553a and 3e1ff9f, not run): `shareRoot` lower-cases a share's server and name before comparing it with the session's own share, so `//srv/<U+212A>` would count as the session's `//srv/k` if a server keeps the two apart; and `sameDir` lower-cases when it passes over a PATH entry equal to the session folder, which errs toward passing over. Deferred as RS-A-sharefold. Repro idea: a session on `//srv/k` and a value naming `//srv/<U+212A>/x`, through `farGitConfig`; needs a server that has both shares.
- Not run: macOS locally, the two long suites (CI), a real hook, NTFS (this machine's temp drive is ReFS; the system drive's temp folder, NTFS, ran the repro script).
- Round 1, on f97553a..3e1ff9f (PR #39). Fable (Windows and WSL, exported copies) on the code, Sonnet on the docs. No input that f97553a refuses and 3e1ff9f accepts: CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run, on 8,941 Windows and 13,417 WSL cases, and `dotEnd` compared with Windows itself on 56,328 path texts (only NUL device forms left over). Three findings in the changed code, each CONFIRMED here and fixed in the batch after 3e1ff9f:
  - The exact key made one folder many. A junction keeps its target in the case its maker wrote, and `localPath` gives that text back. Repro: under `.git/modules` (through `farModules`) or `.git/refs` (through `farGitConfig`), folders `a/a/a/a`, and beside each `a` 60 junctions to it, each target written in another mix of upper and lower case. f97553a: 5 listings (12 for refs), under 0.2 s. 3e1ff9f: 605 (612), 4.8 s. Fable measured 65 s for 1,800 junctions (not re-run). Fix: `onDisk(real)`. Where a link was followed, the path is respelled by `fs.realpathSync.native`, taken only when it differs in letter case alone (rounds 2 and 3 changed this: see there), so a path on a `subst` or mapped drive keeps its own text. Used in `linkedEntries`, in `moduleGitdirs` (for a gitdir's `objects` folder too) and for the real path in `farGitConfig`. With the fix: 5 (12). The test holds the same shape smaller (`a/a/a`, 20 junctions beside each, 60 in all) and compares its listings with the same shape whose targets are written as on disk.
  - A repo above the session was checked twice when the session folder is a junction whose target is written in another case: 308 listings on f97553a, 616 on 3e1ff9f, the same answer. Fixed by the same `onDisk`: 308. Left as it is: a session path given in another case than the disk's, and through a link, is still looked at twice above the link (same answer, at most double).
  - False refusals. The dot rule ran on every value whose key ends like a path setting. `[alias] adddir = add .`, `lsdir = !ls -la ./src` and `newworktree = worktree add ../wt` answered `odd-config-path` (`''` on f97553a). Fix: `WORDS_KEY`, the sections `alias` and `pretty`, where the user picks the key's name and the value is a command line or a log format. Those values are checked as on f97553a (links followed, `C:x` and `\x` refused), without the dot rule. `distinctPaths` keeps them apart from path settings, so `adddir = k.` can't stand in for `attributesFile = k.`. Still refused, by the rule: `core.attributesFile = notes.../attrs`, and a key of one's own such as `x.path` with a URL holding `a. /b`.
  - Tests: new assertions in the same test, each failing on 3e1ff9f's `gitbin.js`. 11 single changes of this batch: 10 fail the test. The eleventh, `onDisk` taking any respelling, gives the same answers on a plain drive; it differs on a `subst` or mapped drive, and this machine has neither (untested). The 16 earlier single changes still fail (15 on Windows, 1 in WSL), and the test passes in WSL. Lists: `muts-fold-r1.json` and `muts-fold.json` in the scratchpad.
  - What `onDisk` costs, CONFIRMED here: `.git/refs` with 3,000 junctions, each to a folder of its own, through `farGitConfig`: 1.05 to 1.08 s on f97553a, 1.32 to 1.41 s with the fix (three runs each, alternating which ran first).
  - The docs review (Sonnet): stale counts and pointers in this file and a tricolon in MANUAL's `odd-config-path` row, fixed in the same batch.
  - Test notes from the review: `dotEnd`'s first regex is a quick pre-test, so dropping it changes no answer; the control `'.../k'.slice(1)` repeated `../k` and is gone.
  - Deferred from round 1, older behavior:
    - RS-A-dottracked (fixed since, section RS-A-dottracked), FLAGGED as serious (access), CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run, seen on f97553a and 3e1ff9f: `gitfacts.js` `farWorktree` looks at a tracked folder `d.` by its literal name, finds nothing and passes it over, while git on Windows opens `d`. With `d` a junction, where it leads is not checked. Repro: a repo whose index holds `d./f`, a junction `d` to a place the check refuses, then the commit sweep.
    - RS-A-manylinks, CONFIRMED here for 3,000 links (above), INFERRED beyond: a followed link in a gitdir costs about 0.35 ms on f97553a and 0.45 ms with the fix, and the gitdir budget counts it as one entry of 100,000. Repro: `.git/refs` with N junctions to N folders, `farGitConfig` timed.
    - `moduleGitdirs` has no entry budget of its own (CONFIRMED by Fable in the code, not re-run, seen on f97553a and 3e1ff9f; only `MAX_MODULE_GITDIRS` and the depth stop it). With the keys right its cost is as on f97553a. Kept with RS-A-biglisting. Repro: `.git/modules` holding very many folders with no HEAD, `farModules` timed.
    - `farModules` called alone with `gitdir: ../g.` and a real folder named `g.`: `unreadable-config` on f97553a, `''` on 3e1ff9f (CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run). INFERRED harmless: git refuses such a pointer (see "Not a fault" above).
- Round 2, on 3e1ff9f..edd988d. Fable on the code, Sonnet on the docs. No input that f97553a or 3e1ff9f refuses and edd988d accepts where git would open something unchecked: CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run (96 configs and 6,000 random `.lfsconfig` lists on the three commits; the 709 that 3e1ff9f refuses and edd988d accepts each hold an `alias.` or `pretty.` key; none that f97553a refuses). That git and git-lfs never open an alias's or a log format's value as a path is INFERRED. Findings in the changed code, fixed in the batch after edd988d:
  - The walk cost was not closed, CONFIRMED here. A junction's target can name its folder in ways that differ in more than letter case: with the index stream's name after it (`a::$INDEX_ALLOCATION` opens `a`), or by a short name. Round 1's `onDisk` left those as they were, and each was a key of its own. Repro: round 1's shape (`a/a/a/a`, 60 junctions beside each `a`, each target in another mix of cases) with `::$INDEX_ALLOCATION` added to every target. f97553a: 15 listings under `.git/modules`, 22 under `.git/refs`. edd988d: 605 and 612, 7.6 s. Fable measured 18 s and 34 s on 8 levels of 80 (not re-run). Fix: `onDisk` gives `{ key, at }`. The key is always the path as `fs.realpathSync.native` gives it; `at`, where the walk goes on, is that path only when it differs from the one found in letter case alone (changed in round 3, below). ## Next version: RS-A-storagewalk (2026-11-10, branch `claude/rs-a-storagewalk` off afe9bf9, merged in PR #38 as f97553a)

The user pulled it in on 2026-11-10 ("Yes, fix storagewalk"), after PR #37 merged.

- The fault, CONFIRMED here on afe9bf9 (Windows): `lfs.storage` in 200 spellings of one folder with 10,100 entries through `farLfsEntries` is accepted in 2.7 s, with 200 `statSync` and 200 `readdirSync` calls. 52,000 spellings of an empty folder (16 parts of `./` or `.//` each) are refused after 4.9 s and 45,636 calls of each. `farPathValue` kept its set of walked folders for one value only, and the work limit doesn't count a store's walk.
- The fix (`gitbin.js`, both copies): the folders `lfs.storage` values led to are kept for one check (`STORES`, set by `limitedWalk` like the lookup cache), by the exact path `localPath` found (lower-cased on Windows in 3dbc23c, which round 1 showed wrong; see below). A folder or file already seen under the same path text costs no disk call. Two limits bound what that leaves: more than 64 different folders (`MAX_LFS_STORES`), and no further folder once those before it held 20,000 looked-at entries between them (`MAX_STORE_ENTRIES`; `linkedEntries` reports the count through `tally`). Past either the answer is `many-config-paths`.
- Why two limits: the first version had only the 64. Folders of files are cheap (64 of 12,000 files: 1.3 s), but 64 stores laid out as git-lfs does it (`objects/ab/cd`, 10,100 folders each) were accepted in 45 s, CONFIRMED here before the commit. One such store takes 0.65 to 0.8 s on Windows: that is older behavior (the store budget of 10,000 entries), not changed here.
- After the fix, CONFIRMED here: the 200 spellings take 22 ms on Windows and 18 ms in WSL with one call of each. The 52,000 spellings are refused in 0.46 s (WSL 0.23 s), by the work limit. Nested stores on Windows: one 0.8 s, two 1.4 s (accepted), a third after two of 10,000 refused at 1.6 to 1.7 s (WSL 0.15 s). The most accepted, three nested stores of 9,997, 10,000 and 10,000 looked-at entries: 2.3 to 2.4 s on Windows, 0.23 to 0.27 s in WSL (`probe-sw-worst3.js`). That is above the "1 to 2 seconds" SECURITY.md gives for the work limit's slowest inputs, and SECURITY.md says so for the stores. A limit of 10,000 would bring it to about 1.6 s but refuse any second store after a full one; kept at 20,000 (a judgment, for the user to overrule).
- The test: in "git pointers: many path values are checked once each, and too many are refused (next version)", counted calls of `fs.statSync` and `fs.readdirSync`. It fails on afe9bf9's `gitbin.js` in both copies (`list: 200` for 1). 20 single changes of the fix: 17 fail the test (16 on Windows, "a store's answer is dropped" in WSL only, where links can be made; the key's case on Windows only, since round 1 the lower-casing put back). The other three give the same answers as the fix: the cap answering `''` after setting the limit below 0, and the store set kept or not put back after a check (each shows only together with the other). The list is `muts-sw.json` in the session's scratchpad, not kept in the repo.

## Next version: RS-A-manyvalues (2026-11-09, branch `claude/rs-a-manyvalues` off bb493f5, merged in PR #37 as afe9bf9)

The user's "Yes, fix manyvalues", after PR #36 merged.

- CONFIRMED here on bb493f5 (`probe-manyvalues.js`, scratchpad, through the real `farGitConfig` on scratch repos, Windows): 20,000 lines of `file = a` took 4.2 s (Fable saw 6.4 s), the same with 20,000 different values (5.0 s) or 20,000 different keys (4.2 s), each accepted.
- Worse than reported, CONFIRMED here: 1,000 lines of `worktree = w` took 80 s, and 20,000 did not finish in 120 s. Each `core.worktree` value added up to four folders to the list every path value is tried from, repeats included, so the work grew with the square of the count. 200 different worktrees with 200 different files took 6.8 s.
- Reach: `farGit` (the repo's config files, includes followed) and `farLfsEntries` (`.lfsconfig`, from the worktree, the index or HEAD). Each file is at most 8 MB. That a real hook dies on its limit is INFERRED: no real hook was run on it. It needs a `.git/config` or `.lfsconfig` an attacker wrote.
- Fix, in `lib/gitbin.js`, both copies: `distinctPaths` keeps each different value once, whatever its key. A `core.worktree` counts once for each folder its file is in (it is taken from there), and `lfs.storage` is a kind of its own (its folder is walked). More than 200 different values (`MAX_PATH_VALUES`), or more than 8 different `core.worktree` values (`MAX_WORKTREE_VALUES`), in 578532e (the review batch below changed both numbers), keep git out with a new reason, `many-config-paths`, before anything is walked. `farLfsEntries` now walks its path values after the whole file is read, so a file with a bad path value before an include answers `lfs-config` where it answered the path's reason; both keep git out.
- After the fix, same probe: every case answers in 6 to 51 ms. The slowest accepted one is 100 different `lfs.storage` values (51 ms). The slowest shapes still accepted, CONFIRMED here (`probe-manyvalues-worst.js`, scratchpad): 8 worktrees with 192 other values took 220 ms, 375 ms with 40 parts in each value, and 285 ms with 192 `lfs.storage` values. Not run: 200 values near the 32,767-character limit each.
- Test: "many path values are checked once each, and too many are refused" (`handoff-checks.test.js`, both copies). It counts the calls of `path.resolve`, no timing. It fails on bb493f5's file and on ten single changes (`mutate-manyvalues.js`, scratchpad). Eight fail here. The other two (the two kinds in `distinctPaths`) need symlinks, which this machine can't make, so they were run in WSL (node 18), where both fail and the test passes with the fix.
- `MANUAL.md` section 16 has the new reason's row.
- Not run: a real hook on these inputs, the two long suites, macOS. CI covers the last two after a push.
- Round 1, on bb493f5..578532e (PR #37). Fable (Windows and WSL) on the code, Sonnet on the docs. No input went from refused to accepted: CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run, on 51 crafted cases and about 7,950 random repos; the two `.lfsconfig` reorder cases were the only answers that differ.
- Regression in that commit, CONFIRMED here (`probe-manyvalues-r1.js`, scratchpad, git 2.56.0.windows.4): with `init.defaultSubmodulePathConfig=true` at `git init`, `git submodule add` writes `submodule.<name>.gitdir = .git/modules/<name>` into the superproject's config, one different value for each submodule, and the key matches `PATH_KEY`. 201 of them answered `many-config-paths` where bb493f5 answered `''`. Fixed in the batch: `MAX_PATH_VALUES` is 2,000, the same as `MAX_MODULE_GITDIRS`. 2,000 such entries are accepted in 0.6 s. Of git 2.56's own config names (`git help -c`), 40 match `PATH_KEY`, and seven have a name part: `submodule.<name>.gitdir`, `includeIf.<condition>.path`, `guitool.<name>.needsFile` and four `<tool>.path` names, so the submodule one is the only one that grows with the repo (CONFIRMED here by that list; git-lfs and git-annex names not checked).
- Older behavior the count limit did not bound, CONFIRMED here: 8 worktrees with 192 values of 31,700 characters each (6 MB) took 31.6 s (Fable: 32.9 s, and 32.0 s in WSL), accepted, the same before and after 578532e. Bounded in the batch, since it is the same function and one line: the values' length together may not pass 256 KB (`MAX_PATH_VALUE_TEXT`). That input is now refused in 25 ms. A value over 32,767 characters isn't counted toward it: it is refused for itself, and counting it made one long value answer `many-config-paths` where it answers `odd-config-path` (seen as two failures of the long-path test in `--sums --quick`, fixed before the push). `MAX_WORKTREE_VALUES` went from 8 to 4, since each one multiplies the work of every other value and a real repo sets one or two.
- The slowest inputs still accepted after the batch (wrong: round 2 found far slower ones), measured here on Windows: 1,996 values of 130 characters with 4 worktrees 1.4 s, 1,996 short values with 4 worktrees 1.2 s, 2,000 short values with none 0.5 s, 8 values of 32,000 characters with 4 worktrees 0.7 s. Not run after the batch: WSL timings (Fable saw Linux up to 2.6 times slower on such shapes before it).
- Still open from that round, CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run, older behavior: `lfs.storage` given as 200 spellings of one folder with 10,100 entries is walked once for each spelling's first sight, 3.1 s on Windows. The limit of 2,000 values lets more spellings through than 200 did. Deferred as RS-A-storagewalk, flagged as the same class. Repro: a folder of 10,100 entries, `[lfs]` with `storage = ../x/../big` in 200 spellings, through `farGitConfig`, timed. Fix idea: key the walked set by the real folder before the walk (it is, by `real`; the cost is reaching `real`), or one `lfs.storage` value at most.
- Docs from that round: MANUAL section 16's `.lfsconfig` row and SECURITY.md's limits list now name the new reason, and the row's "the same value repeated counts once" says "under the same kind of setting".
- The test after that batch (rewritten since, see round 2): boundaries at 2,000 and 2,001 values, 4 and 5 worktrees, 8 and 9 values of 29,951 characters, and 300 submodule gitdirs accepted. Eleven single changes to the fix fail it, nine here and the two link ones in WSL (node 18).
- Round 2, on 578532e..9aaf498 (Fable, Windows and WSL). The three limits of that batch (2,000 values, 256 KB together, 4 worktrees) are gone again: the round showed they bound three numbers and not the time, and refuse real repos. What it found, each CONFIRMED here unless said (`probe-manyvalues-r2.js`, scratchpad):

One worktree 500 existing folders down with 1,999 values 178.5 s, and four spellings of one existing folder with a 4,000-character path with 200 values 254 s in WSL (both CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run). 578532e refused these shapes at 201 values, so 9aaf498 made it worse.

- The fix after round 2: the limit is on the work, counted where it is done. While one repo's path values are checked (`limitedWalk`), each walk in `localPath` counts 256, twice its text's length, and for each part an eighth of the path up to it; past 8 MiB counted (`MAX_PATH_WORK`) the walk answers "not local" and the check answers `many-config-paths`. The limit is put back to none when the check returns. A value is also tried once for each different folder and each different text (on Windows the two readings of a value are the same text, so every try ran twice). `MAX_WORKTREE_VALUES` is 16, since every value's tries are built from all those folders before one is walked. `distinctPaths` only drops repeats now.
- Sizes, CONFIRMED here with an unlimited copy of the file that reports its count: 2,000 submodule gitdirs count 3.06 million (4.09 million with names of 135 characters, 5.58 million in a repo eight folders further down); 20,000 different short values 29.6 million; 4 long worktrees with 100 values 28.1 million; 8 worktrees with 192 values of 31,700 characters 123 million.

The slowest answer found is that 1.4 s. Not run: a search for a slower shape under the limit (the next review is asked for one), macOS.

## Next version: RS-F-codexwalk, and the items kept as they are (2026-11-09, branch `claude/rs-f-codexwalk` off 4407d3c, the same branch and PR as the section "RS-A-longpath, RS-A-spread and RS-A-migrate")

The last items of the user's "work on all of them one at a time". One fix, and nine items looked at again and left, each with what was checked.

- RS-F-codexwalk, CONFIRMED here on 13889c1 (`probe-codexwalk.js`, scratchpad, a scratch Codex home with empty folders): `oldCodexCopies` took 0.7 s on a cache 20 wide at each of 3 levels and 1.6 to 2.2 s on one 30 wide (931 folders listed), with no sign that it stopped. The time grows with the folders listed, 1.7 to 3 ms each here on a deep temp path on C:. That a cache 200 wide (40,201 folders listed) runs past session start's limit is INFERRED from that rate, not run. The cache is the user's own disk.
- Fix, in `lib/health.js`, both copies: `maxFolders`, 1,000 by default. The search stops after looking in that many folders and says so in `incomplete` ("it stopped after looking in 1000 folders"), which `--copies` already reports as a search that stopped early ("could not be checked" when nothing was found). Session start says so only when it found a copy, and is silent otherwise. A cache 45 wide now stops at 1,000 folders in 2.1 s. A real cache has a marketplace, plugin and version level, and a few hundred folders at most.
- Test: "the search of Codex's cache stops after a number of folders and says so" (`handoff-checks.test.js`, both copies): the whole cache at the default and at exactly its 44 folders, cut short at 43, and the folders opened counted at a limit of 10 and of 0. It fails on 13889c1's file and on four mutations (`mutate-codexwalk.js`, scratchpad): the count never grows, one folder more than the limit, stopping without saying so, saying so without stopping. Its first run failed with the fix in place, on a count of 45 worked out by hand (LESSONS).
- Not run: a real Codex session start on a wide cache, the two long suites, macOS and Linux. CI covers the last two after a push.
- Changelog note: the Codex copy's search for an old jb-memory-codex in Codex's plugin cache now stops after 1,000 folders and says that it stopped early. Before, a very large cache could hold session start up.

Looked at again and kept as they are. The user sees each one and can pull it in:

- RS-H-compactrace, still CONFIRMED at `decide()` level only. Reach, INFERRED from the order of events, not run: it needs a compaction's entry written between one hook's transcript read and its save, with a second hook starting in that gap. Claude Code waits for `PostToolUse` and `UserPromptSubmit` hooks before the next request (their output goes into it), and a compaction is a request of its own, so a hook of ours isn't running when one ends. `hooks.json` sets no `async`. The effect, if it happened, is two skipped steps in the next cycle with the top step still asking. The fix would be a cycle identity in the state, a bigger change to code that has had five rounds of race reviews.
- RS-H-stampcap (section "Next version: four deferred items") and RS-H-bigpoint (section "RS-H-nopoint and RS-H-staleseen"), both crafted state only. Kept: the state file is the user's own, and a hand edit can silence the watch in a plainer way (`done: true` with the top level), so neither is a line an attacker has to cross. No crash in either.
- RS-W-gitcap, INFERRED by Fable, not re-run. Read here: past the system's limit for one environment value, `spawnSync` fails and the launcher prints "could not start" and exits 1 (`run()` in `trailmark.js`), so the hook doesn't run and says so. It needs every fact at its cap and 4,095-character paths, which no real session was seen to make.
- RS-D-oddanswer: kept, it is the closed side. An answer that isn't `true` deletes nothing, and the old draft stays until the next push.
- RS-D-gonedraft, now CONFIRMED here for the part that was untested: `gh api repos/<repo>/releases/1 --jq .draft` (an id that doesn't exist, gh 2.103.3) exits 1, and the script runs under `set -euo pipefail`, so the job goes red with the new draft in place. Kept: a red job is seen and a rerun finishes it.
- RS-D-publishgap: no repro, and no way to narrow it. GitHub's delete call has no "only if still a draft" form.
- RS-F-ninenotes: needs 9 notes, and session start makes at most 5 (unchanged).

New from the review of 13889c1..40ee116, CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run, seen on 40ee116, not a regression:

- RS-F-codexlinks: `maxFolders` bounds the folders looked in, not the time. At the limit of 1,000 folders the search took 0.2 s with empty folders, about 1 s with 200 subfolders each, and 14.4 s with 60 junctions in each (5.3 s with 20), since each link is read and checked. 200 junctions each is about 48 s by that rate (INFERRED), past the hook limit. It needs a Codex cache with tens of thousands of links, on the user's own disk. Fix idea: a time budget, as `otherCopies` has. Repro: a cache of 1,000 folders with 60 junctions each, then `oldCodexCopies`, timed. Also from that round: no `jb-memory-codex` folder was missed without `incomplete` being set at any limit from 0 to 46 on either system, and ext4's unsorted listing passes the test.

Round 3, on 40ee116..4bc17dc (Fable, Windows only, through the real `farGitConfig` on scratch repos): nothing CONFIRMED that needs a fix, so the stop rule is met for this branch. All of it CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run: 86 crafted configs (seven path keys, values up to 2 MB, 60 includes, an include chain of 49 files) took 470 ms at worst with no throw, and each got the answer 13889c1's file gives; round 2's input took 7 ms against 5.9 s with the `exactPointer` line removed; with that line alone removed, all eight cases of the counting assertion fail; the test puts the five `path` functions back as it found them. Not run in that round: WSL, the 8 MB timing, RS-F-codexlinks' numbers.

New from the review of 4407d3c..13889c1, older code, CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run, seen on 13889c1 and the same on 4407d3c:

- RS-A-manyvalues (fixed since, section RS-A-manyvalues): many short path values aren't covered by the length limit. A 200 KB config of `[x]` and 20,000 lines of `file = a` takes 6.4 s on Windows and 1.2 s on Linux, and it is linear (50,000 lines 15 s, 200,000 lines 56 s). That a real hook reaches its limit on it is INFERRED. It needs a `.git/config` an attacker wrote. Flagged: the same class as RS-A-longpath. Fix idea: check each distinct value once, or stop at a count of path values. Repro: that config, through `farGitConfig`, timed.
- RS-H-capdeep: inside the hook limit, and it needs folders 300 deep on disk (unchanged; `MAX_PATH_TEXT` doesn't touch it, such paths are far under 32,767 characters).
- RS-A-foundcommits: linear, 1.4 s at 130,000 keys (unchanged).

## Next version: RS-A-longpath, RS-A-spread and RS-A-migrate (2026-11-09, branch `claude/rs-f-codexwalk` off 4407d3c, stacked on PR #35; commit 13889c1 and the review batch after it)

The next items of the user's "work on all of them one at a time". Three items that are one cause in two files.

- RS-A-longpath, CONFIRMED here on 4407d3c (`probe-longpath.js`, scratchpad, through the real `farGitConfig` on scratch repos): one 8 MB path value in `.git/config` took 3.0 s as `core.attributesFile` and 13.8 s as `core.worktree`, and 1 MB took 0.4 s and 1.2 s. The answer was `''`: the value was accepted. That a real hook then dies on its limit is INFERRED: no real hook was run on it.
- RS-A-spread, CONFIRMED here on 4407d3c, and reachable, which the note below had as HYPOTHESIZED: a path value of 130,000 parts (`a/` repeated, a 260 KB value) makes `farGitConfig` throw `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` from `localPath`'s spread (100,000 parts passed; the count depends on the stack). What a hook does then is INFERRED from the code: `hookMemory` in the launcher catches the error and finds no memory folder, so trailmark is silent in that repository where it would otherwise say git was kept out. No git runs and no code runs. A launcher run at session start in an unprepared scratch repo with that value exited 0 with no output, which fits and proves little, since a repo not set up is silent anyway.
- Both need a `.git/config` an attacker wrote (an unpacked archive, say; a clone doesn't carry one).
- Fix, in `gitbin.js`, both copies: `MAX_PATH_TEXT` = 32,767 characters, the longest path any system opens (Windows; Linux stops at 4,096 bytes, macOS at 1,024). `exactPointer` and `localPath` refuse a longer text before any work, and the three places that join a config value to a folder (`farPathValue`, the `core.worktree` loop in `farGit`, and an include in `farConfig`) answer `odd-config-path` when the joined text is over the limit, so it isn't called a network path. An alternates line over the limit is still answered `network-config` (INFERRED by Fable from the code, a refusal either way). On Windows a value of 300,000 `../` is still accepted, as before: Windows collapses it to a short path, which is then checked where it leads. Every caller of `localPath` was listed (about 45, in `gitbin.js`, `gitfacts.js`, `trailmark.js`, `contextwatch.js`, `plainfile.js`, `transcript.js`, `health.js`): each reads its `null` as "not local" and refuses or compares by name, which is the closed side for a text no system can open. After the fix an 8 MB value and the 500,000-part value are each refused in about 10 to 25 ms.
- Test: "a config path value longer than any path is refused without being walked" (`handoff-checks.test.js`, both copies): 40,000 letters and 200,000 parts as `core.attributesFile`, `core.worktree` and `include.path`, `gitFacts` reporting the refusal, 4,000 letters still accepted, 32,767 letters refused as `odd-config-path` once joined, and `localPath` and `sameFolder` called directly. It fails on 4407d3c's file (`''` where `odd-config-path` is expected) and on each of the four added lines removed alone (`mutate-longpath.js`, scratchpad), each at the assertion that names it. The limit in `exactPointer` is the fifth line. It was taken out in 40ee116, because with the include join checked no test failed without it, and round 2 showed what it was for (below), so it is back with an assertion of its own: no function of Node's `path` is handed a text over the limit, for four keys and two shapes of value. Counted, not timed.
- Review of 4407d3c..13889c1 (Fable, security and regression, on Windows and on Linux in WSL). One finding that needed a fix, in the test: its "still accepted" case used one name of 4,000 letters, and on Linux an `include.path` that long is answered `unreadable-config` (a name stops at 255 bytes), before and after this change. Reproduced here in WSL (`probe-fine.js`, scratchpad), and the case is now three parts of 200 letters, accepted on Windows and in WSL. macOS is INFERRED, CI runs it. Its second finding, the `network-config` word for an include at the limit, was reproduced and fixed with the third join check. CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run: every caller reads `null` as a refusal, git itself refuses a 40,001-character `core.worktree` on both systems, old and new migration trims agree on 549,525 strings, and the parent fails the test on both systems. Round 2, on 13889c1..40ee116 (Fable, Windows and WSL): one CONFIRMED regression in that batch, reproduced here (`probe-dotdot.js`, scratchpad) and fixed. Without the limit in `exactPointer` an over-long value reached `path.resolve` and `path.join` before the join checks, and those normalize the whole text: 500,000 parts of `a/` and then 20,000 pairs of `b/../` (1.05 MB) took 5.9 s here (7.1 s for Fable, 41.7 s in WSL) against 11 ms with the line back. The answer was `odd-config-path` either way, so only time was lost. CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run: git for Windows 2.56 can't open a `../` value of 36,000 characters or more, so the accepted 300,000 `../` case names nothing git reaches; the long-path test fails on 13889c1 only at the include's 32,767 letters, and passes on 40ee116 on both systems.
- RS-A-migrate, CONFIRMED here (`probe-migrate.js`, scratchpad): `samePath`'s trim in `tools/migrate-from-jb-memory.js` is quadratic on a path as Linux resolves it, where a backslash is part of a name: 200,000 backslashes and a letter took 26.0 s, 100,000 took 6.2 s. On Windows `path.resolve` collapses the run first, so it isn't reached there. Its input is the user's own Claude and Codex config, in a tool run by hand. Fixed with the look-behind, as in the other trims: 1 ms at 200,000, and old and new gave the same result on 321,845 strings (300,000 random, and every string of four characters up to length 7). No test: the tool has no test file and exports nothing, so the proof is the probe on the two regexes, not a suite. Not run on Linux: the probe used `path.posix` on Windows.
- The search for the pattern after the fix (`find-trims.js`, scratchpad, shown to find the nine there are): the five `/[\r\n]+$/` trims in `gitbin.js` read at most 4,096 characters (settled in PR #31), `isGitPart`'s `/[. ]+$/` sees one path part, `hook-milestone.js`'s `\d+$` is inside a pattern anchored at both ends, and `store.js` 534 already has the look-behind.
- Not run: a real hook on these inputs, the two long suites, macOS and Linux. CI covers the last two after a push.
- Changelog note: a repository config holding a path value longer than any real path (megabytes, or hundreds of thousands of parts) is now refused at once. Before, the git check could run for seconds on it, or stop with an error that left trailmark silent in that repository. Both copies. The migration script no longer stalls on a config path holding a very long run of backslashes (Linux and macOS).

## Next version: RS-H-nopoint and RS-H-staleseen (2026-11-09, branch `claude/rs-h-nopoint-staleseen` off 7ff6f3f)

The user's "work on all of them one at a time", on the open deferred items, in the order recommended: these two first, then RS-H-compactrace, RS-H-stampcap, RS-A-longpath, RS-W-gitcap, RS-F-codexwalk, the three RS-D items, and last the five kept with a reason (RS-F-ninenotes, RS-H-capdeep, RS-A-foundcommits, RS-A-migrate, RS-A-spread).

- RS-H-nopoint, CONFIRMED here on 7ff6f3f (`probe-nopoint.js`, scratchpad): a state with level 3 and a point that is absent, `null`, negative or a string stays silent at 320k in a 400k window, where the same state with point 167000 starts over and asks. A point of 0 already started over. Reach, CONFIRMED from the code: every published version (0.6.0 on) saves the point with each request, so only a state file edited by hand has this.
- Fix, in `decide()` (`plugins/trailmark/scripts/hook-context-watch.js`; the Codex copy has no context watch): a saved point that isn't a number above 0 counts as a smaller point's, and the 75% line and the first step are then measured from the current point. Such a state starts over at the first step it reaches and asks once more, also in a window that didn't change. A state with a usable point is read as before.
- Test: "a saved state with no usable point starts over at a step of the current point" (`context-watch.test.js`). It fails on 7ff6f3f's two lines, and on four more mutations (`mutate-nopoint.js`, scratchpad): the old condition alone, the line measured from the unusable point, a point of 0 counted as usable, a usable point always counted as grown. 25 of 25 tests named "context watch" pass with the fix.
- RS-H-staleseen, CONFIRMED here on 7ff6f3f (`probe-staleseen.js`, scratchpad), and left as it is, by design: with 31 MB of transcript after the saved `seenAt` the read gets back to it and nothing is asked; with 33, 40 and 100 MB it doesn't (`reached: false`), the state is cleared and the first request goes out again. That request saves a new `seenAt`, so it is one more request per 32 MB. The read took 18 ms at every size past the cap, so no time is lost. Not changed because the code can't tell a compaction in the part it didn't read from none, and asking once more is the safe side (the comment in `decide()`). Reach, INFERRED: a request comes in the last 15% under the compaction point, which is at most 150k tokens of context, and 32 MB of transcript in that span needs lines far larger than what the model is given (a tool result's stored copy of a large file, say). No real session was seen doing it. A fix idea if it ever shows up: the hook moves `seenAt` forward when it keeps a state whose read reached.
- Not run: the real hook and launcher on a state with no point (the test is at `decide()` level), the two long suites, macOS and Linux. CI covers the last two after a push.
- Review of 7ff6f3f..df6b2fd (Opus, regression): nothing CONFIRMED that needs a code fix, so the stop rule is met. CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run: old and new `decide()` gave the same result on 600,000 calls (20,000 sequences of 30) wherever the saved point was usable; a state with an unusable point never asked more than once in a sequence, and no request saved an unusable point, so it can't loop; the new test fails 11 assertions on 7ff6f3f's file, the first being the request for an absent point. Two wordings said less than the code, both read here and fixed in the batch commit: the hook's header comment, and the changelog note below. That wording batch had no review of its own.
- One more change, for a saved point of 0 only: its 75% line and first step were 0 before, so nothing cleared it below the steps, and they are now the current point's. A hand edit only, like the rest.
- Changelog note: a session state edited by hand so that it has no usable compaction point no longer keeps the context watch silent. It starts over at the next step the context reaches and asks once more, whether or not the window changed.

New from this review, older code, CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run, seen on df6b2fd:

- RS-H-bigpoint: a saved point that is a usable number far above any real one keeps the watch silent, as it did before this change. A hand edit only. Repro: `decide({ level: 3, firstAt: 900, seen: 160000, seenAt: 900, point: 999999999 }, {}, <320k in a 400k window>, cfg, 1000)` returns no request.

## Next version: RS-H-flipwipe (2026-11-09, branch `claude/rs-h-flipwipe-deferred-8469ea` off d0b90fe, merged in PR #33)

The user's "Fix deferred item RS-H-flipwipe". Commit e4f04bd (the fix) and the review batch after it (wording only).

- RS-H-flipwipe, CONFIRMED here on d0b90fe (`probe-flipwipe.js`, scratchpad, and the two tests below). It is wider than the note said: with a bigger window of 280k or 300k, every flip back to the 200k window sent the first request again, with no end (198k first, 201k nothing, 199k first, 202k nothing, 198k first). Both fail on d0b90fe, the first with the state `{}` at 201k and the second with a second ask at 199k. Five mutations here (`mutate-flipwipe.sh`, scratchpad) each fail a test: the old condition, no start-over at all, `ref` always the current point, the line alone from the current point, the first step alone from the current point. 24 of 24 context-watch tests pass.

The reach is wider than the note below assumed: `exactPointer` also takes config values, a quoted `\n` is two bytes in `.git/config` and one line break in the value, and the trim is quadratic on line breaks followed by another character. ## 0.6.2: the release step (2026-11-08, branch `claude/release-0-6-2-882412` off 14ff243)

Started on the user's "Yes, start it" (2026-11-08, relayed by the coordinating session). That covers this writing only: every push, PR, merge, tag and publish still needs the user's own word. Scope is what has merged: PR #22, PR #23 and PR #24. No shipped code changes here.

Version 0.6.2: fixes and wording, no new feature, the kind of release 0.6.1 was. Bumped where 0.6.1 was: the three `plugin.json` files (both Codex manifest copies among them) and MANUAL.md's version line, by a script of exact pairs. `git grep 0.6.1` outside the CHANGELOG, `dev/`, `SHA256SUMS` and `LESSONS.md` finds nothing (CONFIRMED). trailmark-watchdog stays 1.7.0: `git diff v0.6.1..HEAD -- tools/windows` is empty (CONFIRMED).

The CHANGELOG section was written from the batch H and batch W changelog notes and checked against the merged code (`gh pr diff 22`, `23`, `24`; `git diff v0.6.1..HEAD`). Two statements differ from the notes, because the code says otherwise:

- RS:658. The note says setup "no longer writes through a link". The batch's own re-check found the text was written nowhere: the junction's missing target was made as a folder and `init` threw EISDIR. The change matters on Windows only, since `'wx'` already refuses a link on Linux and macOS. The CHANGELOG says that.
- RS:638. The note says two hooks "no longer ask for the same first checkpoint twice", which is true, but `crossed` in `hook-context-watch.js` covers only a record read with no `seenAt`. ### 0.6.2 release checklist

1. Version bumps to 0.6.2 (above). Done on this branch.
2. CHANGELOG: a 0.6.2 section. Written 2026-11-08 and re-dated 2026-11-09 in PR #26. **Re-check the date at publish time**: if it publishes on another day, change the heading and run `--sums --quick` again. That is a new commit on `main`, and the job replaces the draft from it.

4. `dev/release-check.sh` passes on the final commit; CI green on all 9 jobs. Done: PR 25 merged with all 9 passing.
5. `dev/release-check.sh --zip <dir>` from a clean tree at the release commit; record the commit and the zip hash here. Check that the run reports the inner sums verified and no links, and that the zip's `.sha256` matches `sha256sum`. Then unzip it, look for a string only this release adds (`installed in a folder whose path` in `hook-session-start.js`; `"websiteURL"` in both Codex manifests; `"version": "0.6.2"` in all three), and check that `CLAUDE.md`, `PLAYBOOK.md`, `LESSONS.md` and `dev/` are absent. The draft's zip, from PR #26's merge commit `54b75a0848462ceb03480fb49d2aaacdeaae9e9e` (`release draft` run 42602389651), passed the same checks on 2026-11-09: `trailmark-0.6.2.zip` sha256 `19aefb533b2f2712a5f1c33b088bc3931755a91fbd4f90fa2ba6378e315cecca`, 114 files, no links, inner sums and `.sha256` verified, its `SHA256SUMS` the same as the repo's, the strings above found, the four dev-only paths absent. `installed in a folder whose path` is in the Claude Code copy of `hook-session-start.js` only, as the CHANGELOG says.
6. Publish: only when the user says so, after PR #26 merges and the job makes the draft. Before publishing, check that the draft targets the merge commit's full id and holds the zip and `.sha256`, that the commit's 9 `tests.yml` jobs passed, and that the checks in item 5 pass on the draft's zip. Publishing the draft makes the tag `v0.6.2` at that commit. Then download the assets back into an empty folder and run `sha256sum -c`. If the job fails, build the zip by hand from the merge commit with `--zip <dir>` and publish as for 0.6.1. ### Deferred from batch H

- RS-H-addcommitdirs, fixed in PR #28 (section RS-H-addcommitdirs). Was: CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run (seen on 047dab7; older, `store.js addCommitDirs` unchanged). Flagged: the same class as RS-F-sinceFor, in the writer. `done()` walks every `swept` key for each `commitDirs` candidate per eviction, so 3,000 crafted entries of each take 8.2 s under the lock in `hook-milestone.js`, and 20,000 run past 180 s; the 9 s hook limit then kills it holding `.jbmem.lock`. Needs a commit command in another folder or sent to the background. Fix idea: cut `list` to `MAX_COMMIT_DIRS` plus the added folders before the loop, and index `swept` by `pathKey`. Repro: `node probe-addcommitdirs.js <pluginRoot> 3000` (notes folder, `batch-h/review1-security-probes/`).
- RS-H-capdeep, CONFIRMED by Fable for one start, not re-run, INFERRED for every start (seen on 047dab7; the real-path walk is older). 64 `swept` keys that are real folders 300 deep cost 3.9 s and 113,000 lstats, the sweep is skipped as out of time, and `swept` isn't rewritten when nothing was read, so each start repeats it with the "git was slow" warning. Inside the 10 s hook limit here, and it needs deep folders on disk. Repro: `node probe-sweep.js <pluginRoot> capdeep 300` (same folder).
- RS-H-samesince, closed in PR #29 (section "Next version: four deferred items"). Was: CONFIRMED by Opus with `decide()`, not re-run (seen on 047dab7; older logic). Two hooks that carry the same `since` (`reached:false`, `boundary` or a shrink) with the context still past the first step each reset and ask. This fix covers only RS:638's no-`since` shape. That parallel hooks reach it is INFERRED. Fix idea: the launcher passes the `seenAt` it read. Repro: `decide()` twice with `since: { reached: false }`, the second on the first's state.
- RS-F2b-limit, by design here (047dab7): past 64 folders in one session the oldest `swept` entry goes, so RS-F2b's loss can still happen there. About 11 KB per session at that size (arithmetic, Opus).
- RS:651, the rest: the warning is the cheap first step. The skills still take the path from `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` unchecked, and MANUAL.md has no row for the warning (row added in 0.6.2).
