Item 1's check: the reason was wrong for `JBMEM_GIT` too. On this machine (Node 26.13, Windows 11) variables of 32,768, 40,000, 120,000, 500,000 and 2,000,000 characters reached a `spawnSync` child whole, by length and sha256, with and without `--permission` (CONFIRMED, probe in the session's scratch folder). Linux's limit: WSL (kernel 6.18, 4 KiB pages) takes a `NAME=value` string of 131,072 bytes and refuses 131,073 with `E2BIG`, and the 30,000-character limit takes at most 90,000 bytes (CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run).

Tests, each shown failing before the text it pins changed (`--only 'codex skills'`):

- Against 9c76a10's skills: "codex skills: a later note that says trailmark's path isn't written stops the fallback too" failed with `tm-checkpoint: a later note stops the fallback`, and the renamed test with `tm-checkpoint: the marker at first mention` (CONFIRMED here and by Opus).
- Against 130c6e9's skills: those two with `the end of the fallback` and `a later note stops the fallback`, and the new "tm-memory-setup's hook check accepts a session-start note with no launcher" with `the note with no launcher` (CONFIRMED here and by Opus).
- bc038a2's assertion can't fail on the old text, which has one such sentence. Its proof is a mutation: a second, wider stop sentence added to all six skills fails with `tm-checkpoint: one stop on that phrase`, expected 2, actual 3 (CONFIRMED here; the same mutation passes with the parent's test file, CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run).
- `--sums --quick` at bc038a2's tree: all PASS, handoff-checks 164/252 with 88 skipped. `--only 'codex skills'` at bc038a2: 4/4.

### Changelog notes for the 0.6.2 section

Codex skills: when no session-start note is present but a note from a trailmark hook says trailmark's path isn't written, the skills now stop and tell the user instead of working the path out. No change for Claude Code users. The comment changes in `gitfacts.js` change no behavior and need no line.

|---|---|---|---|
| F | F1 to F8 (RS:647, 646, 634, 645, 635, 672, 709, and the comment riders RS:696, 698, 708) | Fable and Opus | `store.js`, `trailmark.js`, `health.js` (shared), `handoff-checks.test.js` (shared), in both copies |

Not in the scope:

- O7 (RS:637, the lost repeat ask) was optional and stays deferred: the user said "Keep it deferred" during 0.6.0 and has not pulled it in.
- The flagged-serious items the triage keeps deferred stay deferred: RS:651 (the Claude Code skills take `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` without the path check) and RS:658 (`createOnce` follows a planted junction on volumes without hard links).
- Everything in section 2 of the triage, with the reasons given there.

How the batches run (proposed 2026-11-07, waiting for the user's word to start):

1. This branch (the triage and this section, `dev/` only) goes to main first as a small PR, so each batch starts from a main that holds the scope.
2. Then one session per batch, each in its own worktree off main and with its own PR. Each owner runs its review rows as fresh subagents on `git diff <last reviewed>..HEAD`. Batch S does S1 to S4 now and leaves S5 for the end.
3. Test files: batch F owns `handoff-checks.test.js`, `sweep.test.js`, `commit-folders.test.js` and `copies.test.js`; batch O puts its tests in `security.test.js` or `hardening.test.js`. Both copies each time.
4. Shared files that will conflict: `SHA256SUMS` (F, O and S all change listed files) and this file. Merge one PR at a time; the next branch syncs with main, runs `--sums --quick` again and resolves this file by hand. Only the coordinating session edits this file's top line; a batch adds its own section.
5. Merge order: D first (it ships nothing), then F, O and S1 to S4 as each passes its round. S5 (the CHANGELOG section and version bumps) comes after F and O have merged, since it describes them, then the release checklist.
6. Load: four sessions running `--only` on one machine at once can slow tests (INFERRED). PR #14 made the known ones repeat or skip on their time sign, and every run has its own temp folder (`mktemp -d`, `release-check.sh:40`), but the 20 kept run folders are shared (`release-check.sh:106`) until D2 lands.

The commit-list tests repeat on that sign and then skip after a control (below). | `gitfacts.js` `commitsSince` (shared, by agreement), `store.js` `sinceSection` and `buildInjection`, launcher `sinceHandoff` |
| F4 (RS:645) | CONFIRMED, not ruled out: a cut inside the launcher's path reproduced through `buildInjection`. A shortened note is cut before a path and ends "... (a path is left out here)", at 1,200, 300 and 150 characters. | `store.js` `shortNote` |

The commit-list control (`sinceInTime`, `handoff-checks.test.js`), as of d4ab59c: the tests' sandbox sets `JBMEM_SHOW_SINCE=1`, and the launcher (`sinceHandoff`, both copies) then writes `trailmark since: late elapsed=<ms> left=<ms> budget=<ms> git=<ms>` to stderr when the commit list runs out of time. It only reports, no budget changes. Each session start that says git ran out of time must show the launcher's rule held (the time left is 5000 - elapsed - 1500 within 50 ms, with elapsed measured apart; git's budget is min(1,200, left), or 0 under 400 ms left) and that time really ran out: under 400 ms left before git ran, or git spent its budget less 300 ms (the runner refuses a call with under 250 ms left, so a real timeout spends at least budget - 250). After three in a row, the test skips only if the same lookup with 30 s answers. History: round 1 replaced a floor on the lookup alone (900 ms), which failed a slowdown before git (Opus #2), with whole-run floors; those held at 8 and 16 busy loops only because the first of three starts came in under 1,200 ms (up to 1,184 ms), and the duplicate session batch-f-2 saw a whole-run floor let a forced 0 ms budget skip at 37 to 68% CPU (CONFIRMED by batch-f-2, not re-run), so bfa24ad reads the launcher's report instead, and d4ab59c checks the rule behind `left` and `budget` (review round 3: a left of `5000 - elapsed - 4800` skipped all 14 at rest). Proof on d4ab59c's code with forced faults (scratch, `bf-force2.py` and `bf-force3.py`): at rest, nothing forced 14 pass; the left formula broken (`- 4800`), 14 fail; git's cap at 450 ms, 13 fail and 1 passes (git fit in 450 that time); budget 0, 14 fail (and 14 under 16 busy loops); 3.2 s before `sinceHandoff`, 14 skip; every git call out of time, 14 fail; git log failing, 8 fail; git log faked out of time, 12 fail; 48 busy loops, nothing forced, 12 skip and 2 pass. Earlier, on bfa24ad's code (scratch, `bf-force2.py` and `bf-force-slow.py`), the 14 commit-list tests in both copies: at rest, nothing forced 14 pass; the launcher's budget forced to 0 ms, 14 fail; a 3.2 s busy-wait before `sinceHandoff`, 14 skip (left 186 ms or less); every git call of `commitsSince` out of time, 14 fail; git log failing (not time), 8 fail; git log faked as out of time on its first call, 12 fail (it reports git 444 to 520 ms of 1,200: not a real timeout) and 2 pass. Under busy loops: 16, nothing forced 14 pass and budget 0 fails 14; 32, nothing forced 14 pass and budget 0 fails 14; 48, nothing forced 12 skip (git spent 954 to 1,155 ms of 1,200) and 2 pass. None of the nothing-forced runs failed (CONFIRMED, 2026-11-07).

Shipped wording for batch S (each in both copies unless named):

- Session start, the commit list: "Whether HEAD has commits since the handoff's HEAD <base> isn't known: git ran out of time. Run git log --oneline <base>..HEAD and check the next steps against it before redoing work."
- Session start, a count with no list: "The handoff was written at HEAD <base>; HEAD has N newer commit(s), not listed: git ran out of time" (or "git could not list them"), then the git log line. Its short form at a tight budget: "N commit(s) on HEAD since the handoff's HEAD <base>, not listed here: git ran out of time." ("...: git could not list them.", or "... to fit the budget." as before).
- write-handoff: "[trailmark] commit sweep skipped: this session's start isn't recorded, so commits no hook logged before now can't be looked for. Later sweeps start from this checkpoint."
- A shortened session-start note: the tail "... (a path is left out here)".
- `--copies` and session start, a cache folder that can't be read: "<where> could not be read" in place of "<where> has too many folders".
- Codex `--copies`: "trailmark: the Codex plugin cache could not be checked.", "trailmark: N old jb-memory-codex folder(s) in Codex's plugin cache; the search stopped early, so there may be more.", and under `--show-names` "  the search of the Codex plugin cache stopped early: <reason>", the reasons "Codex's home folder (CODEX_HOME) isn't a folder" and "it stopped at 10 folders".
- Codex session start: "(10 or more folders)" in the note and "10 or more folders named jb-memory-codex" in the warning.

Not user-facing: `JBMEM_SHOW_SINCE=1`, a test knob like `JBMEM_SHOW_GRANTS` (undocumented, as that one is), writes `trailmark since: late elapsed= left= budget= git=` to stderr when the commit list runs out of time.

Files outside batch F's list, by agreement with the coordinating session: `gitfacts.js` (F3) and the Codex `hook-session-start.js` lines 85 to 88 (F5).

Not load: the framing's cut note names the handoff's full path, longer on CI. Reproduced here with a longer TMPDIR (CONFIRMED). Fixed in the test (7 notes, still past the 300 cut). Session start makes at most 4 notes (Codex 5), so it isn't reached in practice. Repro: the F4 test with `[150, 9]` and `--only` under a TMPDIR 30 characters longer.
- RS-F2b, CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run (seen on c19ee63): F2 still loses a commit when the rejoining folder's `swept` entry is gone. `rec.swept` keeps 12 entries (`MAX_SWEPT`), so after 12 newer ones a rejoin falls back to `from` (`commitDirsAt` `[9000,9500]`). Needs 13 or more commit folders in one session. Repro: the F2 test ("a folder dropped past six and joined again ...") with 13 folders swept after the first drops.
- RS-F-sinceFor, CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run (seen on 92794c1; older code, 307e546 and 6df998d). Flagged: a crafted state file stops session start. `sinceFor` (`trailmark.js:517-518`, both copies) calls `sameFolder` (realpath calls) once per key of `swept` and `commitDirsAt` for each folder, and the 5 s sweep budget is checked only between folders. A record with 130,000 keys makes session start take 18 to 21 s, so the hook is killed, its context is lost and `.jbmem.lock` stays until the dead-pid rule breaks it (20.3 s on c19ee63 with `start` kept, 20.9 s on 92794c1 without it). Only a crafted file reaches it: normal writes cap both. Fix idea: compare `folderKey` first and realpath only the survivors. Repro: a record with no start, 130,000 `commitDirsAt` keys and 8 `commitDirs`, then a session start.
- RS-F-codexwalk, INFERRED by Fable (seen on 92794c1; ca46070's session-start call, older walk): Codex session start runs `oldCodexCopies` with no time budget, bounded only by 200 entries per folder over 3 levels. 0.018 ms per folder measured on a 20x20x20 cache, about 146 s extrapolated to full width; not reproduced. The cache is the user's own disk. Repro: a Codex cache 200 wide at each of 3 levels, then a Codex session start, timed.
- RS-F-enotdir2, CONFIRMED by Opus with ENOTDIR stubbed, not re-run (seen on 92794c1; `list()` in `health.js:134`, older): with `CODEX_HOME/plugins` a file, or CODEX_HOME reached through a file (Fable F3, INFERRED), Windows says nothing (ENOENT, `incomplete: ''`) and Linux and macOS say the Codex cache "could not be read" (ENOTDIR). The same class as round 1's #1, one level down; `otherCopies` uses the same `list()`. Repro: write a file at `<home>/plugins`, call `oldCodexCopies(home, {detail:true})` with and without an `fs.opendirSync` stub that throws ENOTDIR.

## Batch D: `dev/release-check.sh` (2026-11-07, branch `claude/batch-d-release-check-f5bf41` off d86b628)

Dev only: no file of the release zip or of `SHA256SUMS` changes. D1 to D3 are from the approved scope. D4 was added by the review-strategy session on 2026-11-07. Its own PR, which merges first.

| # | Item | Fix | Check: fails on d86b628, passes on HEAD |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | RS:707 (R3-2): after Ctrl-C the script exited 130, so a calling bash loop went on to its next round | `stop_suites` stops the suites, cleans up, resets the traps and raises the same signal again (`kill -s INT $$`, the same for TERM and HUP); `exit` stays as the fallback | probe "D1: the loop stopped with the script" (d86b628: "round 1 rc=130", then a second round) |
| D2 | RS:663: 20 kept run folders in all, so passing `--only` runs pushed out a failed run's output | `prune_kept` keeps the 20 newest failed runs' folders and, apart from them, the 20 newest passing `--only` runs' (a file named `passed`, written as the folder is made). A folder without the mark counts as failed, so folders made before this change are kept as failed ones | probe "D2" (d86b628: 0 of 20 failed folders kept) |
| D3 | RS:705: `--only` with a name pattern ran every file at Node's default concurrency | the pattern form passes `--test-concurrency=$AT_ONCE` (`AT_ONCE` moved above the `--only` branch); the single-file form runs one file and needs no cap | probe "D3" |
| D4 | `sums_list` sorted in the shell's locale: `--sums` in this shell (`LANG=en_US.UTF-8`) reordered all 88 lines of `SHA256SUMS` with the same hashes. CONFIRMED by the review-strategy session (four names under both locales) and here. Older than this batch (d86b628, line 200). CI's `sha256sum -c` passes in any order, so the cost was diff churn and conflicts between batches that regenerate the file | `LC_ALL=C sort` | probe "D4" (d86b628: 88 lines move) |

The probe: `bash dev/probes/release-check-probe.sh [script]` (default `dev/release-check.sh`). A fake `node` first on `PATH` stands in for the tests, so it takes 10 to 35 s and doesn't depend on load. For the parent: `git show d86b628:dev/release-check.sh > <scratch>/old.sh` and pass that path. The D4 check runs `--sums` in the repo and puts `SHA256SUMS` back. It says SKIP where `en_US.UTF-8` sorts the names bytewise (locale not installed).

Neighbours (CONFIRMED here or by the round 1 reviewer, run on both commits):
- The temp folder is removed after INT, and TERM leaves no suite and no temp folder. The TERM check is a guard: the parent's `exit 143` passes it too.
- A Windows caller (pwsh, node) now sees 256 times the signal's number (512 for INT, 3840 for TERM, 256 for HUP), where it saw 130, 143 and 129. Bash callers still see 128 + the number. Nothing in the repo reads these codes, and CI doesn't run this script. Written in the `stop_suites` comment.
- From round 1 (CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run): INT, HUP and TERM to the loop's group, two INTs 2 s apart with a suite that ignores TERM (the KILL path), and INT during the sandbox-check step of a `--quick` run all stop the loop on HEAD and not on the parent. An INT to the script alone doesn't arrive when a non-interactive script started it in the background: bash inherits INT as ignored, on both commits.
- D3's cost, a narrow pattern ("launcher: hooks run sandboxed", 2 tests), A B B A with A the parent: suite times 14, 16, 16 and 10 s, CPU mean 72% (43 to 99) from other sessions. Round 1's reviewer: a 6-test pattern took 8 to 9 s with the cap, 5 to 10 s without.

Checks: `--sums --quick` at ce6fcfa: all PASS, handoff-checks 158/246 with 88 skipped (the same as 5566010), `SHA256SUMS` unchanged under the UTF-8 shell. A first run at f030087 overlapped an edit of the script and didn't count (LESSONS.md, "A script edited while a run of it was going").

Review:
- Round 1 (Opus, `d86b628..ce6fcfa`): nothing CONFIRMED that makes the script worse than the parent. Four Low CONFIRMED items, fixed in 83817dc: the Windows exit codes (documented), the TERM check's label, a false D4 pass without the locale (now SKIP), and a passing run's folder unmarked for a moment after `keep_dir` (now marked as it is made). The last one is a race window, so it has no failing-first check: the round 1 repro (an unmarked newer folder) gives 19 of 20 on HEAD and 18 on the parent, since an unmarked folder counts as failed by design.
- Round 2 (Opus, `ce6fcfa..83817dc`): nothing CONFIRMED as a defect, nothing that can change a test result. It re-ran the probe on d86b628 (D1 to D4 fail), ce6fcfa and HEAD (all pass), SKIP under a missing locale and under C.UTF-8, a full run with three failing suites (one folder, three .tap files, no mark), both `--only` forms passing and failing, and `keep_dir` failing (a "could not make" line per suite, exit codes unchanged). One CONFIRMED nit, the probe's header leaving out SKIP, is fixed in the commit that records this round (comment only, not reviewed again). By the stop rule the batch is clean.

Deferred (dev tooling, by the dev rule):
- (INFERRED, round 1, seen on ce6fcfa.) Until this merges, sessions on main's script still prune the shared kept-output folder to 20 in all. Repro: run d86b628's script with `--only` against a `trailmark-release-check` folder that holds more than 20 run folders.
- (INFERRED, round 2, seen on 83817dc; probe only.) D4's SKIP test and its pass test compare against HEAD's committed order, not bytewise order. If a SHA256SUMS in en_US order were ever committed (CI checks only hashes and the line count), the probe would say SKIP with the locale installed. Repro: give the SKIP `cmp` an en_US-sorted copy of HEAD's names. Fix idea: compare `LC_ALL=C sort` with `LC_ALL=en_US.UTF-8 sort` of the names. Today HEAD's file is bytewise (CONFIRMED by round 2).
- (HYPOTHESIZED, round 2, seen on 83817dc.) A few microseconds remain between `mkdir -p "$KEEP"` and the `passed` mark, in which another run's prune would count the folder as failed. No repro. Fix idea: make the folder under a temporary name with its mark, then `mv` it into place.

- `--sums --quick` at 8f7894c: PASS, handoff-checks 158/246 with 88 skipped (this desktop's static skips: 30 NO_LINKS, 9 WIN, 1 ODD_NAME, 4 others per copy). One earlier run failed 8 commit-list and cut-note tests with the listed sign (no COMMITS section, three tries, 57 to 64 node processes from the other batches). Those passed 10 of 10 alone, and the next full `--quick` passed.

Reviews (Opus row only: no file in the security row, and no shipped doc changed):

- Round 1 (`d86b628..9c30cd0`) found four CONFIRMED and two INFERRED items, all fixed in 8f7894c.
- Kept by design: copying a handoff forward makes its escapes permanent in the file. `</div>` stays `<\/div>`, and a bidi control is dropped. It is the same escaped text session start shows the model. CONFIRMED by round 1; the user can pull a change back in.
- Round 2 (`852a560..8f7894c`) found nothing that makes 8f7894c worse than 852a560, and one CONFIRMED edge. The user chose to fix it first (4abc77a).
- Round 3 (`ef91ce2..4abc77a`) found nothing CONFIRMED. Clean under the stop rule. It probed: a tag in mid-path, upper-case or 9 hex digits, a fake tag then the real one, a tag equal to a known prefix, `exec=` earlier in the line, a line cut by `oneLine`, and odd or corrupt `sessions` values. None throws or shows path text.
- Kept by design (round 3, INFERRED from code; the effect is CONFIRMED by its test's last case): a real session's tag drops from the `status` line when the state has no record for it. Session start writes the note before its state record, and that record is skipped on a busy lock (`hook-session-start.js` `recordSessionStart`). State also drops sessions after 14 days, while `lastNote` reads older logs. The effect is display only; the log is unchanged. A path that ends in a known session's prefix shows that prefix, which reveals nothing the state doesn't hold.

Deferred to the release after, each seen on 8f7894c:

- INFERRED, low: `handoffShown` splits on `\r?\n` only, so a lone `<` before U+2028 or U+2029 isn't escaped. Session start's `framed` escapes it. A tag split across U+2028 is still caught. Repro: `handoffShown('- ends in <\u2028- next\n')`.
- INFERRED, low: transcript's `fail()` prints the transcript path unescaped (round 1, not in the diff). Also `conflict()` repeats `frameMarker()` inline, since `store.js` (batch F) doesn't export it. Style only.
- HYPOTHESIZED: a `//?/` prefix on a refused path isn't stripped. Node gives the `\\?\` form.
- RS:662's `search` neighbor: `search` can still print a SESSION_START line's exec path, behind its data line. Unchanged; left as RS:662 described it.

## Load-proof tests (2026-11-07, branch `claude/load-proof-tests` off 5566010)

PR #14 (https://github.com/cptjamestiberiouskirk-pixel/trailmark/pull/14), opened 2026-11-07 after review round 3 and a merge of main (208d5fb, 5579e63, then 0a8a666 for a conflict here). It waits for its 9 jobs and the user's word to merge.

The goal: no test fails because shipped code ran out of a wall-clock budget it is designed to run out of. Fixed in the tests only: no shipped file and no environment setting changed (so no security row). Each test that needs a budget's in-time path now repeats the call while its result shows that budget's out-of-time sign, as the sweep tests' `settle()` does. Where the sign names time for certain, the test skips with the reason after the repeats. Where it doesn't (the commit list), it fails after three tries, so a fault that always gives the sign still fails. Any other reason for an early stop fails at once.

Budgets in `plugins/trailmark/scripts` and what covers them:

| Budget | Where | Tests that need it in time | Now |
|---|---|---|---|
| copy check 300 ms | `health.js:170` (`otherCopies`); session start `trailmark.js:365`, `--copies` `:711` | 13 handoff-checks copy and rename tests and `copiesOf`'s users, 6 in `copies.test.js` (per copy) | `copiesInTime` (handoff-checks: repeat for 30 s, then skip), `walkInTime`, `startInTime`, `listInTime` (`copies.test.js`: five tries, then the result as it is) |
| commit list, at most 1.2 s | `trailmark.js:406` (`sinceHandoff`), `gitfacts.js:703` (`commitsSince`) | 7 handoff-checks tests | `sinceInTime`: three tries, then the result as it is |
| git facts and commit facts, 5 s less what the launcher used | `trailmark.js:341`, `:350` | basics "launcher: hooks run sandboxed ...", commits "0.3.3 hardening: the PreCompact status ...", sweep "0.3.4 commits: another session's commit ...", sessions "hooks: a busy lock costs the reminder ..." (since review round 2) | `lateAgain` (three tries, then skip) and `inTimeTest` (skip; that step can't be repeated); the busy-lock test repeats its commit hook in a loop of its own (three tries, then skip) |
| the launcher's hook limit, 4.5 s for commit-start, 9 s for the others | `trailmark.js:219` | sessions "hooks: a busy lock costs the reminder ...", security "0.5.1 commit hook: a background commit command ..." | `hookInTime` (three tries, then skip), `hookTest`; each try a new tool-call ID |
| a busy lock's wait, 2 s | `store.js:679` (`updateState`) | the busy-lock test's old 6 s bound on the whole hook | timed in the test's own process: `updateState` against the held lock must give up within 4 s |
| sweep 3 s | `trailmark.js:471`, `:537`, `:655`; `gitfacts.js:641` | the sweep tests | unchanged: they already skip (`sweepTest`) |
| commit-start's command parse, 300 ms (1.5 s by default) | `hook-commit-start.js:42`, `commands.js:2149` | none seen failing; a test command parses in well under 1 ms | unchanged |
| a git call's own 3 s; the module walk's `left() < 800` | `paths.js:67`, `:79`; `gitbin.js:355`, `:760`, `:918` | none seen failing | unchanged |
| Windows rename retry 2 s | `store.js:202` | the junction tests (EPERM/EBUSY, B8) | unchanged |

| a fact dropped with no marker (status, or a commit) | not run | the tests fail in both copies |
| hook limit 50 ms on the first call (milestone, commit-start) | fail | 2/2 pass each |
| hook limit 50 ms on every call | fail | the two tests skip with the reason; whole files fail: `sessions.test.js` 8 of 21 and 7 of 20, `security.test.js` 5 of 13 and 6 of 16 |
| a hook that exits 1 without the stop message | fails | fails |
| busy lock, one busy loop per core (16), 3 runs each, alternating | 2 of 3 runs failed (`hook took 7997` to `8711 ms`) | 6/6 pass |
| `updateState`'s wait at 9 s | fails | fails (`updateState waited 9027 ms`) |

So a fault that makes a budget always run out still fails tests for every budget covered: the copy check's direct walks and, since review round 1, `--copies` (F3), the commit-list tests, the rest of `commits.test.js` and `basics.test.js`, and the hook tests in `sessions.test.js` and `security.test.js`. The busy-lock and hook-limit rows are CONFIRMED by the subagent that made those fixes (report in the review-strategy session's notes folder, `load-proof/lockhook-report.md`), the git-facts rows by the one that made those (`load-proof/gitfacts-report.md`), not re-run here; the whole-file rows for git facts and the hook limit, and every copy-check and commit-list row, are CONFIRMED here. No timing is compared between old and new: the runs weren't alternated.

What is still load-sensitive, and why:

- The commit list fails after three tries in a row (every call late: 14 of 14 fail, by design). The text doesn't say git ran out, so a skip there could hide a fault. The product fix stays deferred ("Session start's commit list under load").
- 0.3.4's last step: the side pass for another session's folders (`gitfacts.js:547`, about 1 s) can run out without writing any marker, so the test still fails there with no sign to key on (HYPOTHESIZED cause of the 13:02 failure, as before).
- The busy-lock test no longer catches a hook that waits on the lock twice (INFERRED from the code: two 2 s waits stay inside the 9 s limit, so nothing stops the hook). A hook with a longer wait of its own did pass it on 91415bb, by skipping (CONFIRMED, review round 1 F1, below); since round 1 that fails when the run with the lock free leaves room for the wait. Comparing the hook against itself without the lock can't fill the rest under load: the difference swung from -0.7 to +6 s (CONFIRMED by the subagent's measurement).
- In the commit-start test, the milestone hook after it and the first session start can hit their 9 s limit too; they aren't repeated, since repeating them isn't proven safe (INFERRED).
- A session start cut short while `--show-names` finished, with no time reason from `--show-names` in ten tries, fails the copy tests (review round 2, C). In a simulation with session start late on 99% of calls and `--show-names` on 50%, that is 0.09% of calls; at equal rates (90% or 99%) none in 20,000. The real rates are HYPOTHESIZED. It stays so a fault of session start's own still fails.
- The busy-lock test's commit hook, stopped three times with the lock busy, fails if the same hook with the lock removed then takes under 6 s. With the commit hook as its own control, a steady load can't fail it (INFERRED from round 2's timings: 11 to 12.5 s free at 24 busy loops). A changing load can: it needs three stops of 9 s or more, then a free run under 6 s, so the free run more than 3 s faster beyond the lock's wait, and the hook against itself swung by up to +6 s (line above). So a false failure is HYPOTHESIZED possible, never seen; the three-stop chain was never reached in a loaded run.
- "copies: the command the note gives ..." keeps its round 3 to 5 form (it accepts a check that ran out, with diagnostics) rather than repeating, so it still checks less under load.
- `list()` in `health.js` (lines 133 and 150) reports any error opening or reading a folder, other than ENOENT, as "<folder> has too many folders". CONFIRMED by a probe (`probe-list.js`, scratch): EBUSY and EPERM, at open and mid-read, both give "the plugin cache has too many folders". Whether Windows gives such a transient error there is HYPOTHESIZED, never seen. The tests don't repeat on it: it isn't the time reason, so a test fails with that wording, which now points at this item. Decided: a read error should be a reason of its own ("could not be read"), but that changes shipped wording (what `--copies --show-names` prints; no shipped doc names the reason, CONFIRMED by grep), so it goes to the next version with the docs and Opus rows; see "Deferred to the next version".

### Review round 1 (`git diff 5566010..91415bb`, 2026-11-07)

Opus, regression and design (the diff touches tests and `dev/` only, so no security or docs row). Report in the review-strategy session's notes folder, `load-proof/review1.md`. Four findings, each reproduced here before the fix; fixed in one push:

- F1 (CONFIRMED, reproduced: with `updateState`'s wait at 20 s in `hook-milestone.js`, the busy-lock test skipped 2 of 2 in 33 s on 91415bb). A lock that hangs the hook read as a slow machine. Fix: after three stops with the lock busy, `hookInTime` runs the subagent hook once with the lock free; if that run takes under 6 s (9 s limit, less the 2 s wait, less 1 s), the test fails, else it skips as before. The margin keeps a slow machine from failing it: a free run near the limit says nothing. (Wrong for the commit hook on d6a01ac: its free run was the subagent hook, which took 30 to 40% of the commit hook's free time in round 2's timings; round 2, A.) Proof: the new test fails in both copies (2 of 2, "took 409 ms with it free" and 398 ms) with the 20 s wait; 2/2 pass as shipped.
- F2 (CONFIRMED, reproduced: `probe-helpers.js` drove the old `copiesInTime` 1,708,788 times in 0.3 s, then it threw the skip). A session start cut short for a reason of its own ("the check failed": its call threw) while `--show-names` finished was repeated until the 30 s ran out, then skipped. Session start's note gives no reason, so the helper now tries that case five times and returns the result, and the test fails on it. (Five tries also failed on time alone, since both walks have the same budget; round 2, C.) The comment that said every reason stops every check the same way is corrected. Proof: the same probe gives the result after 5 tries; its other five cases give what they gave before.
- F3 (CONFIRMED by the reviewer, not re-run on the old tests: with the `--copies` call's budget at -1, `trailmark.js:711`, 2 of 78 failed, both by accident). `--copies` has its own call to `otherCopies`, which only one test per copy needed to finish. Fix: "session start: warns of an old cached copy ..." (`copies.test.js`, both copies) also checks that `listInTime` lists the planted copy. Proof: the new test fails in both copies at its new check (`copies.test.js:118`, "the check stopped early: it took too long") with that budget at -1; 2/2 pass as shipped.
- F4 (CONFIRMED in the code). In "0.3.3 hardening: the PreCompact status runs no fsmonitor, hook or submodule ..." (`commits.test.js`, both copies), the git-timeout skip came before the checks that git started nothing (no marks, no `run_command`), so a late run checked neither. They now run first; only the check that status ran waits for an in-time run. Not proven by a forced fault: making git both start a program and run out of time in one run wasn't built (the order is plain in the diff).

Checks after the fix: 62/66 pass, the 4 skips the Windows-only folder names (`--only` over every changed test, both copies); all PASS, handoff-checks 158/246 with 88 skipped, the same as 5566010 (`--sums --quick`).

### Review round 2 (`git diff 91415bb..d6a01ac`, 2026-11-07)

Opus, regression and design. Three findings; each checked here before the fix, fixed in one push:

- A (INFERRED from timings CONFIRMED by the reviewer, not re-run here). F1's lock-free control was the subagent hook even when the commit hook was the one stopped. The reviewer's times, lock busy / commit hook free / subagent hook free: idle 2.9 / 0.87 / 0.35 s; 16 busy loops 5.8 to 6.2 / 3.5 to 4.0 / 1.5 s; 24 busy loops 14.5 to 15.9 / 11 to 12.5 / 3.1 to 3.9 s. So three commit-hook stops under 24 to 30 loops would fail on load alone, which the reviewer didn't reach in 3 runs. Fix: `hookInTime` takes the lock's path, removes it, and runs the stopped hook itself. Proof: the new test fails in both copies, the free run taking 663 and 671 ms with the 20 s wait (the tests from d6a01ac: fail too, with the subagent hook's 367 and 349 ms: on an idle machine the two controls agree, and the difference is only under load).
- B (CONFIRMED by the reviewer, not re-run under load here: at 30 busy loops 1 of 2 runs of unedited d6a01ac failed in both copies; older than this branch). In the busy-lock test the launcher's commit lookup can run out of its own budget (5 s less what the launcher used, `trailmark.js:341`). The notes then get `COMMIT_UNRESOLVED why=git-timeout` in place of the COMMIT line the test expects. It wasn't on "Load-sensitive tests". Fix: the commit hook is repeated while that marker is new and the COMMIT line is missing, three times, then the test skips naming the marker. Proof, with `commitFacts`'s budget at 5 ms in a scratch tree: on the first call per repo, the tests from d6a01ac fail in both copies at the COMMIT line, with `COMMIT_UNRESOLVED why=git-timeout` in the kept notes, the new ones pass 2/2 after a repeat; on every call, the new ones skip 2 of 2, naming the marker; with no forced fault, 2/2 pass. A commit lookup that always runs out still fails `commits.test.js` (12 of 26 per copy, "Proof" above).
- C (CONFIRMED by a simulation, `probe-c.js`; the real rates HYPOTHESIZED). Both copy walks have the same 300 ms budget, so a session start cut short while `--show-names` finished can be time alone, and F2's five tries then failed the test: 2.4% of calls with both late 90% of the time, 90% with session start late 99% and `--show-names` 50%. Fix: once any `--show-names` in the window gives the time reason, the case counts as time (repeat, then skip); ten such tries with none are returned and fail. Simulated, 20,000 calls each: 0%, 0% and 0.09% fail at those rates; the F2 fault (session start always cut short for its own reason) still fails, after 10 tries.
- Also: the PreCompact hardening test reads its trace only if git wrote one (HYPOTHESIZED by the reviewer: a run where no git call started would throw ENOENT before the skip). F3 and F4 had nothing CONFIRMED.

Checks after the fix: 62/66 pass, the 4 skips the Windows-only folder names (`--only` over every changed test, both copies); all PASS, handoff-checks 158/246 with 88 skipped, the same as 5566010 (`--sums --quick`).

### Review round 3 (`git diff d6a01ac..5e90286`, 2026-11-07)

Opus, regression and design. Nothing CONFIRMED wrong in the code; A, B and C each behave as designed (CONFIRMED by round 3's repros, not re-run here):

- A: with the commit hook made 7.5 s slower on its own, 5e90286 skips 2 of 2 and d6a01ac fails 2 of 2 (its control was the cheaper subagent hook). With the lock's wait at 20 s, both fail 2 of 2.
- B: a commit dropped with no marker fails 2 of 2; the commit lookup at 5 ms on every call skips 2 of 2 naming the marker (d6a01ac fails 2 of 2). The payload has no `tool_use_id`, so the hook's same-command rule doesn't apply, and `knownCommits` keeps a repeat from writing a second COMMIT line.
- C: session start's check throwing fails 2 of 2; its budget at -1 on an idle machine fails 2 of 2. With `--show-names` also cut short 30% of the time, 5e90286 skips 2 of 2: the designed cost, since one time reason from `--show-names` lets a fault of session start's own skip under load.

Text: the inventory row for git facts named helpers the busy-lock test doesn't use (CONFIRMED, fixed); the commit-hook control's line now says how a changing load could still fail it (HYPOTHESIZED); a slow commit hook that no test catches is deferred ("Deferred to the next version"). By the stop rule, this round ends the branch's review: nothing CONFIRMED in code changed since round 2.

Taken off "Load-sensitive tests" by this branch, as they stood on 5566010:

- The busy-lock test's 6 s bound. Test: "hooks: a busy lock costs the reminder, never the commit log line; a payload with a BOM still parses" (test 44 of the Claude Code `trailmark.test.js`; also in the Codex copy). Sign: `hook took <ms> ms` over 6000. CONFIRMED 2026-11-05 on the 0.6.0 round-4 tree: 3 of 3 failed with one busy loop per core. With that load the hook's median was 4.3 to 6.8 s in fdd46b2, 6df998d, 0612f86 and the round-4 tree (5 runs each; the order of the runs, not the version, decided which was slowest), and about 2.7 s idle. The bound dates from 0.4.0. Repro: one busy loop per core, then `dev/release-check.sh --only 'a busy lock costs the reminder'`. Fix idea: time `updateState` against a held lock in the test's own process, and keep the hook check to its exit status and the log line.
- The commit hook's time limit (added 2026-11-05, with the review-strategy session). Test: "0.5.1 commit hook: a background commit command that has committed in one other folder but not yet in a second adds both to the session's commit folders" (#150 of the Claude Code `trailmark.test.js` in round 8's check of 586329e, #159 in the Codex copy). It is also one of the sweep tests above, which skip; this is its other sign. Sign: the test fails at the status check after `--hook commit-start` (exit 1), and its kept folder holds only the SESSION_START note, with no `.jbmem.lock` and no `pending/`. The hook's stderr ("took too long and was stopped") isn't in the TAP. Proof (review round 8, CONFIRMED by round 8, not re-run here): with the launcher's limit for `commit-start` cut to 50 ms (normally about 4.5 s), the test fails that way, at that step, with that state, on 586329e and on df88236; S1 is RULED OUT, since `commit-start` takes no lock. Seen in round 8's full check of 586329e (2026-11-05 12:54, CPU 100%); load as the trigger is INFERRED.
- The copy check's budget at session start (added 2026-11-07). Test: "copies: the command the note gives prints no folder name; --show-names, given only in the warning, lists them (0.6.0)" (`handoff-checks.test.js`, both copies). Sign: it fails at `handoff-checks.test.js:2050` (`--show-names lists it`), and the session-start message holds "WARNING: the check for other trailmark copies in Claude Code's plugin cache stopped early, so there may be copies it didn't see" and no "another trailmark copy is". Seen once, in CI: PR #10's run 42399384038 on 5a1e1a4, windows-latest Node 26, Codex copy; the other 8 jobs passed. Of the 6 failed runs among the last 60, this is the one; none of the other 5 has it (CONFIRMED by round 4, which read their logs). Proof (2026-11-07, a scratch worktree of 5a1e1a4): with the session-start hook's `otherCopies` call given `budgetMs: 0` (`trailmark.js:365` in the Claude Code copy, `:686` in Codex), the test fails in both copies at line 2050 with that message, the same as CI; as shipped it passes (2 of 2). So the cause is CONFIRMED as the hook's 300 ms budget running out (`health.js:170`), a designed outcome the test didn't accept. main (5a8dd2a) fails the same way with that budget at 0 (CONFIRMED). Since the round 3 batch the test takes that outcome (see "Review round 3"), so this sign no longer fails it: the TAP shows the diagnostic "session start: the check stopped early" instead. Five other handoff-checks tests and two `copies.test.js` tests per copy still need session start's walk to finish ("Deferred to the next version"). The plain `--copies` call a moment later in the same test found the copy. What made the first walk that slow on the runner is HYPOTHESIZED: runner load, or a scanner reading the files the test had just made. PR #10 doesn't change the code or the CI step this runs in (CONFIRMED by its diff). Neighbors and the deferred fix in "Deferred to the next version".

## PLAYBOOK.md and LESSONS.md (2026-11-07, PR #12, merged as 208d5fb)

The user asked for both files at the repo root, for development only, filled in by sessions as they work, and for "all existing and future sessions" to use them.

- `PLAYBOOK.md`: checklists for planning, code and docs, testing, reviewing, releasing and milestones, plus the known limits and a "Do not attempt" list. `LESSONS.md`: a dated log (what went wrong, why, the rule going forward, status), seeded from this file, CLAUDE.md, the project notes and PR #10's lessons. Those came from the review-strategy session and the speed-up session's `faster-checks/lessons-pending.md`, merged where they overlapped, not re-run here.
- `export-ignore` in `.gitattributes` keeps both out of the zip and out of `SHA256SUMS`. CONTRIBUTING.md names them with the other files the zip leaves out. CLAUDE.md's second paragraph tells every session to re-read both before significant steps and to log lessons.
- Sessions: the project memory note `playbook-and-lessons.md` carries the rule into every session, on any branch. The review-strategy, speed-up and time-limit sessions were told, and the review-strategy session confirmed it adopted them. After the merge, the speed-up session could no longer be reached; its lessons were already in.
- Checks: `dev/release-check.sh --sums --quick` passed on ccf4d06, after main with PR #10 was merged in (SHA256SUMS regenerated for the conflict). `--zip` from ccf4d06: inner sums verify, and there is no `PLAYBOOK.md`, `LESSONS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` or `dev/` in it (CONFIRMED with `unzip -l`). A Sonnet docs review found two CONFIRMED wording issues, both fixed in b89b644: the release checklist heading repeats per version, and Codex refuses only a checkpoint's first write. CI: all 9 jobs passed. Merged with auto-merge on the user's "Yes, auto-merge". The Codex bot's code review of ccf4d06 completed with no findings. Its security review completed with the findings field empty (`null`), and the PR has no inline comments.
- Open lessons with owners: the review-process change in CLAUDE.md and `tests.yml` (review-strategy session, now unblocked by PR #10) and the time-budget audit (load-proof session; done, see "Load-proof tests", and its LESSONS entry is updated).

## Faster local checks (2026-11-06, branch `claude/faster-checks` off cf06986)

The two speedups the user agreed to on 2026-11-06 for one small PR after 0.6.0: the suites at the same time in `release-check.sh`, and `trailmark.test.js` split into files. Pushed 2026-11-07 on the user's "Push and open PR", with main's 0.6.0 publish record (5a8dd2a) merged in.

- 01ef1a4: `release-check.sh` starts the three suites in the background, each with its own temp folder (`$T/s1`, `s2`, `s3`: short, since a test's paths repeat it), so a failed test's kept folders still go to its own suite's kept output. One summary line per suite as before, now with the suite's time. Ctrl-C stops the background suites.
- f05b383: each `plugins/*/tests/trailmark.test.js` is now nine files, one or more of its old sections each (`basics`, `sessions`, `hardening`, `commits`, `sweep`, `copies`, `security`, `commit-folders`, and `context-watch` for Claude Code or `codex` for Codex), plus `helpers.js` with what more than one file uses. The code was moved by a script that only cuts at top-level statements and checks the pieces rebuild the old file; a line-by-line comparison finds two changed lines per copy (the sweep test reads `sweepRepeats()`, since `helpers.js` keeps the count) and the new file heads. A file that counts tool-use IDs keeps its own `toolSeq`; the ID prefixes differ, so IDs stay apart. CI and `release-check.sh` take every `*.test.js` of a copy except handoff-checks, so a new file can't be left out. No split file is identical in both copies, so the shared-file list in CI is unchanged.
- 4c79b13: two files at a time in each trailmark suite (`--test-concurrency=2`, `AT_ONCE` in `release-check.sh`), chosen from the runs below.

Measured on this machine (16 logical cores), full `dev/release-check.sh` with `TMPDIR=/e/DevTemp` (the user variable is `E:\DevTemp`; this shell still had the old one, so each run set it), CPU sampled every 5 s. Times per suite are from the summary lines (the base run's from its TAP). The bench runs were in a separate worktree at each commit; to see which tests skipped, a bench-only line copied each suite's TAP out (not committed), and the 3-at-a-time and the first 2-at-a-time runs set the concurrency on f05b383 by hand. 93a9c0d is 4c79b13 before a comment fix.

| Run | Wall | handoff / Claude Code / Codex | CPU mean, p90 | Node processes (max) | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| base cf06986 | 1,136 s | 151 / 507 / 472 s | 14%, 24% | 21 | pass; skipped 88 / 15 / 16 |
| suites at once, 01ef1a4 | 566 s | 171 / 560 / 528 s | 23%, 35% | 28 | pass; same skips |
| split, all files at once, f05b383 (1) | 266 s | 260 / 228 / 209 s | 53%, 75% | 69 | pass; 88 / 16 / 17: "past six" skipped in both copies |
| split, all files at once, f05b383 (2) | 271 s | 264 / 231 / 209 s | 54%, 72% | 67 | FAIL: one handoff-checks test (commit list); "past six" skipped in both |
| 3 files at a time (1) | 333 s | 225 / 327 / 301 s | 40%, 53% | 35 | pass; "past six" skipped in both |
| 3 files at a time (2) | 329 s | 224 / 322 / 303 s | 40%, 53% | 35 | pass; "past six" skipped in both |
| 2 files at a time (1) | 392 s | 209 / 385 / 360 s | 34%, 44% | 30 | pass; same skips as base |
| 2 files at a time, 93a9c0d (2) | 416 s | 208 / 409 / 379 s | 32%, 42% | 30 | pass; same skips as base |
| 2 files at a time, 93a9c0d (3) | 424 s | 211 / 419 / 390 s | 31%, 40% | 30 | pass; "past six" skipped in the Claude Code copy |

So the check went from 18.9 min to 6.5 to 7 min (2.7 to 2.9 times faster). handoff-checks is the longest suite now under the others' load (208 to 264 s against 151 s alone).

Load-sensitive tests (the list below), reread for this change:

- The commit list at session start: the one failure, handoff-checks "commits since the handoff: listed at session start, with next steps they may cover" (Codex copy, 15 s), in the second all-at-once run. Its output has a COMMITS section saying "HEAD has 2 newer commits, the newest 0 shown:" over an empty list, the listed sign (this test makes two commits, so 2, not 1). Put down to load in that setting. Its TAP, kept folder and CPU log are in the review-strategy session's notes folder, `faster-checks/split-uncapped-run2-20261106-203615`. At two files at a time it did not fail: 0 of 3 full runs, and 2 of 2 in the `--only` reruns below.
- The sweep test "0.6.0 commit folders: past six, a folder a sweep has read since it joined goes first ...": every skip had the listed sign, "every sweep for 90 s ran out of time (N in a row)", N from 17 to 22. It skipped in 4 of 4 copy-runs all at once, 4 of 4 three at a time, and 1 of 6 two at a time; 0 of 4 at base and with the suites at once. Proof that the split itself didn't break it (f05b383, both copies): alone on an idle machine it passed (2 of 2), and the whole `commit-folders.test.js` passed alone (15/15 and 8/8, no skips). Reproduced as load (CONFIRMED): with handoff-checks and both trailmark suites (3 at a time, without `commit-folders.test.js`) started in the background, the test run alone skipped in both copies while CPU was 47% with 33 node processes, then passed in both as the load ended (38%, then 13%). It did not skip under seven busy loops at 47% CPU (2 of 2 passed in both copies) or beside handoff-checks alone at 23% (2 of 2). So CPU time alone doesn't starve the sweep, other test processes do; what in them (process starts, the file system) is HYPOTHESIZED.
- The busy-lock 6 s bound, the commit hook's time limit (#150) and the git-facts budget tests: passed in all eight full runs after the base one.
- `--only` over every listed test (the pattern in the PR; 46 tests in both copies, every file at once), twice at 4c79b13: 46/46 passed, none skipped (99 and 101 s; CPU mean 34 to 35%, max 69%).

CI (PR #10, run 42394748510 on 86fd88a): all 9 jobs passed. Node runs one file per core less one by default, so CI's trailmark steps ran 3 files at once on Linux and Windows and 2 on macOS, with no cap. The pass and fail counts per job are the same as main's last run (42322139667 on 5a8dd2a): handoff-checks / Claude Code / Codex pass 240 / 175 / 168 on Linux, 232 / 175 / 168 on macOS and 218 / 166 / 158 on Windows, none failed (CONFIRMED with `gh api` job logs). Job times went from 380 to 939 s on main to 196 to 649 s (Linux about 390 to 210 s, Windows 798 to 939 s down to 556 to 649 s). So CI needs no cap.

### Review round 1 (`git diff 5a8dd2a..86fd88a`, 2026-11-07)

Opus for regression, Sonnet for docs, and the Codex review bot on GitHub. No security row: the diff touches none of its files.

- (Codex P2 and Opus, CONFIRMED here.) The Ctrl-C trap's `kill 0` reached whatever started `release-check.sh`: a script started without job control shares its caller's process group. Repro: a wrapper that starts `release-check.sh --only 'past six, a folder a sweep has read'` in the background and sends TERM to it alone; the wrapper died (exit 143). Fixed: each suite starts under `set -m`, so it has a process group of its own, and the trap sends TERM to those groups only, waits for them, then exits 130. With the fix the wrapper survived in every run, the suite's whole process tree (node, its test-file children, git, conhost; 5 to 8 processes, listed by parent id) was gone right after, and Ctrl-C (INT through `timeout -s INT`) did the same. CI never runs this script.
- (Found while checking that fix, CONFIRMED here.) After a stop, the EXIT trap's single `rm -rf` left the stopped suite's folder (`s1/tmp/jbmem-*`) behind in 4 of 5 runs of the fixed trap on Windows, though no process of the suite was left. Windows frees a stopped process's files a moment late (INFERRED). Fixed: the cleanup retries for up to 5 s and says so if it can't. 3 of 3 TERM runs and 1 INT run then left nothing.
- (Sonnet, CONFIRMED.) CONTRIBUTING.md still named `tests/trailmark.test.js`, and so did the head comment of both `handoff-checks.test.js` copies. The Claude Code `commit-folders.test.js` holds seven context-watch tests that its description didn't mention. One CONTRIBUTING command was unquoted (`tests/*.test.js`). All fixed; no doc outside `dev/` names `trailmark.test.js` now.
- (Opus, INFERRED; deferred, see "Deferred to the next version".) `--only` runs every file at Node's default concurrency, not `AT_ONCE`.
- Checked and fine (Opus, CONFIRMED by its scripts, not re-run): every old test is in exactly one new file with an identical body (175/175, 168/168), apart from the two `sweepRepeats()` lines. No file uses a name it neither declares nor imports, and the `toolSeq` prefixes don't collide. The CI line works in bash on all three runners.

CI on 5a1e1a4 (run 42399384038): 8 of 9 jobs passed; windows-latest Node 26 failed one handoff-checks test, the copy check's budget at session start (under "Load-sensitive tests", not from this PR).

- F1 (CONFIRMED). The trap took INT and TERM but not HUP, and since round 1 the suites have process groups of their own, so a hangup to the script's group (a closed terminal, a dropped SSH) left them running. Repro: start the script as its own job and send HUP to its group. On ff2e3e0: exit 129, the test kept running, on Windows and on Linux. On 68f5a6b: exit 129, the test stopped, on both. Each signal now exits 128 + its number: 130 for INT, 143 for TERM (130 before), 129 for HUP, the shell's own convention, so a caller can tell which one stopped the run.
- F2 (CONFIRMED; trigger HYPOTHESIZED). The stop's `wait` had no bound, and each further signal ran the stop again. Repro: a fake `node` that ignores TERM, INT and HUP, then TERM, TERM and HUP one second apart. On ff2e3e0 the script exited 130 after 2 s and left that suite running, on both systems. That is a different form from Opus's repro, which hung 21 s: either way the stop didn't end the suite. On 68f5a6b it exited 143 after 12 s on Windows and 10 s on Linux, with nothing left: the stop sends TERM, polls the groups for up to 10 s, kills what is left, and ignores further signals meanwhile. A suite that does stop on TERM ends the poll at once (one round; 325 ms on Windows, 205 ms on Linux, `poll-time.sh`), so the bound costs nothing in a normal stop. Native node on Windows ends at TERM, so only an msys child that ignores it can trip the bound there.
- F3 (INFERRED, not tested: no bash before 4.4 here; WSL has 5.2). Under `set -u`, `"${a[@]}"` of an empty array fails as unbound on bash before 4.4, macOS's 3.2 included. `SUITE_PIDS` is gone (see F4), and the expansions that can be empty (`SUITES`, `RESULTS`, `TCC`, `TCX`) use `${a[@]+"${a[@]}"}`. `HC` is a fixed list of two and `old` in `keep_dir` is read by index, so neither changed. On bash 5.3 the form gives nothing for an empty array and keeps an element with a space whole (checked).
- F4 (CONFIRMED on Windows, not reproduced on Linux). A signal between a suite's start and the next line, which recorded `$!`, missed that suite. Repro (`f4-stress.sh`, 30 runs each): TERM at a random moment while jobs start. Recording `$!` (as ff2e3e0) left a job running in 30 of 30 runs on Windows and 0 of 30 on Linux. Reading `jobs -rp` (as 68f5a6b) left none in 30 of 30 on both. Chosen: read the groups from the job table, because bash enters a job there as part of starting it, before any trap can run, and `-r` leaves out finished suites, so a stop after `finish_suites` signals no group whose number may have been reused. The table is read through a file. Round 3 found `$(jobs -rp)` gives the same numbers on bash 5.3 and 5.2; the file form stays, since it needs no subshell.
- Neighbors, the same on both systems: TERM from a wrapper that shares the script's group (the wrapper lived, the test stopped), Ctrl-C (INT through `timeout`: exit 130, stopped), a stop before any suite (TERM 1 s into `--quick`: exit 143 at once, no suite started), and a normal `--only` run (the same summary on both commits). No case left a temp folder. Not tested: macOS and bash 3.2.

### Review round 3 (`git diff ff2e3e0..5a1e1a4`, 2026-11-07)

Opus for regression, run by the review-strategy session: all four round 2 fixes hold, nothing CONFIRMED regresses. Its checks (CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run): HUP stops the test in 382 ms, the TERM-ignoring child is stopped after 12.2 s, `jobs -rp` leaked 0 of 12 against 12 of 12 for `$!`, a normal stop takes about 370 ms, nothing reads the exit codes, and `--quick` took 223 s against 239 s. The batch also holds the fix for CI's failure on 5a1e1a4.

The KILL went to every suite's group even when the poll had found them all gone, the normal case, and Windows reuses process numbers quickly, so a new group with a dead suite's number could be killed. Now only a group the poll still finds is killed, and only after the poll ran out. Probe (`bash -x`, counting KILLs; scratch scripts `batch-verify.sh` and `kill-probe.sh`, Windows): a normal stop sent 1 KILL on c02207a and 0 with the fix, and the test stopped both times. A suite that ignores TERM got 1 KILL on both, exit 143 after 13 s, nothing left.

### A: the context watch (2026-11-04/05)

- New: `hook-context-watch.js` (sandboxed), `lib/contextwatch.js` (launcher side), the `context-watch` entry in `HOOKS` and `hooks.json` (PostToolUse with no matcher, UserPromptSubmit, 10 s), `contextWatch` in `.jbmem.json` (`on`, `steps` 80..99, `reserve` 20000..50000; defaults 85/90/95 and 33000).
- Docs facts (Claude Code docs, read 2026-11-04 by a subagent, quoted with URLs; model-config, env-vars, settings-reference, hooks): `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` wins over the settings (documented, so the HYPOTHESIZED order is now CONFIRMED by the docs); a model's `modelSettings` entry wins over the top-level value in the same file; "auto" is the model's own window; default is the model's context limit (1M for Fable, Sonnet 5+, Haiku 5.5, Opus 4.7+; 200k for others and unknown IDs); the window is capped at the model's context; `CLAUDE_AUTOCOMPACT_PCT_OVERRIDE` is a percentage 1..100 of the window and can only lower the point. The 33k reserve is not documented (still INFERRED from `preTokens`).
- Choices: no "fallback window" setting, since the docs give the default per model (the prototype's 200k guess is replaced by it); the window is capped at the model's own; the percentage override is honored. A request ignores `hints.minMinutesBetween` (last chance before the compaction) and sets `lastHintAt`, so a milestone hint does not follow straight away.
- Speed: the launcher runs `contextwatch.fastPath()` before loading anything else and exits below the earliest step any configuration allows (80% of the window less 50k). Measured (`cwtime.js`, scratch, 15 runs each, 1.2 MB transcript tail): bare `node -e 0` median 70 ms; the hook below the step median 89 ms (min 82, max 122). Before moving the fast path ahead of the module loads it was 292 ms against 172 ms bare (a busier moment), about 50 ms of it module loading. A test checks that no child process starts below the step.
- Start over: the launcher reads the transcript back to the time of the last request and passes the smallest size and any `compact_boundary` since; the hook starts over on a compaction, a shrink by a fifth, a fall below 75% of the compaction point, or when it could not read back that far. Nothing is written below the first step.
- Codex: not ported. Codex rollouts do record usage (`token_count` events with `last_token_usage` and `model_context_window`, CONFIRMED in a local rollout file) but its compaction point (`model_auto_compact_token_limit`, unset here) and default are not established, and its hooks have no UserPromptSubmit. Claude Code only in 0.6.0; README and CHANGELOG to say so. trailmark-watchdog does none of this (it watches plugin files).
- Tests (`trailmark.test.js`, 9): the prototype's 14 cases plus the steps-before-the-point check at 400k and 200k, the worktree/sandbox case, refused transcript paths, the variable, the percentage override, the cap, prompts, a shrink and a dip. All 9 fail on d6b705e and pass now.

### B4, B6, B7, B9, B11: the git checks (2026-11-05)

`gitbin.js`, both copies. Tests in `handoff-checks.test.js` (both copies, five new): each fix's test fails on d6b705e and passes now, on Windows (B4, B7, B9; B6 passes there, its bug is POSIX only) and in WSL (node 18.19.1, git 2.43.0: all four). The B11 test is POSIX only and passes on d6b705e too, as a test for the 0.5.1 fix.

- B6: `farPathValue` takes a relative value read from a base folder that isn't there (`missingBase`) as text only, since the kernel can't go through the missing folder. `localPath` still refuses any `..` after a part that isn't there. A worktree that is a link to a share is still refused (in the test).
- B7: `farModules` sets `CHECK_CWD` to the repo's top for its run (and restores it): `git status` runs in the top (`gitfacts.js:101`), so that is the folder a relative setting comes from. Before, a direct call took the folder of whatever `farGitConfig` ran last. The test makes the other folder's answer differ with a junction loop (no network, no extra rights); the first draft checked `//evil.invalid/share/x`, which `farGitConfig` allows and looks up, so it was replaced.
- B9: a listing that fails with ENOENT or ENOTDIR while the folder is still a folder gives `odd-path` (`unlisted`), in `linkedEntries` and `moduleGitdirs`. EACCES is passed over as before (git can't read it either). The test fakes Node's no-d_type error on one folder.
- B4, narrowed: `moduleGitdirs` now walks every folder of a submodule gitdir's `objects/` except the two-hex ones (`objectsIn`), so a gitdir named like `vendor/objects/evil` (or deeper) has its config checked. `linkedEntries` already walked those. Two-hex names (`vendor/objects/ab`) are still passed over unlisted. Measured (`b4cost.js`, scratch, 200 submodule gitdirs of 256 loose-object folders each, Windows): d6b705e `farGitConfig` 261 ms, `farModules` 134 ms; listing each two-hex folder 10.1 s and 8.7 s; looking for a HEAD in each 4.1 s and 3.5 s; this version 221 ms and 149 ms. `farModules` already used 2.2 to 3.4 s of gitFacts' 5 s on the 200-submodule test (0.5.1 notes). Why the rest can wait: git 2.56 refuses to open such a gitdir by name (0.5.1 Fable review), and a probe (`b4git.sh`, scratch) found that neither git 2.43 nor 2.56 read a submodule gitdir's config by name during `git status --porcelain` when the worktree has no `.git`, not even the control (`modules/vendor`). So whether the hook's git commands ever open `modules/<name>` by name is not established; from memory of git's source (not checked), `git status` reaches a submodule's refs only after the worktree's `.git` is found valid. Left for later: a two-hex nested gitdir, if a cheap way to find one turns up.
- B11: "a link (not a folder) inside a leftover's objects/ab is followed and a share refused", skipped on Windows (a symlink to `//evil.invalid/share`).

### B1: the link tests on Windows (2026-11-05)

- Of the 42 `skip: WIN` tests in `handoff-checks.test.js` (both copies), 30 now skip only where links can't be made (`NO_LINKS`, the probe `trailmark.test.js` uses): the 26 that need nothing but links, three with their POSIX-only line split out (478: `..` after a link is checked both ways, text on Windows; 659: the 450-character folder; 1198: a line break in a file name), and the 0.6.0 B11 test. The other POSIX ones keep `skip: WIN` with their real reason (a `'/' + path` link target, control characters in names, quoted alternates, a shell-script git, `..` after a link); five said "making links needs Developer Mode" before.
- Given no type, Node on Windows stats a link's target to choose file or folder: for `//evil.invalid/...` that would look the share up (and could send credentials) before the code under test runs. On Windows the file wraps `fs.symlinkSync` so a link to a network target is made as a file link without looking.
- Local runs: this account can't make symlinks, so the 30 still skip here; 51 pass, 0 fail, 42 skipped (49 passed before; the two hard-link tests add 2). CI's Windows runners can make links: they run the 30 for the first time, so a failure there is new information, not a regression.

Four other tests fail in this WSL on HEAD too, so not from 0.6.0: git-lfs isn't installed (CONFIRMED for the LFS test), and the grants, installers and tab-in-a-value tests (INFERRED: node 18's older permission flag, no `claude` CLI, git 2.43). CI's Linux job is the reference.

### B5 and B10: the session's commit folders (2026-11-05)

- B5: `store.addCommitDirs` (both copies) replaces the three `commitDirs.slice(-6)` spots in `hook-milestone.js` and `addOthers`. Each folder gets `rec.commitDirsAt[dir] = [from, settled]`: `from` is the window of the command that added it (`gitfacts.windowStart`, from the PreToolUse record and `duration_ms`), and a later command there moves only `settled` (93e1231); `settled` is when that command was done (`now`, or `now + windowMs()` for one sent to the background). The sweep (`sweepFacts`, both launchers) starts a commit folder no earlier than `from`; the session's own folders still start at the session's start. Past six, a folder read past `settled` goes first, then the oldest; the folders being added stay. No time known (no record, no `duration_ms`): no entry, swept from the session's start as before.

Name the commit in the handoff at the next checkpoint." The folder still can't join (no state write without the lock).

### Reviews (2026-11-05)

- Docs (Sonnet, on `git diff d6b705e`): 8 findings, each CONFIRMED in code here and fixed: SECURITY's transcript row (two token counts do reach Claude), MANUAL's `[1m]` against `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_1M_CONTEXT`, the CHANGELOG's window list (the settings `env` block), how-it-works "nothing is written below the first step" (a start-over writes), the `git-timeout` row (no note after a failed command), the CHANGELOG's eviction wording, MANUAL section 15's "names the jb-memory copy", and the copies count.
- The copies count was a code bug from B3: `hook-session-start.js` (both copies) kept `.slice(0, 3)` from when it listed folders, so 5 copies read "3 other copies" (`otherCopies` finds up to 10). The slice is gone, there and on the Codex `oldCodex` list. New test in `handoff-checks.test.js` (both copies, "copies: session start counts every copy it found"): fails on the tree before the fix ("3 other copies" with 4 on disk) and passes with it, both copies.
- Security (Fable, on the scripts' diff since d6b705e): nothing exploitable. F1: the B4 comments said git 2.56 won't open a gitdir under `vendor/objects/<name>`. `f1.js` (scratch) CONFIRMED that git 2.56 opens one through a submodule's `.git` file, two-hex and not, and that `farNested` refuses both first (`network-config`). Comments in `gitbin.js` (both copies) and the CHANGELOG line now say so. Also from F1, as before 0.6.0: `moduleGitdirs` passes over `refs/`, `logs/` and `objects/` as a gitdir in its own right; git 2.56 opens a gitdir there only through a `.git` file too (the reviewer's probe), so deferred. F2: `transcriptFile` accepts an NTFS alternate data stream of a file in the projects folder; local, and only numbers come out: no change.
- Regression and design (Opus, on `git diff d6b705e -- plugins`): three findings, each reproduced here and fixed with a test that fails on the code before the fix (the scratch `prefix/` copy) and passes now:
  1. Images: `extraTokens` counted `JSON.stringify(tool_response).length / 4`, so an image Read (base64: 229k characters for a 1093x1351 JPEG in a local transcript, CONFIRMED) added about 57k tokens, and every image asked at a real 20k on a 200k model; under 75% the state started over, so the next image asked again. Now an image (base64 under `base64` or `data` in an object whose `type`, `media_type` or `mimeType` is `image/...`) counts 1,600 tokens, one call adds at most 25,000 (Claude Code's Read and MCP output limit), and `decide()` asks only when the transcript's own size is at least 75% of the compaction point, the start-over line. Test: "an image counts as one image ..." (old: asked "Context about 188k" at 20k). That the hook's `tool_response` matches the transcript's `toolUseResult` is INFERRED (the reviewer's and mine). A first draft took any long base64-alphabet string for an image, which counted a 40,000-character `x...` prompt as 1,600; caught by a spot check before any test ran.
  2. The cap from the model's name: `claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929` (and any name the families don't match) is capped at 200k even with `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW=1000000`; after one checkpoint it said nothing up to the real compaction. Transcripts never carry `[1m]` (the reviewer found none in 68k model fields). Now a request bigger than the capped window lifts the cap (`uncapped` from `windowFor`: the setting, or 1M). Test: "a request bigger than the window the model's name gives ..." (old: silent at 830k). Left, for a 1M context the name doesn't show: early asks at the steps of the 167k point while no handoff is written (one, if the first gets a checkpoint), in each cycle; deferred below.
  3. B5 join time: `hook-milestone.js` measured the window from the sandboxed script's start, later than the launcher's by as long as the launcher ran, so with no PreToolUse record a folder could join after its commit's reflog second and the sweep missed it. Now the launcher passes `procStart` in `JBMEM_GIT` (both launchers) and the hook uses it. Test (both copies): "a folder's join time is measured from the launcher's start ..." (old: 2.6 s late with the launcher held 3 s).
- Also from the Opus round: the handoff-checks test title "module walks skip refs/ and objects/" now says "... and the loose-object folders of module gitdirs", which is what it checks.

### Review round 2 (2026-11-05)

- Docs (Sonnet, on the round-1 fixes): 6 findings plus one style point, each checked here:
  1. Prompts were capped at 25k like a tool's result, but Claude Code sets no such limit on a prompt, so a large paste was under-counted and the request came late. Now only a tool's result is capped; a prompt counts in full (up to 1M). Test "a prompt counts in full, and an image counts as much as the high-resolution tier allows" (old: silent at 280k + a 200k-character prompt on a 400k window).
  2. An image counted 1,600 tokens, the old standard-tier figure. Claude's vision docs (read 2026-11-05) give ceil(w/28) x ceil(h/28), at most 1,568 on the standard tier and 4,784 on the high-resolution tier (Claude 4.7 and later). Now 4,784; the 75% rule keeps that from asking early. Same test, second half (old: silent at 308k + one image).
  3. how-it-works said nothing is written before a step except to clear a request; `decide()` also writes `done` when the handoff was written since the first request (hook-context-watch.js line 73, CONFIRMED). Said now.
  4. MANUAL section 7 offered a model name ending in `[1m]`, which transcripts don't carry. Dropped there; the paragraph says the name is read from the transcript and how the cap lifts.
  5. The CHANGELOG's two-hex line now says an older git may also open such a gitdir by name, as the gitbin.js comment does.
  6. The git-timeout note fires on any state-file failure but access denied, not only a busy lock (CONFIRMED in hook-milestone.js). how-it-works and the CHANGELOG say "can't be updated (for example ...)".
  - Style: "decisions, corrections and next steps" (a tricolon) is "decisions and next steps" in the hook's text, its comment and the CHANGELOG. No test matched that phrase.
- Regression (Opus, on the round-1 fixes and the Sonnet round-2 code): three findings, each reproduced here and fixed with a test that fails on the code before the fix:
  1. The 75% gate from round 1 made the first request late: two large results in a row from under 75% (124k + 20k on a 200k model) asked nothing, and the next call asked at 170k, past the 167k point (also with the percentage override and a small window). Now an ask may come from under the line; one made there doesn't start over at the line (only after a compaction or a shrink), so it can't repeat call after call, and once the transcript reaches the line the steps start over if the handoff was written since, so an early checkpoint doesn't end the requests. Test: "... an ask from an estimate under 75% of the compaction point neither repeats nor ends the steps" (old: silent at 124k + 20k).
  2. The Codex note's count of old jb-memory-codex folders stopped at 3 at its source (`oldCodexCopies`, health.js, shared), so the B3 slice removal changed nothing there. Now up to 10, as `otherCopies`. Test (both copies): "copies: every old jb-memory-codex folder is counted ..." (old: 3 of 5).
  3. The round-1 image check exited on the fast path (20k + 25k), so it passed with image detection off. It now runs at 120k, where counting the base64 as text would ask; CONFIRMED that it fails with `isImage` forced false. Its stale "1.6k" comment is gone.
- Two handoff-checks failures in one quick check (test 12, a branch named like the SHA; test 85, the junction `.git`): both pass alone 3/3. A 6-run loop of both handoff-checks suites (`hcloop.sh`, scratch, full TAP kept) reproduced the junction one in run 2 (test 179, the Codex copy): B8, below. Its runs also fail "rename: the installers refuse ..." in both copies every time, because the loop's snapshot has no `tools/` (the test runs `tools/install-check.js`); not a product failure.
- Test 12 and the other "commits since the handoff" tests, CONFIRMED a matter of load: `loadrepro.js` (scratch) ran them 5 times idle (0 failed, about 14 s each) and 5 times with 16 busy-loop Node processes (5 failed, 70 to 86 s each). Under load session start drops the commit section although the handoff names a HEAD with a newer commit; the hcloop runs that failed this way (3, 4, 6) ran beside my other test runs. INFERRED from the code: the only paths that drop it then are time limits, a git call in `commitsSince` that doesn't answer within its budget (at most 1,200 ms for up to five calls, trailmark.js line 390) or under 400 ms left in session start. Not from 0.6.0 (the code is the same at d6b705e); deferred below.
- The loop's 43 failures, CONFIRMED: on Windows, Node 26.13's `--permission` refuses the writer's writes inside the memory folder it was granted once that folder's path is about 268 characters (266 passes, 268 fails, bisected with `initwhy.js` in the user's Temp); with `JBMEM_SANDBOX=off` the same folder sets up. The scratchpad's long path put the test folders past it. Not from 0.6.0; deferred below.

### Review round 3 (2026-11-05, on the round-2 fixes)

- Scope: `scratchpad/round3.patch`, the round-2 fixes against the files as the round-2 reviewers saw them (rebuilt by `r3base.py`, which undoes the round-2 edits; its health.js matches fdd46b2, as it should). The round-1 and round-2 fixes are committed as 6df998d; `dev/release-check.sh --sums` passed on it: all 10 checks, handoff-checks 106/190, Claude Code 151/166, Codex 146/163.
- Regression (Opus): one bug in the new start-over rule, one design hole, both CONFIRMED by its sequences and fixed:
  1. The carry-on branch kept the early ask's `seenAt`, so the launcher still read the sizes under the line from before it, and the next call cleared the state: a second first request with no write (124k + Edit asks; 124.5k, 126k, 127k; a fresh first request at 143k). Now `seenAt` moves with `seen`.
  2. An early checkpoint could still end the requests at the line: after a checkpoint, the next over-estimate past 75% (126k + 25k) asked again at once from the estimate, and a second checkpoint there ended the asks about 41k before the compaction. Now an ask made while the transcript's own size is under the first step is kept until the transcript itself reaches that step; there the steps start over if the handoff was written since, else the state carries on (with `seen` and `seenAt` from then).

### The stale ask after `/compact` (2026-11-05, the user: "Fix it first")

- Found by Opus in round 5 (from fdd46b2 on, CONFIRMED with the launcher): once an ask and a handoff write had come before a manual `/compact`, the next prompt asked again with the size from before the compaction ("Context about 315k tokens, auto-compaction near 367k"). `fastPath` took the last request, from before the compaction, and the boundary only started the state over. INFERRED that hooks see this order: 5 of 9 manual compactions in local transcripts had a prompt before the first request after the compaction (1 of 61 automatic ones; Opus's count, the check below counted 62 automatic).
- Fix: `readUsage` without `since` returns null when a main-thread `compact_boundary` comes after the last request, so `fastPath` exits silent until the next request. A subagent's (sidechain) boundary doesn't count.
- Test: "a prompt after /compact, before the next request, doesn't ask with the size from before the compaction". On 9032cb0 it fails at the prompt with the 315k ask; with the fix all 15 context-watch tests pass. A mutant without the sidechain check fails it at "asks at 315k".
- Checked in 104 local transcripts (2026-11-05; `node dev/probes/compactions.js` repeats it, read-only): 71 main-thread compactions (9 manual, 62 automatic). After every boundary the first entry is the summary, no request with a size comes before it, and the first request after it carries the new size (median 11% of the boundary's `preTokens`, at most 62%). CONFIRMED for these transcripts: a boundary after the last request means the transcript holds no size for the context now.
- Release check: `dev/release-check.sh --sums` passed every check (tests passed of all: handoff-checks 106/190, Claude Code 155/170, Codex 148/164, the rest skipped; CPU median 48%). `--sums --quick` with the doc sentence in `docs/how-it-works.md` then failed one test, "a commit subject posing as markup is escaped", at 09:26 to 09:30 local with the CPU at 84 to 100%; the check kept no output. Not from this change: `readUsage` runs only for the context watch, not at session start (CONFIRMED in code). Three reruns of handoff-checks with the output kept (09:46 to 10:00): the first overlapped two `--quick` runs of another session and failed 10 tests with the CPU at 97 to 100%, this one among them with "HEAD has 1 newer commit, the newest 0 shown" (the commit list under load, CONFIRMED by output); the other two, with that session idle, passed 106 of 106. That the 09:26 failure had the same cause is INFERRED (same test, high CPU). `--quick` at 09:59 passed every check.

### Review round 7 (2026-11-05, on `git diff 9032cb0..887e17f`)

- The fix is committed as 887e17f, after the checks above (`--quick` at 09:59 passed every check).
- Regression and security (Opus): nothing CONFIRMED in the changed code. `fastPath` is the only caller of `readUsage` without `since` (CONFIRMED by grep). Crafted transcripts through the old and new `readUsage` (CONFIRMED with a scratch script): the new one returns null for a boundary followed by a `<synthetic>` entry, by a sidechain request, by 1.4 MB or 8.6 MB of tool results, for two compactions in a row and for compaction, request, compaction; a sidechain boundary still gives 315000. Tool output, prompts and file text can't make a main-thread boundary (they are escaped strings or nested values); forging one takes a direct append to the transcript, and silences the watch only until the next request. The extra check costs 2 ms or less against about 70 ms of Node start.
  - An automatic compaction mid-turn can put a tool result between the boundary and the next request: one of 76 boundaries in local transcripts has the in-flight `tool_use` copied with zero usage, then its `tool_result`, then the request (INFERRED that the hook sees this order; that transcript's timestamps are out of order). The old code used the 966k size from before that compaction; the new one stays silent. The docs now name both cases.
  - The test's last assertion ("the steps started over") would also pass through the shrink rule, so it doesn't prove the boundary path on its own; the fix doesn't claim that path.
- Docs (Sonnet): no contradictions between docs and code; MANUAL.md and CHANGELOG.md need nothing. Fixed after it: "the next prompt comes before the next request" was stated as fact (now "can come", with the mid-turn case), "a compaction" now says the main thread's, two counts and two run descriptions now say whose they are, the probes moved from the session scratchpad to `dev/probes/` so they can be re-run, the 42-test claim has its labels, the test counts say what they count, and a three-item list was reworded. The commit message of 887e17f keeps the older wording.

A later command now moves only `settled` (93e1231). Test: "0.6.0 commit folders: a folder already swept keeps its sweep start ..." (`trailmark.test.js`, both copies).
