  1. "git pointers: links on the way to a gitdir, config or include" expected '' for a `.git` that is a link. On Windows that keeps git out by design (`linked-gitdir`, review round 13, SECURITY.md). Test corrected in e5b50a8. It runs only where links can be made, so CI's next Windows run is its check.
  2. "lock: a real lock taken before .break is held is never moved aside (loop round 9)": `withLock` returned instead of throwing. Cause, CONFIRMED here with a junction (this account can't make symlinks): on Windows an exclusive create (`'wx'`) goes through a link or junction at the name whose target is missing, and makes the target. SECURITY.md says a linked lock is removed, never followed. The same code is in 0.5.1 (CONFIRMED in main's `store.js`). Fix (e5b50a8): `openNew()` in `store.js` (both copies). On Windows anything at the name counts as taken (EEXIST, as a file there would), and a file opened through a link swapped in after that look is closed and counts as taken too. It is used for the lock, `.break` and the state file's `.bad` copy. CHANGELOG: "Memory folder (Windows)". New test: "lock and state copy: a link whose target is missing is never made through (0.6.0)" (handoff-checks, both copies; it runs everywhere, with a junction on Windows).
  - Other `'wx'` creates are left as they are: the temp names of `atomicWrite` and `createOnce` are random, the history copy's name holds the time to the millisecond and a pending record's the tool-use id, and `createOnce` writes in place only where the drive has no hard links. A link swapped in between the look and the open is still followed, as SECURITY.md's "Links in the memory folder" says.

PowerShell also ends a `"..."` string at the curly double quotes U+201C, U+201D and U+201E as at `"`: in both versions a folder text `a”; <call>; “b` ran the call. - Review round 8's regression pass on 1af93af and 31e1461 (CONFIRMED by round 8 and ruled to count by the review-strategy session, since each breaks the stated rule of code this PR adds), all in how `--copies` prints a folder name:
  - S4 (point 1 of round 8's first report): a name holding the text `<U+202E>` printed like one holding U+202E itself.
  - S3a (point 2): the cut at 600 characters could fall inside an escape, so a line could end in `<U+2...`.
  - S3b: the cut was by UTF-16 unit, so it could split a surrogate pair (U+FFFD on stdout).
  - S3c: the cut kept the start, so a 129-character Windows cache path holding 80 soft hyphens printed as 600 characters of `<U+00AD>`, without the `\trailmark\0.4.0` that names the copy (round 8 reproduced it with the launcher).

### `TAG_LIKE`'s time (2026-11-06, the user: "Pull into 0.6.0")

- Found while fixing round 9's F1 and listed under "Deferred to the next version" in 71d7a78; moved here. In `store.js` since 75c9957 (0.5.0) or earlier. v0.5.1 has it (CONFIRMED with v0.5.1's `store.js`: 324 ms at n = 600, 2,536 ms at n = 1,200).
- The cause (CONFIRMED by the pattern and the timings): in 71d7a78's `TAG_LIKE`, runs in a row could each take invisible characters, from an opening tag's name to the spaces before its end. With no tag end after the run, the engine tried every way to split it, so the time grew with the run's length cubed: `escapeTags('<a' + U+200B x 3,000 + '!')` took about 43 s on 71d7a78.
- At session start (CONFIRMED by the session-start test below, on 71d7a78's `store.js`): with such a line in the handoff, the launcher stopped the hook after about 9.1 s ("hook-session-start.js took too long and was stopped"), so the session got no handoff.
- The user, through the review-strategy session: "Pull into 0.6.0". It is the one item pulled through the scope freeze.
- Fix, both copies' `store.js`: `TAG_LIKE` is built from strings so that each run matches a stretch of text one way only. The comment above it gives the reasons:
  - The run before a name's separator takes no separator, and the run after it stops at the first character that can't be in a name.
  - U+FEFF is both invisible and a space, so those runs leave it to the tag's end.
  - Four Hangul fillers are both letters and invisible: the run before an opening tag's name stops at them, and the name starts there.
  - A role word's letters are taken whole, with a lookahead and `\1`.
  - Every class that holds the invisible characters writes them as `TAG_INV`, so `SHOWN_TAG_LIKE`'s builder still finds all 10. `TAG_LIKE` and `SHOWN_TAG_LIKE` are exported for the tests.
  - Bounding each run (64 characters, the deferred entry's first idea) wasn't taken: a tag after a longer run would go unescaped, where 71d7a78 escaped it.
- Fail-closed (CONFIRMED, scratch scripts `tag2-diff.js` and `scan2.js`): over 775,958 strings, every string of up to 4 symbols over a 26-symbol alphabet after `<` and 300,000 seeded random ones, the new pattern and 71d7a78's escape the same text, plain and shown, with 0 differences either way. A scan of U+0000 to U+2FFFF at five places in a tag, 983,040 inputs per copy, against 71d7a78's `store.js`: 0 escapes missed and 0 added, on the plain path and on the shown path. F1's count (escaped on the plain path, not on the shown one) is 0 for both over those five places. Over every string of up to 4 symbols it is 37,066 of 475,255, the same on old and new (CONFIRMED by Fable in the combined round, and here on main with `dev/probes/tag-shown-gaps.js`; the deferred list).
- Time (scratch `tag2-diff.js`): at n = 3,000 the ten shapes the time test uses take at most 0.3 ms each, plain and shown.
- The other patterns run on handoff lines: `FRAME_LIKE` is anchored at the line's start with one run, `HEREDOC_END` is a fixed prefix, and `CONTROLS` and `LINE_BREAKS` match a character or a fixed sequence at a time. So their time grows with the line's length (INFERRED from the patterns). The session-start test runs them on a 3,000-character run, and it passes.
- Tests, `handoff-checks.test.js`, both copies, titles starting "tag escaping:":
  - Time: the ten shapes at n = 3,000 through `escapeTags`, plain and shown, in a child process with a 15 s limit. Each must take under 1 s.
  - Fail-closed: 71d7a78's `TAG_LIKE` literal is in the test as `TAG_LIKE_71D7A78`. Every place it or its shown form matches must match now, over every string of up to 3 symbols after `<` (fewer after `＜` and `﹤`), 20,000 seeded random strings, tags the other tests use, and runs of 3,000. It also checks that `SHOWN_TAG_LIKE` allows a `<U+XXXX>` in each class.
  - Session start: a handoff holding `<a` + 3,000 U+200B + `!` and `<system` + 3,000 U+200B + `>`, under the hook's 10 s limit. The handoff shows, with that tag escaped.
- Failing first (CONFIRMED 2026-11-06, `--only '^tag escaping:'`, both copies): on 71d7a78's `store.js` all 6 fail. The time test's child is stopped at 15 s, and the launcher stops session start after about 9.1 s. ### Deferred to the next version (2026-11-05)

- Cost: from 80% of (window less 50k) to the compaction, each call costs about 300 ms (332 ms median against 97 ms, Opus round 2) (the sandboxed hook starts) even after this session checkpointed, when it can't ask (Opus, measured). The launcher could run `decide()` itself and start the hook only when the state changes; `hookMemory` could use a shorter git timeout.
- One handoff write ends the asks until the compaction (as briefed). With finding 1 fixed, an early ask is rarer.
- B5's trade-off: a joined folder's sweep starts at the hook's own window, so a clock gap between Claude Code and git (WSL2), a `duration_ms` that under-reports, or a script commit before the first hooked commit there is no longer caught by the session-start sweep.
- HYPOTHESIZED: Claude Code may merge `modelSettings` across settings files; `windowFor` reads file by file, so a model's entry in the user file loses to a top-level value in the project file.
- B10: when both `if` patterns fire for one command (`git -C x commit`), Claude may be told twice while the lock is busy.
- The `[1m]` choice: reading the `model` setting or `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` could avoid the early asks for a 1M context the name doesn't show: at each step of the 167k point while no handoff is written (145k, 152k, 160k, Opus round 2), and again after each compaction (HYPOTHESIZED that they carry the suffix).
- Over-estimates, which now can ask early once per cycle (Opus round 2): an Edit's result carries the whole old file (`originalFile`), counted up to 25k; an 8-image `browser_batch` counts 25k against about 9.5k measured; a Read's image could be counted from `file.dimensions`. Parallel tool calls each see the same base, so their results don't add up (from before 0.6.0).
- HYPOTHESIZED (Opus round 2): usage entries carry `iterations`; none had more than one here. If usage ever sums them, a request could look bigger than a 200k window and lift the cap.
- From Fable F1: `moduleGitdirs` passes over `refs/`, `logs/` and `objects/` as a gitdir in its own right, as before 0.6.0; git 2.56 opens those only through a `.git` file, which `farNested` checks.
- Seen 2026-11-05 while two sessions ran handoff-checks at once (another session's kept TAP, `%TEMP%\trailmark-release-check\20261105-094638-1362`): about 45 tests in each of that session's two `--quick` runs failed because git's own `git init -q` in the test helper `repoAt` exited non-zero with nothing on stderr, with 46 node processes running. HYPOTHESIZED: processes failing to start under that many concurrent ones; the exit code, which would show it, isn't in the TAP. This session's rerun that overlapped them failed 10 tests, none at `git init` (CONFIRMED by its TAP). Whether the commit list under load is the same mechanism is HYPOTHESIZED too.
- Session start's commit list under load (from before 0.6.0, seen 2026-11-05): when `git log` alone runs out of time, the count survives without entries and the text reads "HEAD has 1 newer commit, the newest 0 shown:" over an empty list; it should say that git was too slow to list them. The commit-list tests fail on a busy machine for the same reason; a test-only budget would make them hold. CONFIRMED again 2026-11-05 with the CPU at about 72%: the injection test got the empty list, and the cut-note test got no section (`sinceHandoff` returns null when under 400 ms are left or git fails; which of the two is INFERRED).
- From Opus round 3: past 10 old jb-memory-codex folders the Codex note says "10 folders", with no sign of the cutoff (`otherCopies` marks its own). An image counts 4,784 tokens on a standard-tier model too (Haiku 4.5, at most 1,568); the provisional rule keeps that from asking more than once.
- From Opus round 4: a session that switches from a smaller to a bigger window (another model) while its context is in the step zone gets one more request. Since round 5 the state keeps the biggest point asked under, so switching back and forth adds no more.
- From Opus round 6 (older; CONFIRMED with the launcher, the same on 0612f86's code): `w.level` counts steps against the point of the last ask, so after a repeat ask under a smaller point (level 3 of 167k), the bigger window's later steps (90% and 95% of 217k) never ask, with no handoff written. One repeat ask is lost. A fix: store the size of the last ask and compare its level under the current point. Codex's review of PR #4 (comment 4482482345, on 3f07517) found the same case with `decide()`: 202k under the 250k window, 198k under 200k, then 207k and 210k under 250k stay silent (CONFIRMED by Codex, not re-run). The user (2026-11-05): "Keep it deferred".
- (Copilot on PR #4, comment 4483809356; seen on 586329e, the hook and launcher the same as at 887e17f.) Two context-watch hooks whose launchers both read the session record before the first request was saved carry no `since`, and `decide()` takes a missing `since` as a reason to start over: the second asks again at the first step, even when a checkpoint landed in between (CONFIRMED with `decide()`). That parallel tool calls make such a pair is INFERRED: each runs its own PostToolUse hook. Claude then gets the same first request twice; nothing is lost. A fix: the launcher passes the `seenAt` it read, and under the lock a state with a newer `seenAt` is kept. Deferred 2026-11-05 under the stop rule: the review-strategy session's answer for the user, "(a) defer". Repro: `node -e "const {decide}=require('./plugins/trailmark/scripts/hook-context-watch.js'); const cfg={on:true,steps:[85,90,95],reserve:33000}; const c={tokens:320000,base:320000,win:400000,pct:0,since:null}; const a=decide({},{},c,cfg,1000); console.log(JSON.stringify(a.ask), JSON.stringify(decide(a.w,{},c,cfg,1001).ask))"` prints two first-step requests.
- From Opus round 6: three windows in one cycle (250k, then 200k, then 225k) lose the third window's repeat asks; the first ask was already past all its steps. Rare (two setting or model changes in one cycle, INFERRED).
- From Opus round 5 (older): once a request drops back under the model's named window, an ask can say "Context about 198k tokens" with compaction "near 167k".
- (Fixed in the tests on `claude/load-proof-tests`, 2026-11-07: see "Load-proof tests".) The busy-lock test's 6,000 ms limit (from 0.4.0) fails on a busy machine in every version (CONFIRMED 2026-11-05: 3 of 3 with one busy loop per core). The hook waits 2 s for the lock; the rest is process starts, which slow with load. The launcher cuts the writer off at 9 s, so a wait that long already fails the test's exit-status check. (Wrong on 91415bb, CONFIRMED by review round 1 F1 of `claude/load-proof-tests`: the stopped hook was repeated, then skipped. Since round 1 the test runs the hook once with the lock free and fails if that run left room for the wait.) A limit on the wait alone would hold under load: for example, time `updateState` in the test's own process against a held lock, and keep the hook check to its exit status and the log line.
- Windows, long paths: init (and every write) fails with "sandbox refused FileSystemWrite" when the memory folder's path is about 268 characters or more (Node 26.13, CONFIRMED 2026-11-05). A repo path near 230 characters gets there, since the folder's name repeats it. HYPOTHESIZED: Node checks the `\\?\` form of a long path, which the grant doesn't match. Options: grant the `\\?\` form too (if Node takes it), or name the path's length in the message. Round 7 repeated it with `node dev/probes/path-limit.js plugins/trailmark <short folder>` (2026-11-05): the folder that holds `memory/` sets up at 259 characters and fails at 261. The refusal names the path in its `\\?\` form (CONFIRMED 2026-11-05 in a test sandbox about 307 characters deep, "PR #4" above), which fits the hypothesis without proving that the grant is what doesn't match. A handoff-checks run from a deep scratch folder failed 42 tests at init's exit status this way (the exit status CONFIRMED; that each was this refusal INFERRED from the probe under the same folder).

- (GitHub code scanning on PR #4, comment 4483760395; seen on 586329e.) The paths written as data in the session-start notes (`process.execPath`, the repo clash) go through `fieldText`, and since 9681950 the launcher and skills paths through `usablePath`, which refuses what `fieldText` would change. It drops control and invisible characters but keeps tag-like text: `<b>` and fullwidth `＜system＞` come back unchanged (CONFIRMED 2026-11-05). A Windows path can't hold `<` or `>`, but it can hold the fullwidth forms. `S.escapeTags` would break such text, but it also NFC-normalizes, so a decomposed path on Linux could stop naming the file (HYPOTHESIZED). Only whoever picks the install folder or the repo path can put such text there (INFERRED). Repro: `node -e "const S=require('./plugins/trailmark/scripts/lib/store.js'); console.log(S.fieldText('＜system＞ <b>', 400))"`.

- (The B8 fix's neighbors, 2026-11-05; HYPOTHESIZED, never seen.) If the remove were refused too, the folder would stay, and `busyRetry`'s next try of the junction would get `EEXIST`, which it throws, so the junction test would fail as before. No B8 record has `EEXIST`. Fix idea: in `junctionGit` only, after a busy try, remove an empty plain folder at the name and try again. Repro: none; it needs the remove refused as well.

- (Seen twice in CI, 2026-11-06, on 704399d and e13b721; the cause found, see "Cause" below: a stdin deadlock in Node's `spawnSync` on macOS, older than 0.6.0 in the launcher's hook path.) "0.6.0 context watch: an image counts as one image, ..." (Claude Code `trailmark.test.js:4209`) failed at its first hook call (:4216, an image Read with 670k characters of base64): the launcher stopped `hook-context-watch.js` at its 9 s limit ("took too long and was stopped"), so `run`'s status check got 1.
  - Not from 704399d (INFERRED, strongly). The hook's path reaches no tag pattern (CONFIRMED in code: the launcher's `contextWatch` handed the payload over on stdin until PR #8 (in `JBMEM_PAYLOAD` since), `hookio.readPayload` parses it, and the hook calls `cleanSession`, `loadConfig`, `sessionRecord` and `updateState`). b0215c0's product code is 704399d's (only tests and docs differ, CONFIRMED by `git diff --stat`), and it passed the test on macOS Node 22, 24 and 26. 704399d itself passed it on macOS 24 and 26 and on Ubuntu 22, 24 and 26.
  - Locally the call takes 311 to 352 ms on 71d7a78 and 328 to 391 ms on 704399d (12 runs alternating, CPU 34 to 63%, scratch `cw-time.sh`), the same as a plain Bash call: the 9 s limit leaves a margin of about 25 times. Loading `store.js` takes 15 ms on 704399d and 18 ms on 71d7a78.
  - The failing runner wasn't slow overall: its Claude Code suite took 196 s, against 205 to 280 s on the other macOS jobs of 71d7a78, 704399d and b0215c0. So it isn't on the load-sensitive list, since load isn't shown. HYPOTHESIZED then: a one-off stall on that runner, in the hook or before it. Ruled out by the probe below: the time goes in the hook's stdin, and the launcher's git call took 6 to 8 ms in the stalled calls.
  - It recurred: at e13b721 (PR #7, dev/ only, plugins as on main), macOS Node 26, job 128119772303 (run 42283938235; 19:55:58 to 20:06:13Z), the same test failed at :4224, the Edit call with `originalFile: 'y'.repeat(400000)`, with the same message. That is 2 in about 12 macOS jobs, none on Linux or Windows, and both on the test's two biggest payloads.
  - Cause (CONFIRMED on macOS CI, 2026-11-06, probe run 42291578841 on the scratch branch `claude/cw-stall-probe`, 44c2cc7; jobs 128146074301, 128146073893 and 128146074411 for Node 22, 24 and 26): the launcher hands the hook its whole payload through `spawnSync`'s `input`, and on macOS that call sometimes deadlocks. `dev/probes/cw-stall/stall-probe.js` (on that branch) called the hook 100 times per size (100 bytes, 80k, 400k, 670k) in three forms: through the launcher, through it with `JBMEM_SANDBOX=off`, and a bare `node` parent that `spawnSync`s a bare `node` child with the same `input` (no trailmark code). 9 of 3,600 calls stopped at the 9 s limit: launcher 1, without the sandbox 2, bare 6, on all three Node versions. All 9 had a payload of 80k or more (0 of 900 at 100 bytes). Each was caught live after 2.5 s. In all 9 `sample` shows the parent in `SyncProcessRunner::TryInitializeAndRunLoop` waiting in `kevent`, and `lsof` shows it still holding the other end of the child's stdin socket. In 8 the child is blocked in `read` on fd 0 inside `readFileSync` (`hookio.readPayload` for the hook); in the ninth it had exited by the time it was sampled. So nothing in the hook is slow and nothing is written. The hook never gets past reading its stdin, and the sandbox, git and the state lock play no part. In the first stall the hook had nothing to write anyway (CONFIRMED in code: `decide` changes nothing at 124.8k, so `updateState` isn't called).
  - INFERRED: a lost wakeup in the `spawnSync` loop on macOS (the child's stdin is a Unix socketpair). A payload that fits the socket's buffer is written at once, so the parent never waits. That fits the 0 of 900 at 100 bytes, but that count alone doesn't prove the size matters (p about 0.1). The capture whose child had exited while the parent waited on until the limit points at the parent's side. Two outside reports show the same stacks on macOS: jesdi/general-skills#99 (Node 24.21, 1 MiB input) and zhangwy324/better-code-review#163 (CI; its fix gives the child a file as stdin). Not re-run here. The probe's second step, with the Claude Code suite running alongside, had 0 stalls in 1,440 calls.
  - Real users (INFERRED): on macOS the context watch passes the fast path only near the compaction point (80% of it). There, a call with a big tool result (an image Read, or an Edit of a big file, whose `originalFile` is in the payload) can hang: 0.1 to 0.3% of such calls at the probe's rate on CI runners (not measured on a user's Mac). Claude Code then waits about 9 s after that tool call, the hook exits 1 with "took too long and was stopped", and that call's checkpoint ask is lost (a later call can still ask). The milestone hook (both copies) also gets the raw payload, including the commit's output, so a big output could stall it the same way (INFERRED, not tried). Session start and precompact payloads are small.
  - Fix: PR #8, branch `claude/hook-stdin-deadlock`, 99607f5 on 03da9fd, then a778ace and a6a025b, both copies (2026-11-06; reviewed, wording fixed in 8983422 and 2633ecc, merged as 40a5a06). `hookio.hookInput` keeps only the payload fields the hook scripts read (plain values of an allow-list, `tool_input.command` and `run_in_background`, the exit fields of `tool_response`). The launcher hands it over as JSON in `JBMEM_PAYLOAD`, as `JBMEM_GIT` already travels, and nothing on stdin, so no socket is written to at all. A field filter alone would have left a long command on stdin. `JBMEM_PAYLOAD` is scrubbed from the launcher's environment with the other `JBMEM_` variables, and `readPayload` takes it only when `JBMEM_LAUNCHED` is 1. Windows caps a variable at 32,767 characters, so a payload over 30,000 characters of JSON starts no hook script, and the launcher says so on stderr. In practice that takes a very long command. The launcher stays read-only (no temp file).
    - The context watch's estimates can't change (CONFIRMED in code: `contextwatch.fastPath`, `contextwatch.js:313`, works them out in the launcher from the whole payload and hands them over in `JBMEM_GIT`; the hook never reads `tool_response`). The new test records them: 124,797 for the 670k image Read, 149,000 for the 400k Edit and 174,000 for a 200k prompt, the same on 03da9fd (CONFIRMED, `--only` with 03da9fd's scripts, 2026-11-06).
    - Failing first (CONFIRMED, `--only` with 03da9fd's scripts): all 5 new tests fail there, the hook got 400,318 to 670,498 characters on stdin or `hookInput` is missing. With the fix, `--only` on them and every context-watch, launcher, hook and commit test passed, 187/201 with 14 platform skips (Windows symlinks and shell scripts in place of git), and `--sums --quick` passed.
    - My own test assumption, corrected (CONFIRMED the same on 03da9fd): in the Codex copy a failed commit command is logged only when its printed text names the commit (`[master <sha>] ...`), so the test gives it that way.
    - On macOS, 0 stalls with the fix (CONFIRMED, probe run 42300018402 on 00a5778, which is a778ace plus the probe, 2026-11-06; the user approved one run, and the branch was deleted from GitHub after it). Each variant ran 1,260 calls (75 alone and 30 beside the Claude Code suite, at 100, 80k, 400k and 670k characters, on Node 22, 24 and 26). Over 2 s: launcher 0, no sandbox 0, `park` with the text on stdin 0. The bare node control, `spawnSync` with `input`, stalled 8 times (at 80k and 400k, on Node 24 and 26, about 9 s each, killed at the timeout), with the same stacks as before (`ReadFileUtf8` in the child, `TryInitializeAndRunLoop` in the parent). Not covered by the run: a6a025b's `--from` change, which doesn't touch stdin. The probe is kept locally on `claude/cw-stall-probe` (00a5778). Its `park` variant is new.
  - `write-handoff` and `park`, fixed in a778ace and a6a025b. Their text can pass the sizes that stalled (CONFIRMED in code: a handoff over its budget is still saved with exit 2, the budget goes up to 100,000 characters, `store.js:572`, and `park` has no size cap). The launcher no longer reads their text and writes it on: text on stdin reaches the script as it is (`stdio` 'inherit'). A `--from` file is checked by the launcher and closed, and the script opens it in the memory folder, which it may already read, and reads it only if it is still the checked file (same device and file ID, one link; `readChecked` in `memory.js`, `JBMEM_FROM` and `JBMEM_FROM_ID`, both scrubbed by the launcher). No cap, and the launcher stays read-only.
    - First try, a778ace: the checked descriptor itself as the script's stdin. Windows CI failed it (all three jobs, "drop files: --from removes only incoming-*.md drop files ..."): `EPERM` renaming over `handoff.md`, since Windows can't replace a file while a handle to it is open, and `--from=handoff.md` is allowed. CONFIRMED locally on a778ace by a new case in the 124k test (that CI test skips here, as making links needs a privilege). a6a025b passes it.
    - Tests (fail first, both copies): "0.6.0 write-handoff and park: a 124k text reaches the script whole, and the launcher writes none of it to the script's stdin" fails on 99607f5 ("write-handoff got 124922 characters written to its stdin by the launcher") and on a778ace (the `EPERM`). It also covers the script refusing a `--from` file with another file ID or a second hard link. `--only` on the write-handoff, park, `--from` and launcher tests: 101/115, 14 platform skips. `--sums --quick` passed.
    - Same CI run at a778ace, Windows Node 22 only: "injection: invisible tag characters ... (loop round 9)" had no COMMITS section, the commit list's sign under "Load-sensitive tests". Put down to load by that rule, not re-run.
  - Repro of the stall: push `claude/cw-stall-probe` again, or run `node dev/probes/cw-stall/stall-probe.js plugins/trailmark <out> 100` on a Mac.
  - The test isn't on the load-sensitive list: load isn't the cause.

- (Review of PR #8, D1; CONFIRMED by the review, re-run there; seen on a6a025b.) PostToolUse's payload is about 72 characters longer than PreToolUse's for the same command (29,315 against 29,387 in the review's run). A command in that band writes a commit-start record but gets no milestone ask. The record is harmless, since records are kept for 6 h and removed by design (`store.js:1367-1370`). The lost ask is the same as the over-cap skip's. Fix idea: check the cap against the larger of the two payloads in both hooks. Repro: `hookInput` on a Pre and a Post payload for a command of about 29,300 characters.
- (Review of PR #8, D2; CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run; seen on a6a025b.) The comment above `PAYLOAD_MAX` in `hookio.js` and `docs/how-it-works.md` give Windows' 32,767-character cap on a variable as the reason for the 30,000 limit. Fable passed 40,000- and 120,000-character variables through `spawnSync` on Windows 11 with Node 26, whole. The limit still guards POSIX limits and stays. Fix: wording only, give the reason that holds. Repro: `spawnSync` with a 40,000-character variable on Windows.
- (Review of PR #8, D3; fixed in release-state on 2026-11-06.) The entry "Not from 704399d" said the launcher hands the payload over on stdin. It now says that held until PR #8.
- (Fixed in batch D, 2026-11-07: see "Batch D".) (PR #10's review round 1, Opus; INFERRED; seen on 86fd88a.) `release-check.sh --only` runs all 20 test files at Node's default concurrency (15 on 16 cores), not at `AT_ONCE`. A pattern that matches many tests across files could bring back the all-at-once load that made the "past six" test skip. The 46-test pattern passed 46/46 twice that way, none skipped. Fix: pass `--test-concurrency=$AT_ONCE` to `--only` as well, after timing a narrow pattern with it. Repro: read the `--only` branch of `release-check.sh`.
- (Fixed in the tests on `claude/load-proof-tests`, 2026-11-07: see "Load-proof tests".) (PR #10's CI and round 3, CONFIRMED; seen on c02207a; the test that failed in CI is fixed in the round 3 batch.) Five other handoff-checks tests per copy need session start's copy check to finish inside its 300 ms budget, so a slow machine can fail them the way CI failed "copies: the command the note gives ...": "copies: session start counts every copy it found, not the first three", "copies: the launcher's own path reaches Claude and Codex only as it is on disk, or not at all", "copies: a launcher whose path the notes would change is not named", "rename: a copy under the old name jb-memory is still found, counted at session start, and named only by --copies --show-names" and "rename: an installed jb-memory is named too, the installed Trailmark is not" (55, 56, 58, 61 and 63 of each `handoff-checks.test.js` at c02207a), and in `copies.test.js` "session start: warns of an old cached copy without naming its folder, in the warning or the note, inside the size cap" and "session start: a huge handoff plus the copies note still fits the size cap" (3 and 9 in each copy at 90aaad4; added by round 4). None has failed in CI. Fix: the same as for that test, or a larger budget for session start's walk in a test through a test-only setting (the launcher is in the security row). Repro: the session-start hook's `otherCopies` call (`trailmark.js:365` in the Claude Code copy, `:686` in Codex) with `budgetMs: 0`, then `dev/release-check.sh --only plugins/<copy>/tests/handoff-checks.test.js` (5 failures per copy) and `--only plugins/<copy>/tests/copies.test.js` (2 of 13 in the Claude Code copy, 2 of 10 in Codex).
- (Fixed in batch D, 2026-11-07: see "Batch D".) (PR #10's review round 3, R3-2; CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run; the same on ff2e3e0 and 5a1e1a4, so not new in this PR.) After a Ctrl-C, `release-check.sh` exits 130 instead of re-raising INT, so a shell loop that runs it keeps going (`dev/repro-git-timeout.sh` loops it so). Repro: `for i in 1 2; do bash dev/release-check.sh --only hb.test.js; echo "iter $i rc=$?"; done` run as its own group, INT to the group after 5 s: both commits print "iter 1 rc=130" and start iteration 2. Fix: after the cleanup, `trap - INT; kill -INT $$` (and the same for TERM and HUP).
- (Review of PR #8, D4; cosmetic; seen on a6a025b.) The rewrapped cliMode comment in `trailmark.js` (about line 615) leaves a short line. Repro: read it.
- (Load-proof tests, 2026-11-07; the wording CONFIRMED by a probe on 5566010, the trigger HYPOTHESIZED, never seen; older than 0.6.0.) `list()` in `health.js` (lines 133 and 150) reports an error opening or reading a cache folder, other than ENOENT, as "<folder> has too many folders": EBUSY and EPERM, at open and mid-read, all give "the plugin cache has too many folders". A transient error there on Windows (a scanner holding the folder) would then fail the copy tests with that wording, not the time reason, and the user would read a wrong reason. Fix: a reason of its own, such as "<folder> could not be read". It changes what `--copies --show-names` prints (no shipped doc names the reason, CONFIRMED by grep), so the docs and Opus rows. Repro: `node probe-list.js <plugins/trailmark/scripts/lib/health.js>` (the review-strategy session's notes folder, `load-proof/`; it stubs `fs.opendirSync` in its own process only).
- (Load-proof tests, 2026-11-07; INFERRED from the code, on 5566010 plus the branch; the longer wait CONFIRMED and fixed in review round 1, F1.) The busy-lock test now times `updateState` against the held lock in its own process instead of the whole hook, so it no longer catches a hook that waits on the lock twice. (A longer wait of its own was caught again in round 1.) A hook-against-itself comparison can't replace it under load (the difference swung from -0.7 to +6 s). Repro: read `hook-milestone.js` for its `updateState` calls; a scratch second `updateState` in the commit path would pass the test.
- (Load-proof tests, 2026-11-07; INFERRED.) In "0.5.1 commit hook: a background commit command ...", the milestone hook after commit-start and the first session start aren't repeated when the launcher stops them at 9 s: repeating them isn't proven safe. Repro: the launcher's limit for those hooks cut to 50 ms in a scratch tree, then `--only 'a background commit command that has committed'`.
- (Load-proof tests, review round 3, 2026-11-07; INFERRED, on 5e90286.) A commit hook that is slow on its own, under the 9 s limit, is caught by no test: the busy-lock test skips it (CONFIRMED by round 3: a scratch 7.5 s sleep in the commit hook skips 2 of 2 on 5e90286, and fails 2 of 2 on d6a01ac only because that control was the cheaper subagent hook), and no test bounds the commit hook's run time (CONFIRMED by grep). That bound left with the busy-lock test's old 6 s limit, which load broke. Repro: the sleep in a scratch tree, then `--only 'a busy lock costs the reminder'`. A fix would need a bound that holds under load, such as a time from the hook's own trace rather than the wall clock.

### Codex: a checkpoint needs an approval every time (the user, 2026-11-05)

- Seen by the user in Codex 0.5.1: each checkpoint's save hits the sandbox, and Codex retries with escalated permissions. That is the documented default (`tm-checkpoint/SKILL.md` line 41, MANUAL.md line 262, the Codex README step 3): the memory folder is outside the workspace. Open question for the user: whether the skill should ask for escalation on the first try, skipping the write that is bound to fail. The catch: it would also ask people who added the folder as a writable root.

### Release checklist

1. Version bumps to 0.6.0: `plugins/trailmark/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, `plugins/trailmark-codex/.codex-plugin/plugin.json`, `plugins/trailmark-codex/plugin.json`, MANUAL.md's version line. `grep -rn "0\.5\.1"` for anything else naming the current version.
2. CHANGELOG: a 0.6.0 section, dated when released.
3. Reviews by CLAUDE.md's model table, each on the diff since the last reviewed commit.

### Confirmed since review

- CONFIRMED (2026-11-02, git 2.56.0.windows.2): Git for Windows takes `..` as text from a `.git` that is a junction. With `top\.git` a junction to `elsewhere\gitdir`, both `include.path = ../x` and a `commondir` of `../common` were read from `top\`, not `elsewhere\`. That held when git found `.git` itself (it reported the origin as `.git/../x`) and when it was given `--git-dir`. A copy without a junction read the same files, which shows the probe worked. The check would start where the junction leads, so the two would read different files, and the `linked-gitdir` refusal on Windows (`gitbin.js`) is needed. Not tested: other pointers (`objects/info/alternates`, a worktree's `gitdir` file) and directory symlinks as opposed to junctions.

### Done: the silent sweep skip (2026-11-03, branch `claude/loving-cannon-9abde1`)

- A sweep that runs out of time sets `stopped: 'out-of-time'` (`trailmark.js` `sweepFacts` at each budget gate; `gitfacts.js` `sweepCommits` when a folder is left unread because of the 800 ms gate or a git call refused or timed out). `write-handoff` prints "commit sweep skipped: out of time ..." and session start adds it to the warnings. Only fixed text is printed. `swept` moves only for folders read, as before. Timer values unchanged. `JBMEM_TEST_SWEEP_MS` can only lower the sweep's budget.
- Tests (each copy): the 0.3.4 sweep test repeats a sweep that reports the skip, for up to 90 s, and counts what all of them logged. If every sweep in that window runs out of time it is skipped with the reason, since nothing a finished sweep logs can be checked then. Two new 0.5.1 tests force the out-of-time path (launcher gates, and `sweepCommits` with 700 ms). Both fail on 4182717 for the intended reason (CONFIRMED).
- Under load (CONFIRMED, 2026-11-03, 32 busy threads): old code failed the 0.3.4 test 3 of 3. New code with a 20 s burst during the run: 7 of 7 sweep tests passed in 4 runs (one run repeated a skipped sweep once and then logged the commits). New code with every core busy for the whole run: no sweep finished in 90 s (6 and 7 skips in a row), so the 0.3.4 test and the 0.5.1 launcher test's last step were skipped with that reason; the 0.5.1 launcher test's out-of-time checks passed before that.
- Review: Fable, round 1 found one CONFIRMED test defect (an empty folder list could report `stopped` when the first git call timed out); fixed in code, `stopped` now needs a folder left unread. Round 2 found nothing CONFIRMED. Noted there: a bug that made every sweep report out-of-time would show as two skipped tests (90 s each) rather than failures, so a skip on an idle machine needs reading; and an invalid folder that the time gate passes over counts as unread (a harmless extra report).

### Done: repoRoot when git times out (2026-11-03)

- The defect: when `git rev-parse` timed out (3 s, or 1000 ms for commit-start, up to 500 ms in the sweep), `lib/paths.js` `repoRoot` returned the folder itself. In a linked worktree or a subfolder, `findMemoryDir` then looked for a memory folder of that folder's own.
- What it did, CONFIRMED on 4182717 with a stub that makes `git rev-parse` throw ETIMEDOUT (`node -r`, launcher process only), both copies:
  - commit-start in a linked worktree wrote no record and printed nothing (with git normal it writes one). This caused the burst failure of "a commit command still running" above.
  - The worse case, HYPOTHESIZED before, is CONFIRMED: `dir --json` in a worktree resolved to the worktree's own project folder (Claude Code makes one for each folder a session runs in) with method `key:...`, which needs no `--force`, and `init` set that folder up (Claude Code copy; the Codex copy's `init` needs `JBMEM_CODEX_INIT=1` and resolved the same folder).
- The fix: both timeout branches of `repoRoot` (after `--git-common-dir` and after `--show-toplevel`) return `localRepoRoot(cwd)`, the lookup from `.git` entries on disk that the kept-out path already uses, else the folder itself. Errors other than a timeout are handled as before. With the same stub, `dir` and `init` in a worktree now use the main checkout's folder and commit-start writes its record.
- Test (each copy): "0.5.1 paths: when git times out in a linked worktree or a subfolder..." runs the launcher with that stub: `dir` from a worktree and a subfolder, `init` from a worktree, and the commit-start record. Fails on 4182717 at `dir` (the worktree's folder) in both copies; passes now. The stub fails every `rev-parse`, so only the first timeout branch runs; the second (after `--show-toplevel`) is reached only with git older than 2.31 and is untested.
- Reviews: Opus on the test commit d01ccd4 found nothing (it checked by mutation that the wrapped tests still fail on a real bug). Fable on the repoRoot change: safe to ship. `localRepoRoot` gives the root git would give for a main checkout, linked worktree, subfolder and bare-repo worktree; a `.git` file planted to point straight at another repo's `.git` gives the planted folder (git gives the other repo), as on the kept-out path; the memory folder stays under the projects folder whatever root is chosen. One CONFIRMED finding, reproduced here: after the timeout, `localRepoRoot` can run `git config --file` once more (3000 ms limit) when `.git/config` holds `//` (an https remote). The comment that said "no second call" is corrected; the time budget is deferred below.

### Done: the commit hook when git times out (2026-11-03)

- What a user saw, CONFIRMED (test with the `node -r` stub that times out `git rev-parse` in the launcher, HEAD 9471aeb): a commit in the session's folder got "Commit landed." and `COMMIT_UNRESOLVED why=git-timeout`; the next checkpoint's sweep logged it `(found later)`. A commit in another folder (`git -C other commit`) got no output at all and the same log line, and was never logged: the sweep reads only the session's home and `commitDirs`, and that folder wasn't added.
- The fix: `gitfacts.commitFacts` returns the folder git timed out in (`folder`, absolute, git-timeout only); `store.checkCommitFacts` keeps it only when it is an absolute path under 4096 characters; the milestone hook adds it to the session record's `commitDirs` (as for a logged commit, last 6) and tells Claude "A commit was not logged yet: git was too slow (it timed out) in ... The next commit sweep logs it, at a checkpoint or the next session start.", whether or not a reminder is due.
- Test (each copy): "0.5.1 commit hook: when git times out, ..." Fails on 4182717 at the message (both copies) and, with the message checks taken out, at the other folder's commit (never logged). Passes now.
- Review: Fable on e6c24fa found no security issue (the new `folder` is the same value logged commits already put in `commitDirs`; every later git call there goes through `farGitConfig`). Its CONFIRMED points were in the docs: the CHANGELOG misdescribed the old output, and the lock-busy fallback (deferred below) wasn't named. Both corrected with the next commit.
- The clone test ("another clone with its own memory folder") accepts B's hook reporting the timeout, then has B's sweep log the commit in the clone's memory; both checkpoints use `settle`. Under full load (32 busy threads), each test alone, 2 rounds per copy: 8 of 8 passed, none skipped (before: the clone test failed 4 of 4). Whether a given run hit the timeout branch wasn't recorded.

### Done: git checks (2026-11-03)

- The root lookup without git (`gitbin.js` `localRepoRoot`): after a `git rev-parse` timeout it could run `git config --file` (3000 ms limit) when `.git/config` held `//`. CONFIRMED (stub, worktree with an https remote): HEAD e6c24fa made calls `config, rev-parse, config`. Instead of passing the caller's time limit through, `farGit` gets a `locate` option that returns as soon as the gitdir and commondir are known, before any config is read; `localRepoRoot` never used the reason `farGit` returned, only those two, so its answer is the same and no git starts. Now: `config, rev-parse` (the first is the check before git runs, unchanged).
- A plain file inside `.git` whose name isn't valid UTF-8 (a branch `b\xff` and its reflog): CONFIRMED on Linux (WSL, git 2.43): git makes and uses the branch; `farGitConfig` went from `""` to `odd-path`. Now a dirent that is a plain file with such a name is passed over (files are never followed); a folder or link with one, or a type the file system doesn't report, still gives `odd-path`.
- A folder in `.git/modules` with no `HEAD` (a removed submodule's leftover): its `objects/xx` folders were walked. CONFIRMED with the budget lowered to 1000: 1500 loose objects gave `many-entries`; the same with a `HEAD` passed. Now `objects/xx` under any `modules/.../objects` is skipped, as for a real submodule's gitdir; other entries there still count (the test checks `many-entries` for 1500 entries under its `refs/`).
- Tests (`handoff-checks.test.js`, shared): three 0.5.1 tests. The root and modules tests fail on e6c24fa (`config, rev-parse, config`; `many-entries`) and pass now. The odd-name test needs a file system that allows such names (skipped on Windows and APFS by a probe); run in WSL (node 18): passes now, fails on e6c24fa.
- Review: Fable on 4152c3c found no CONFIRMED defect. It checked that `localRepoRoot`'s result is unchanged and that only `repoRoot` calls it; that an odd-named link, FIFO or include still keeps git out (WSL) and a real U+FFFD folder on Windows still does; and that a branch named `modules/x/objects/ab` is still counted in the repo and in a real submodule, and a leftover's `objects/ab` link to a share still gives `linked-gitdir`. A harmless over-skip, CONFIRMED by it: in a leftover with no `HEAD`, `ab` folders under any `.../objects` there are skipped (git never opens such a folder).

### Done: the note for a commit sent to the background (2026-11-03)

- CONFIRMED (4152c3c, both copies, reminders unthrottled): a `run_in_background` commit whose hook ran before the commit existed printed nothing and logged nothing; the note came only when the commit already existed.
- Now, when the hook finds nothing for such a command, it adds the tool call to `recentBg` and prints the note once ("A commit command was sent to the background; its commits are logged by a later sweep once it finishes."); a second run for the same tool call prints nothing. The record still isn't marked done, so the sweep holds for it as before.
- Test (each copy): "0.5.1 commit hook: a commit command sent to the background gets its note ...": fails on 4152c3c (no output), passes now.
- Review: Opus on 316c517 found nothing CONFIRMED. Its INFERRED point, reproduced here and then CONFIRMED: the note's tool call ID went into `recentBg`, so a later run for the same ID counted as a repeat and dropped a `COMMIT_UNRESOLVED why=git-timeout` line, its note and the folder. The note is now kept in its own list (`bgNoted`); the test runs that case with the git-timeout stub and fails on 316c517. Its doc points (the lock-busy and "commit already there, no reminder due" exceptions) are named in how-it-works.md.

### Done: tests clean up their temp folders (2026-11-03)

- CONFIRMED: 10 passing `launcher:` tests left 9 `jbmem-*` folders in `%TEMP%` (2,897 were already there from earlier runs; not removed, since some may be kept on purpose, such as `jbmem-4hucLT` below). `release-check.sh` set only `TMPDIR`, which Node ignores on Windows.
- Now `realTemp` (all four test files) records each folder, and an `afterEach` removes them when the test ends, unless it failed (named as `# kept <dir>`) or `JBMEM_TEST_KEEP=1`; an `after` removes any made outside a test. `release-check.sh` also sets `TEMP` and `TMP` to its folder when `cygpath` is there (Git Bash).
- Checked: with TEMP pointed at an empty folder, 21 passing tests across both files left 0 folders.
- CLAUDE.md's "Conventions" line on test temp folders now matches (2026-11-03, the user: "Yes, update it").

### Done: the module gitdir walk under load (2026-11-03)

- Seen during the version wording commit: "superprojects: many module gitdirs stay fast, one on a share skips only status" (handoff-checks, both copies) failed 3 of 3 alone with `statusSkipped: git-timeout` (expected `submodule-pointer`) while the machine was 82% busy with 35 node processes not started by this session. It failed the same way at 9471aeb, 316c517 and f79d559, so it isn't from this round's changes; it passed in the full check a few minutes before. INFERRED cause: `farModules` walks 200 module gitdirs and stops at its time gate (`left() < 800`) when the CPU is busy. Not yet reproduced with load started on purpose, so not proven.
  - Proven (2026-11-03, the user asked for it): with every core busy (`burn.js`, 32 threads), the test failed 3 of 3 (`expected: 'submodule-pointer'`, `actual: 'git-timeout'`, 7.7 to 8.7 s each). A probe of the same fixture (scratch, not committed) logged where gitFacts' 5000 ms went. CONFIRMED two ways to run out: (a) `farModules` started with 2991 and 4338 ms left and checked 49 and 148 of the 168 module gitdirs that come before `s7` in listing order (about 45 and 24 ms each) before its gate (`left() < 800`) stopped it; (b) the first git call (`branch`) took 4149 ms and timed out, so the walk never ran. Idle, the walk checks all 168 in 2.2 to 3.4 s (13 to 20 ms each) and the test passes. The walk starts no git: one `farModules` over 200 gitdirs made 26,403 `lstat` calls on 1,810 distinct paths (`localPath` lstat's every part of every path, about 132 calls per gitdir). Status is skipped either way (fail closed); the test's reason and its `farGitConfig < 2000 ms` check (2099 ms in one loaded run) depend on time. Decided (the user): "Speed up the walk".
- Fix (gitbin.js, both copies): `localPath` keeps each lstat result in `LSTAT_SEEN` while one check runs (the outermost `withShare` call) and drops it when that check returns. `localPath` outside a check uses no cache.
- Measured (`measure.sh`, scratch; 3 runs each, 200 module gitdirs): the walk alone, idle 2.1 to 2.4 s before and 0.37 to 0.38 s after; with every core busy 16.9 to 27.1 s before and 2.4 to 3.0 s after; 26,403 lstat calls before, 2,412 after. `gitFacts` with the hook's 5 s budget, idle: 2.4 to 2.9 s before, 0.8 to 0.9 s after. With every core busy it still runs out (`git-timeout`) before and after, since git's own calls take seconds then; the walk was no longer the cause in the run that reached it (28 gitdirs in 442 ms, started with 1241 ms left).
- Tests (each copy): "0.5.1 superprojects: the module gitdir walk looks at each folder once per check" fails on e01c60f (26403 lstat calls; limit 6000). "... what one check found is not kept for the next" swaps a folder for a junction between two checks; it passes on e01c60f (no cache) and fails on a version whose cache isn't dropped. The superprojects test gives `gitFacts` 60 s (its subject is which gitdir is found, not the hook's time). Under full load the three passed 3 of 3 (before: the old test failed 3 of 3).
- Review: Fable on the gitbin.js diff found nothing CONFIRMED wrong. CONFIRMED by it: no cache outlives a check, a throw inside one included; no wrapped check calls another; `localPath` outside a check (plainfile.js, health.js, gitfacts.js and others) stays uncached. Notes, not changed: `farLfsFile` and `farLfsEntries` don't use `withShare`, so they run uncached (the slower, safe side); the older per-folder `farCache` of `farGitConfig` verdicts is kept across checks, as before 0.5.1; a swap during a check gives the same verdict as a fresh check unless its target is a share, which an attacker could equally set up after the check.

### Done: PR #1's first CI run and review (2026-11-03)

- CI at 7eeb33c: "0.5.1 superprojects: the module gitdir walk looks at each folder once per check" failed on every Linux and macOS job (`expected: ''`, `actual: 'network-config'`); Windows passed. CONFIRMED in WSL (git 2.43): each module gitdir's `core.worktree = ../../../s<i>` is also read from that worktree, `s<i>` didn't exist, and outside Windows `localPath` refuses a `..` after a missing folder. The older superprojects test passed there only because `network-config` also counts as `submodule-pointer`, and it stopped at `s0`, not `s7`. Both tests now make each submodule's folder, as a checkout has it, and the lstat test checks the folder first, as `gitFacts` does. In WSL the committed file failed that test and the new one passes; three other handoff-checks tests fail in WSL with both files (Node 18 there, below the supported 22) and passed on CI.
- Copilot's review of the clone test ("another clone with its own memory folder"), CONFIRMED with B's commit hook run under the git-timeout stub (scratch copy, not committed): the recovery wrote the clone's handoff with a version read in the main repo and was refused ("the handoff changed after you read it"); with that fixed, the check of B's commit line failed on the sweep's `(found later)` marker. Both fixed in both copies; the forced run passes in both copies.
- CI at f3fd44a: "a commit command sent to the background gets its note ..." failed on the three Ubuntu jobs (`expected: 0`, `actual: 1`; the notes held `COMMIT <sha> init session=11111111`). CONFIRMED cause: a command's commit window opens up to 3 s before it (1 s before its PreToolUse record or 2 s before its duration says, then down to the whole second the reflog keeps), and the test left 2.2 s (two ticks) between the sandbox's first commit and the record. With the ticks taken out (scratch copy), it failed 3 of 3 with the same line; with the reflogs aged 10 minutes, as `multiFolder` does, it passed 3 of 3. The test now ages them in place of the ticks.
- Review: Fable on that fix found nothing CONFIRMED wrong (the folders come only from `commitFacts`, vetted as the existing paths are, and the sweep checks them again); its two INFERRED edges are in the deferred list.
- Copilot (second review), CONFIRMED with a new test: a commit command sent to the background in another folder (`git -C`) whose hook ran before it committed recorded only `bgNoted`; that folder wasn't in the session's `commitDirs`, so a later sweep never read it, though Claude was told one would log the commit. `checkCommitFacts` (store.js, both copies) now also returns the folders `commitFacts` checked, and that hook adds the ones outside the session's own repo to `commitDirs`. Test (each copy): "a commit command sent to the background in another folder adds that folder ...": fails on f3fd44a (the notes hold only SESSION_START), passes now.
- Copilot (third review, on 284b15f), two points, both in code this PR added:
  - CONFIRMED (WSL, folders read logged): a submodule gitdir at `.git/modules/vendor/objects/ab` (a submodule named `vendor/objects/ab`) was passed over as a loose-object folder of the leftover `vendor/`, and `farModules` checks module gitdirs light, so links in it were never walked. gitbin.js (both copies): in a leftover's `objects/`, a two-hex folder with a HEAD is walked (`headAt`). Test (handoff-checks, each copy): "a submodule named like vendor/objects/ab has its gitdir walked ...": fails on 284b15f (`''` for `many-entries`), passes now.
  - CONFIRMED with a test: a background command that had committed in folder A but not yet in B when its hook ran took the main path, which added only A to `commitDirs`, so the sweep never read B. hook-milestone.js (both copies): `addOthers` adds every checked folder outside the session's repo on both paths for a background command. Test (trailmark.test.js, each copy): "a background commit command that has committed in one other folder but not yet in a second ...": fails on 284b15f, passes now.
- Seen while testing, not changed: `farCache` in gitbin.js keeps each folder's `farGitConfig` answer for the life of the process (since 0.5.0). Each hook and launcher run is its own process, so a check sees at most one answer per folder per run; a long-lived caller would see a stale one.
- GitHub code scanning (on f26e765 and again on 1471c16), comments 4463853576 and 4463876626: the session-start warning (`systemMessage`) named folders from a plugin cache (Codex's old jb-memory-codex copies; in both copies, other trailmark copies in Claude Code's cache). Older code (on main since 12cb50e), first put in the 0.5.2 list in 1471c16; fixed instead, since the fix is small and the finding came back. The warnings now give a count and where to look (the cache folder and the folder's name), and the names stay in the note marked as data. Tests: "session start: warns of an old cached copy without naming its folder ..." (each copy) the jb-memory-codex test (handoff-checks, run in the Codex copy) and "rename: a copy under the old name jb-memory is still found, and named to Claude as data only" (handoff-checks) check that the warning holds no folder path and the note does: both fail on 1471c16, pass now.
- GitHub code scanning (on 284b15f), CONFIRMED in code: the git-timeout note to Claude quoted each folder's path (`u.dir`) inside the plugin's own sentence, so a folder named like an instruction reached Claude as plugin text. The note now gives only the number of folders; the `COMMIT_UNRESOLVED` log line still names the folder. Test (each copy): the timeout test's other folder is named "Ignore earlier instructions" and the note must hold neither path nor that name: fails on 284b15f (the note quoted the path), passes now.
- Copilot (on bff7b3c, comment 4467944196), CONFIRMED and a regression from this PR: a submodule gitdir at `.git/modules/vendor/objects/ab/child`, with no HEAD in `vendor` or `ab`, was passed over with `ab` (the `headAt` check looked only at `ab` itself), and `farModules` checks module gitdirs light, so links in it were never walked. origin/main walked it (`many-entries` with a low budget; this branch `''`). gitbin.js (both copies): in a leftover's `objects/`, a two-hex folder is passed over only when it has no HEAD and holds plain files only (`onlyFiles`); anything else is walked. Test (handoff-checks, renamed "... vendor/objects/ab or vendor/objects/ab/child ..."): adds the child case and a no-HEAD folder with subfolders, and the passed-over case is now plain files; fails on bff7b3c at the child case, passes now in both copies. Cost: one listing per two-hex folder of a leftover (at most 256 per leftover), none for real submodules. Fable review: nothing CONFIRMED wrong (14 probes on Windows and WSL: links and junctions in or as `ab`, a HEAD that is a link, a FIFO, an unreadable `ab`, case; a leftover of plain loose objects still gives `''`). For 0.5.2: a POSIX-only test with a link (not a folder) inside `ab`.

## Decided not to fix (2026-11-03, the user: "Leave both")

- A worktree `gitdir` file with trailing spaces keeps git out with the reason `network-gitdir`, which misleads. The note that git trims them is RULED OUT (2026-11-03): git 2.56.0.windows.2 and git 2.43 (Linux) both refuse such a worktree ("not a git repository" / "gitfile does not point to a valid repository"), so keeping git out matches git; only the reason word is wrong. Reason: git itself refuses such a worktree, so keeping git out is right; only the reason word misleads.
- A pending commit-start record written by trailmark-codex 0.4.5 has no `host` field and can be released early, within 11 minutes of upgrading. CONFIRMED (2026-11-03, `openCommitStart` on a record without `host`, a later command of the same session done): released, where a record with `host: codex` holds; both are released once 11 minutes pass. The field came in 2484cbd (in v0.5.0). Codex users coming from 0.5.0 already have `host` (0.5.0 is published, 2026-11-02), so the window applies to upgrades from 0.4.5, and it needs a Codex commit command running across the upgrade and the same session finishing a later one inside 11 minutes, and the effect is one commit logged by the wrong session's sweep. Reason: it needs a Codex commit running across the upgrade and a later command of the same session within 11 minutes; the worst effect is one commit logged under the wrong session, and it expires on its own.

The hook resolves the memory folder with a 1000 ms git timeout; it timed out, `repoRoot` fell back to the worktree, and the hook found nothing set up and exited silently. This is the `repoRoot` item below.

CONFIRMED (stub, worktree with an https remote): one extra call, about 70 ms when git is fast. When git is slow enough to time out, this can add up to 3 s per config file, past the caller's budget (1000 ms for commit-start, up to 500 ms in the sweep). A hook that runs over is stopped and says so. Fix: pass the caller's time limit through `localRepoRoot` to `configEntries` (gitbin.js, a security file: Fable review).

  - The product is safe: `swept` doesn't advance, so the next sweep finds those commits. The defects are that the skip is silent and the test asserts an outcome that depends on time. Fix for 0.5.1: report a skipped sweep (for example "commit sweep skipped: out of time"), and have the test accept that report plus a later sweep that logs the commits, instead of requiring this sweep to finish.

## Load-sensitive tests (listed 2026-11-05)

These tests fail, or skip themselves, on a busy machine and pass on an idle one. Since `claude/faster-checks` (f05b383) the old `trailmark.test.js` numbers below point into split files: the busy lock is in `sessions.test.js`, the sweep tests in `sweep.test.js`, `security.test.js` and `commit-folders.test.js` (#150 in `security.test.js`), and the git-facts tests in `basics.test.js` (16), `commits.test.js` (89) and `sweep.test.js` (108). The local check runs two files at a time per suite because of the commit-list and sweep entries ("Faster local checks", above). Most of the proof comes from the 0.6.0 work (`claude/release-0.6.0`, its "Deferred to the next version"), where the fixes are deferred. A failure of one of them is put down to load only when its kept output shows the sign named here; anything else is a new failure. A test joins this list only with a proven cause (CLAUDE.md, "Checking a change"). Busy loops: `node -e "for(;;){}"`, started in the background as many times as the entry says. Since `claude/load-proof-tests` (2026-11-07, "Load-proof tests" above), the busy-lock bound, the commit hook's time limit and the copy check's budget are off this list: their tests repeat the call, or skip with the reason, and their entries as they stood are in that section. The commit-list and git-facts entries are narrower.

- The commit list at session start. Tests: the handoff-checks tests that expect a COMMITS section, in both copies: the "commits since the handoff: ..." tests (test 12 is "... a branch named like the SHA does not stand in for it"), "injection: invisible tag characters and bidi overrides never reach the session-start text (loop round 9)", "cut note: an oversized handoff stays inside injectChars ..." and "fit: a small injectChars holds with a commit list, a snapshot and warnings" (added 2026-11-05: no COMMITS section in both copies, in the full checks of c7a194a at 13:02 and 14:24 while a game ran; test 13 in the Codex copy, CONFIRMED from the kept TAP's location, and 112 in the Claude Code copy, since handoff-checks numbers both copies' files in one run). Sign: no COMMITS section in the session-start text, or "HEAD has 1 newer commit, the newest 0 shown:" over an empty list. CONFIRMED on the 0.6.0 branch with the code as at d6b705e (`loadrepro.js`, scratch: 0 of 5 runs failed idle, 5 of 5 with 16 busy loops), and again on 2026-11-05 at about 72% CPU. Seen again at d6b705e on 2026-11-05 09:39 EST: `--only 'a branch named like the SHA'` failed in both copies with no COMMITS section (10.5 s each), and passed 2 of 2 on the next run. CPU sampled just after the failure was 45 to 54% with 46 node processes, so load as the cause of that run is INFERRED. Repro: 16 busy loops, then `dev/release-check.sh --only 'commits since the handoff'`. The product defect is deferred: the text should say git was too slow to list the commits; a test-only time budget would make the tests hold. Since `claude/load-proof-tests`, each of these tests tries three times, so a failure with this sign now means three in a row.
  - Since batch F (92794c1, "Batch F" above), the sign changed: when git runs out of time the text says "git ran out of time" in place of leaving the section out or "the newest 0 shown". The tests try three times on that sign, then skip with "git ran out of time at 3 session starts in a row (left=... budget=... git=...)" after a control that only load passes (the launcher's `JBMEM_SHOW_SINCE` line must show under 400 ms left before git, or git spending its budget less 300 ms). So load shows here only as skips; a failure of these tests, with or without the old sign, is new and gets investigated.
- The sweep tests that skip themselves. Tests: those wrapped in `sweepTest` in `trailmark.test.js` (9 per copy at d6b705e: the "0.3.4 sweep: ..." tests, "0.5.1 sweep: ..." and the "0.5.1 commit hook: ..." timeout and background tests). Sign: a skip, not a failure, with "every sweep for 90 s ran out of time" (`SweepsOutOfTime`), so load shows only as a higher skipped count. CONFIRMED in code (`settle`, `settleCall` and `sweepTest` in `trailmark.test.js`) and with every core busy on 2026-11-03 (0.5.1 work: 6 and 7 skips in a row, no sweep finished in 90 s). On the 0.6.0 branch the Codex copy had 17 skipped at 72% CPU against 16 at 23%; that the 17th was a sweep test is INFERRED (that run's output wasn't kept). A skip on an idle machine needs reading: a bug that made every sweep run out of time would also show as skips (Fable, 0.5.1). Repro: one busy loop per core, then `dev/release-check.sh --only '^0\.3\.4 sweep'`; `--only` keeps the TAP output, where the skip reason is. At 7fb6d6e (2026-11-05) there are 12 per copy, and the first to skip is "0.6.0 commit folders: past six, a folder a sweep has read since it joined goes first ..." (seven folders per sweep, the most of any). Its sign: "every sweep for 90 s ran out of time (N in a row)". It skipped in both copies in the full check on 7fb6d6e, one more skip per suite than before PR #4's fixes. Run alone in both copies with versions alternating, while about 53 node processes of other work kept the CPU at 31 to 100%:

  - 3f07517's (before both PR #4 fixes) skipped 4 of 4 and HEAD's 2 of 4 (C A A C, 12:17 to 12:25).

- The git-facts budget (added 2026-11-05, with the review-strategy session). Tests (the Codex `trailmark.test.js` numbers at c7a194a; the same tests in the Claude Code copy): 16 "launcher: hooks run sandboxed with launcher-collected git facts", 89 "0.3.3 hardening: the PreCompact status runs no fsmonitor, hook or submodule git" and 108 "0.3.4 commits: another session's commit in the same folder while a command ran is left to that session when git printed this command's". Sign: the git-timeout marker in the failed test's kept folder: `uncommitted=unknown` on the PRE_COMPACT line, "Uncommitted files: not read (git-timeout)" in a snapshot, or `COMMIT_UNRESOLVED why=git-timeout`. The product writes it only when git ran out of its budget, so a failure that shows it is this mechanism, and one without it is new and gets investigated. Seen in the full check of c7a194a's tree (2026-11-05 13:02, a busy machine). Since `claude/load-proof-tests`, the three tests repeat the step, or skip, when the marker shows, so the marker no longer fails them; 108's last step can still fail without it (the side pass, below).
  - Proof (2026-11-05 15:58 to 16:02, a clone of df88236, `dev/repro-git-timeout.sh`): as shipped, all pass. With the launcher's two git budgets cut to 5 ms, all three fail in both copies and leave the marker: 89 at its 13:02 assertion, 16 one assertion earlier (the commit hook runs out too), 108 at its first step. With each git call 500 ms slower, 89 fails at its 13:02 assertion in both copies, and the Codex 16 at its 13:02 assertion with the 13:02 log: branch and head read, `uncommitted=unknown` (CONFIRMED). The Claude Code 16 passed in that run. What made git run out at 13:02 is INFERRED: three suites at once, a game, Unity, Edge and Codex sessions.
  - 108: at 13:02 only its last step failed (line 2772, "via an alias" and "explicit"). A uniform slowdown fails it at its first step (5 ms budgets) or not at all (500, 900 and 1,300 ms more per git call, both copies), so the 13:02 failure point is not reproduced. Measured at idle (16:03, each git call logged in the clone): every step's main pass gets about 4,920 ms and makes 3 or 4 git calls taking about 190 to 280 ms. The last step alone adds a 1,000 ms side pass for another session's folders (`gitfacts.js:547`), whose timeout leaves the commits with this command and can't write the marker. So the steps use about the same of the budget, and why only the last ran out at 13:02 is HYPOTHESIZED: one slow git call, or slow process starts.
- Not in this list, since their causes aren't proven to be load (both are in the 0.6.0 deferred list): the `git init -q` failures in the entry "Seen 2026-11-05 while two sessions ran handoff-checks at once" (HYPOTHESIZED: processes failing to start); the same `--quick` check at d6b705e alone on a quiet machine then passed 90 of 176 with 86 skipped and none failed (2026-11-05 10:21 EST, one run). And "Windows, long paths", a matter of path length rather than load: init is refused when the memory folder's path is too long, which a deep temp folder reaches.
